Ý bạn là muốn làm giao diện Dance Studio giống hình mẫu, trong đó:

Nhân vật 3D nhảy nằm trong một khung hình riêng bên cạnh.

Nhân vật tự động nhảy theo nhạc.

Có thể thay đổi nhân vật nam/nữ.

Có thể thay đổi dance style và trang phục.

Khung bên cạnh chạy animation liên tục, đồng bộ với nhạc.

Có thể đưa khung nhân vật này vào video xuất MP4.

Mình đề xuất dùng một viewport Three.js riêng cho nhân vật, có camera, ánh sáng và animation controller độc lập. Như vậy nhân vật có thể nhảy liên tục mà không ảnh hưởng đến khung cảnh chính.

Bạn muốn nhân vật hiển thị theo kiểu 3D toàn thân (từ đầu đến chân) hay chỉ nửa người?


Rõ rồi\! Mình sẽ thiết kế nhân vật 3D toàn thân, luôn nhìn thấy từ đầu đến chân, không bị cắt chân hoặc mất đỉnh đầu khi nhảy.

Các yêu cầu kỹ thuật cho khung nhân vật:

- Hiển thị toàn thân, kể cả khi nhảy, xoay người hoặc dang tay.
- Camera cố định, tự căn chỉnh khoảng cách để không cắt cơ thể.
- Nhân vật nhảy liên tục theo nhạc.
- Chuyển động tự nhiên, có chuyển động hông, vai, tay, chân và đầu.
- Có thể chọn nhân vật nam/nữ, trang phục và phong cách nhảy.
- Nền và ánh sáng đẹp, phù hợp với phong cách neon của NEON PULSE.
- Khi xuất video MP4, nhân vật vẫn giữ nguyên tỷ lệ và vị trí trong khung.

Một điểm quan trọng: nên dùng model 3D có skeleton rig và animation dance thực sự, không dùng nhân vật ghép từ các hình khối đơn giản.

Mình đề xuất khung nhân vật chiếm khoảng 30–35% chiều rộng video, đủ lớn để người xem thấy rõ toàn bộ động tác nhảy.


Vậy mình chốt hướng này cho NEON PULSE nhé: nhân vật 3D toàn thân nhảy theo nhạc trong một khung riêng, camera không cắt đầu hoặc chân, và được tích hợp trực tiếp vào Video Creator Studio.

Khi cần, mình có thể viết prompt kỹ thuật chi tiết để OpenCode triển khai đúng thiết kế này.


Được nhé. Thực tế mình nghĩ nên làm hai việc này trước khi nâng cấp Dance Studio.

## 1. Cho nhân vật tự động nhảy khi chưa có camera

Làm được, không cần webcamSử dụng Auto Dance Mode:

- Nhân vật 3D tự nhảy theo nhạc.
- Phân tích BPM, bass và beat để điều khiển chuyển động.
- Tự chuyển động tác theo từng đoạn nhạc.
- Có animation tay, chân, hông, vai và xoay người.
- Đồng bộ với nhạc và video export.

Sau này có webcam thì bổ sung Real Player Mode, không phải viết lại Auto Dance Engine.

## 2. Giảm tốc độ game ở Easy Mode

Mình đồng ý nên giảm thêm. Easy hiện tại vẫn hơi nhanh thì người mới sẽ khó bắt nhịp.

Đề xuất điều chỉnh:

| Chế độ | Tốc độ mới đề xuất |
| --- | --- |
| Easy | 0.65x |
| Normal | 0.85x |
| Hard | 1.0x |
| Expert | 1.15x |

Ngoài tốc độ, nên giảm mật độ nốt ở Easy:

- Nhiều khoảng nghỉ hơn.
- Ít nốt liên tiếp.
- Hạn chế nốt xuất hiện đồng thời.
- Tăng thời gian phản ứng.
- Không tạo chuỗi thao tác quá dày.

Lưu ý kỹ thuật: Chỉ giảm tốc độ di chuyển và mật độ nốt, không giảm tốc độ phát nhạc. Như vậy người chơi vẫn nghe nhạc bình thường, còn gameplay dễ theo kịp hơn.

## 3. Prompt giao cho OpenCode

Mình soạn sẵn yêu cầu triển khai:

Writing

# NEON PULSE – AUTO DANCE & EASY MODE BALANCING

## TASK 1: AUTO DANCE WITHOUT WEBCAM

Implement an Auto Dance Mode that works without webcam access.

Requirements:

- Use the existing 3D avatar and animation system.
- Automatically generate dance movements synchronized with music.
- Use BPM, beat timing, bass energy and music intensity.
- Support smooth transitions between dance animations.
- Include full-body movement: arms, legs, hips, shoulders and head.
- Keep the avatar synchronized with the master music timeline.
- Ensure the system works in Game Mode and Video Creator Studio.
- Do not require webcam permission.
- Keep Webcam Player as a separate future feature.

Do not use simple random joint rotations as the primary dance animation system. Prefer proper skeletal animation clips and procedural motion blending.

## TASK 2: SLOW DOWN EASY MODE

The current gameplay is too fast even on Easy mode.

Proposed initial difficulty settings:

- Easy: 0.65x
- Normal: 0.85x
- Hard: 1.0x
- Expert: 1.15x

Requirements:

- Reduce note movement speed.
- Reduce note spawn density.
- Increase spacing between consecutive notes.
- Reduce simultaneous note combinations on Easy.
- Increase reaction time.
- Preserve normal music playback speed and pitch.
- Keep hit detection and scoring synchronized.
- Ensure difficulty changes affect gameplay consistently.

IMPORTANT: Do not globally slow down the music or master audio clock.

## TASK 3: TESTING

Test all difficulty levels.

Verify:

- Notes reach the hit line at the correct time.
- No mismatch between note position and hit judgement.
- Easy mode is noticeably easier.
- Auto Dance remains synchronized with music.
- Pause, resume and seek work correctly.
- Video export uses the same dance timeline.

Before modifying code, inspect existing modules and reuse current architecture. Avoid duplicate systems and unnecessary rewrites.

After implementation, provide a report of changed files and actual test results.

Mình khuyên triển khai hai task này trước, chưa cần làm webcam. Đây là bước hợp lý để hoàn thiện gameplay và Auto Dance Engine.



I’ll inspect the existing choreography/pose system and the chart/difficulty pipeline first, then make the smallest changes that reuse the current architecture. The two areas are likely already connected through `AutoPoseProvider`, `Gameplay`, `BeatMapGenerator`, and the studio’s music clock.

The existing Auto Play/Video Studio choreographer is real and already full-body, but the game difficulty profiles still use ad-hoc absolute speeds and have no Expert tier. I’m tracing the gameplay clock, note positioning, difficulty UI, and studio pose update before changing those contracts.

The current game is first-person and has no avatar in the gameplay scene; Auto Play is the game’s existing non-webcam performance mode and already shares `AutoPoseProvider` with Video Studio. I’ll reuse that seam rather than inventing a second dance system, while adding a blend layer for style transitions and making the difficulty profiles explicit and testable.

I’ve confirmed the timing model is already sound: note position is a pure function of `songTime`, and judgement uses the same `songTime` with a separate hit window. I’m now changing only the chart difficulty data/spacing and the note transit speed, so the audio clock and judgement math stay untouched.













The difficulty profiles and chart spacing are in place. I’m running a pure timing/chart harness next to compare all four tiers, including note count, minimum spacing, Easy speed/reaction changes, and exact hit-line position math before touching the browser.




The headless harness passes all four tiers: Easy is **0.65×** speed with a **3.87 s** approach time and **1.21 notes/s**, versus Normal **2.96 s / 2.96 notes/s**, and no tier produces simultaneous notes. Next I’m exercising the actual Game transport and the shared Auto Dance stage in the browser.




The live checks now show the actual Game using the selected speed at every tier, exact hit-line arrival (`z = 0` at the note timestamp, `z = -78` at spawn), and clean pause/resume/seek. I’m doing one short WebCodecs render with instrumentation to confirm the export loop feeds the same `songTime` and avatar stage.

The short export smoke test passed: 60 frames, 1280×720 MP4, 2.020 s, with the final stage pose timestamp (`1.9667`) matching the export timeline. I’m updating the in-repo guide/report with the measured numbers and the one honest limitation: this browser panel reports itself hidden, so I validated state and timeline directly rather than claiming a foreground visual play-through.













Implemented. Full report: [`AUTO_DANCE_DIFFICULTY_REPORT.md`](AUTO_DANCE_DIFFICULTY_REPORT.md).

**Auto Dance**
- Surfaced the game-facing no-webcam mode as **AUTO DANCE**; it reuses the existing `AutoPoseProvider`, `ProceduralAvatar` and `SkeletonView`.
- Full-body motion: 17 joints across hips, spine, shoulders, arms, legs and head, driven by BPM, beat phase, energy, bass and section intensity.
- Added a **0.45 s procedural cross-fade** when changing dance styles, starting from the currently rendered pose instead of snapping.
- Video Studio uses the same stage/provider, so Auto Dance and export share one timeline.
- No `getUserMedia` or camera permission path is used. Future webcam support remains a separate provider seam.

**Difficulty**

| Mode | Speed | Lead time | Hit window | Same-lane gap |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Easy | 0.65× | 3.87 s | ±340 ms | 0.50 s |
| Normal | 0.85× | 2.96 s | ±280 ms | 0.24 s |
| Hard | 1.00× | 2.52 s | ±210 ms | 0.155 s |
| Expert | 1.15× | 2.19 s | ±170 ms | 0.11 s |

Easy now samples every other kick, skips off-beat hats, has no simultaneous notes, a lower sparse-chart floor, and a wider judgement window. Music playback speed and pitch were not changed; note travel speed and chart spacing did.

**Verified**
- `node qa/auto-dance-difficulty-harness.mjs` — **PASS**. Easy: 109 notes / 1.21 notes/s; Normal: 266 / 2.96; Hard: 316 / 3.51; Expert: 331 / 3.68. Zero simultaneous notes in all tiers.
- Actual Game starts at all four difficulties: Easy **20.15 u/s**, Normal **26.35**, Hard **31.00**, Expert **35.65**; gameplay and chart configs matched.
- First Easy note: position at its timestamp = `z 0` (hit line), spawn position = `z -78`; `judgeLane()` returned `delta 0`.
- Pause preserved song time, resume returned to `playing`, and seeking to 10 s reported `10.06 s` while remaining in `playing`.
- Auto Dance produced 17-joint poses; style cross-fade was active mid-transition and cleared after 0.45 s.
- A 2 s WebCodecs export produced 60 frames at 1280×720, MP4 H.264/AAC, 2.020 s duration, with the final pose timestamp matching the final export time.
- `npm run build` — **PASS**, 88 modules; JS 1,028.69 kB (gzip 280.34 kB), CSS 55.44 kB.

**Limitation:** the automated browser tab reports `visibilityState: "hidden"`, so I validated state, timing, stage updates and the offline export loop directly rather than claiming a visible foreground play-through. Interactive first-person Play remains unchanged; Auto Dance is the separate game-facing performance mode.

The next useful step is to make the performer optionally visible during interactive Play, if that is the “Game Mode” surface you want covered.


Kết quả triển khai này khá tốt. Mình đặc biệt đồng ý với việc giữ nguyên Music Clock và chỉ điều chỉnh tốc độ di chuyển, mật độ nốt và hit window.

Có 3 điểm mình muốn lưu ý:

1. Auto Dance: Đã tái sử dụng `AutoPoseProvider`, không cần webcam. Đây là hướng đúng.
2. Easy Mode: 0.65× và 1.21 notes/s là mức giảm đáng kể. Nên chơi thử thực tế để xác nhận người mới cảm thấy dễ chịu.
3. Video Export: Đã kiểm tra đồng bộ timeline, nhưng mới smoke test 2 giây, chưa đủ để xác nhận video dài hoạt động ổn định.

## Bước tiếp theo mình đề xuất

Nên triển khai Hiển thị nhân vật nhảy toàn thân ngay trong Game Mode.

Cụ thể:

- Thêm một khung nhân vật 3D ở bên phải màn hình.
- Hiển thị từ đầu đến chân, không bị cắt khi nhảy.
- Nhân vật tự động nhảy theo nhạc.
- Có thể bật/tắt khung nhân vật.
- Không ảnh hưởng đến gameplay góc nhìn thứ nhất.
- Dùng chung Auto Dance Engine với Video Studio.

Một điều quan trọng: Đừng tạo thêm một avatar engine mới. Hãy tái sử dụng stage, pose provider và animation hiện có.

Mình nghĩ đây sẽ là bước nâng cấp giúp NEON PULSE có bản sắc riêng hơn.

Writing

# NEON PULSE – ADD FULL-BODY AUTO DANCE AVATAR TO GAME MODE

## OBJECTIVE

Add a beautiful, full-body 3D dancing character to the existing interactive Game Mode.

The avatar must appear inside a dedicated panel on the RIGHT SIDE of the gameplay screen.

The character automatically dances in synchronization with the currently playing music.

IMPORTANT:

- No webcam required.
- No camera permission required.
- Do not replace the existing first-person gameplay.
- Do not create a new avatar or dance engine.
- Reuse the existing AutoPoseProvider, ProceduralAvatar, SkeletonView and shared music timeline.
- Preserve all existing gameplay mechanics.

## 1\. LAYOUT

Create a responsive two-area Game Mode layout.

LEFT SIDE:

- Existing first-person 3D gameplay.
- Keep the road, notes, obstacles, hit line and effects.
- Preserve existing keyboard and touch controls.

RIGHT SIDE:

- Dedicated 3D avatar viewport.
- Display the dancing character from head to feet.
- Keep the entire body visible during all dance movements.
- Use a visually appealing stage with neon lighting.
- Add subtle background effects that respond to music.

Suggested layout:

- Gameplay: 65–70% width.
- Avatar panel: 30–35% width.

On smaller screens, provide a suitable responsive layout rather than forcing both panels to remain side by side.

## 2\. FULL-BODY AVATAR

The avatar must:

- Show the complete body, including head, hands and feet.
- Never clip the head or feet during animations.
- Support arm extensions, jumps, turns and wide dance movements.
- Maintain natural body proportions.
- Use smooth skeletal animation.
- Have appropriate lighting and shadows.

Camera requirements:

- Use a dedicated camera for the avatar viewport.
- Automatically fit the entire character inside the viewport.
- Add sufficient margins around the character.
- Do not use the main gameplay camera.
- Avoid unnecessary camera movement.
- Preserve the full-body framing when resizing the viewport.

## 3\. AUTO DANCE ENGINE

Reuse the existing Auto Dance architecture.

Use:

- AutoPoseProvider
- ProceduralAvatar
- SkeletonView
- Existing animation and pose blending systems.

Requirements:

- Dance automatically while music is playing.
- Synchronize movement with BPM and beat phase.
- React to bass, music energy and musical sections.
- Include full-body movement:
  - Head
  - Shoulders
  - Arms
  - Spine
  - Hips
  - Legs
  - Feet
- Support smooth transitions between dance styles.
- Avoid repetitive robotic movement.
- Avoid sudden pose snapping.

The avatar must use the same master song timeline as gameplay.

## 4\. MUSIC SYNCHRONIZATION

This is critical.

The following systems must use the same songTime:

- Music playback
- Gameplay
- Note movement
- Auto Dance
- Avatar animation
- Music-reactive effects

Do not create a separate timer for the avatar.

Pause:

- Music pauses.
- Avatar animation freezes at the correct pose.
- Gameplay pauses.

Resume:

- All systems continue from the same timeline.

Seek:

- Avatar immediately updates to the correct pose for the new songTime.

Do not modify music playback speed or pitch.

## 5\. AVATAR CONTROLS

Add a small control panel with:

- Show / Hide Avatar
- Dance Style selector
- Male / Female character selector, if existing assets support both
- Avatar scale adjustment
- Stage lighting toggle

Keep the controls visually minimal and consistent with the existing NEON PULSE UI.

Remember the user's settings during the current session.

## 6\. VISUAL DESIGN

Design the avatar panel to feel like a professional music performance stage.

Suggested visual elements:

- Dark background.
- Cyan / purple neon lighting.
- Subtle floor reflections.
- Soft spotlight.
- Music-reactive particles.
- Minimal frame border.
- Character clearly separated from the background.

Do not overload the panel with excessive bloom or particles.

The avatar must remain the main visual focus.

## 7\. PERFORMANCE

Avoid introducing a second unnecessary render loop.

Requirements:

- Reuse existing rendering infrastructure where practical.
- Use an independent viewport/scissor rendering pass if compatible with the current renderer.
- Avoid creating duplicate WebGL contexts unnecessarily.
- Dispose of unused resources.
- Pause unnecessary rendering when the avatar is hidden.
- Maintain acceptable FPS on typical desktop hardware.

The avatar must not interfere with note detection, hit judgement or gameplay performance.

## 8\. VIDEO STUDIO COMPATIBILITY

The Game Mode avatar and Video Creator Studio must use the same:

- Avatar assets
- Pose provider
- Dance animation logic
- Music timeline
- Dance style definitions

Do not duplicate choreography logic.

Ensure the avatar can be rendered correctly in the existing video export pipeline.

## 9\. TESTING

Perform the following tests:

1. Start a song in Game Mode.
2. Verify the avatar automatically dances.
3. Verify full-body visibility during jumps and turns.
4. Verify synchronization with the music.
5. Pause and resume.
6. Seek forward and backward.
7. Change dance style during playback.
8. Hide and show the avatar.
9. Resize the browser window.
10. Switch between all four difficulty levels.
11. Verify gameplay scoring is unchanged.
12. Verify Video Creator Studio still works.
13. Verify MP4 export remains synchronized.

## 10\. IMPLEMENTATION RULES

Before modifying anything:

- Inspect the existing Game Mode layout.
- Inspect the existing Three.js renderer.
- Inspect AutoPoseProvider and ProceduralAvatar.
- Inspect the Video Studio avatar rendering pipeline.
- Identify the existing master music clock.

Reuse existing code wherever possible.

Do not rewrite working modules. Do not create duplicate avatar systems. Do not change existing gameplay scoring. Do not alter the audio playback speed. Do not remove existing features.

After implementation:

- Run production build.
- Run relevant automated tests.
- Test actual browser playback.
- Capture screenshots of the new Game Mode layout.
- Report all changed files.
- Report any remaining limitations honestly.

## FINAL ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA

The task is complete only when:

1. The existing game remains fully playable.
2. A full-body 3D avatar appears on the right side.
3. The avatar dances automatically without webcam.
4. The character is never cropped.
5. Music, gameplay and avatar remain synchronized.
6. Pause, resume and seek work correctly.
7. Performance remains acceptable.
8. Video Studio continues to work.
9. Production build passes.

The final result should look like a professional 3D rhythm game combined with a live dance performance stage.

Lưu ý: Mình muốn OpenCode ưu tiên kiểm tra cách render hai khu vực trong cùng một WebGL renderer trước khi tạo renderer thứ hai. Điều này giúp tránh hao GPU và giảm nguy cơ xung đột tài nguyên.



I’ll add the avatar as a true second viewport in the existing renderer, not a second game loop or duplicate avatar system. First I’m checking how `PostFX`, the canvas sizing and the HUD are structured so the split view doesn’t disturb note timing or input.

I’m implementing this as a lightweight secondary viewport fed by the same `Game._tick`: no new render loop, no second choreography engine. The panel will use one extra lightweight WebGL view only for its canvas, while `AutoPoseProvider`, `ProceduralAvatar` and the shared pose cross-fade remain single-source.




























The reuse layer is in place: `PoseBlend` is shared, and the new `GameAvatarView` consumes `AutoPoseProvider` and `ProceduralAvatar`, with optional `SkeletonView` rig overlay. I’m wiring the responsive HUD panel and controls now, then I’ll build and exercise it.

I’m adding the test/report documentation and doing the final production build.



















Implemented the Game Mode full-body dancer panel.

## What changed

- Added a dedicated right-side 3D avatar viewport inside Game Mode, with the existing first-person road, notes, hazards and scoring untouched on the left.

- Style change started the shared camera-safe cross-fade; hide/show and `sessionStorage` preferences worked.
- Four-style, 120-second full-body projection test: maximum NDC joint magnitude `0.768`, with no projected head/hand/foot clipping.

## Limitations

- The avatar viewport uses a second lightweight WebGL context so the existing gameplay camera and post-processing stack remain untouched. It is disposed with the game and skips rendering when hidden.
- At 1024 px and below, the dancer panel floats over the right side of the gameplay view rather than staying side-by-side; the lane buttons remain reachable and the panel can be hidden.
- The pause modal dims the avatar panel while the pose remains correctly frozen.
- The automated browser tab reports `document.visibilityState` as hidden and throttles RAF, so a visible-foreground FPS trace was not available. Render-budget measurements and direct `Game._tick()` checks were used instead.
- The desktop screenshot is a scaled capture of the 1280×720 layout; actual DOM measurements confirm the desktop panel size and lane clearance.

The detailed implementation and test report is in `GAME_MODE_AVATAR_REPORT.md`.

A natural next step is to tune the dancer frame so the character reads slightly larger while keeping the current arm-clearance margin.












































The Game Mode dancer polish is complete. The character is now framed **10.3% larger** (`FRAME_RADIUS` 1.28 → 1.16), while keeping the full body visible and leaving clearance for extended arms, jumps and turns.

## What changed

- **Framing:** tighter camera fit with centre Y at 1.1 m. The FOV and auto-fit architecture are unchanged.
- **Lighting:** a stronger neutral key light, cyan rim, purple fill and warm face fill. The face is no longer covered by the skeleton’s head marker, and viewport-only material lifts make the skin and costume readable on the darker stage.
- **Background:** the neon stage remains, but floor, grid, rings, beams and particles are more subdued. Particles went from 90 to 64; the panel glow, scanlines and vignette were also reduced.
- **No architecture changes:** the avatar is still driven by the existing `Game` render tick and `Game.lastSongTime`. No second render loop, no new dependencies, no changes to gameplay, scoring, music playback or difficulty.

- Female/male form changes re-applied the viewport materials and kept the face marker hidden.

Mình đã xem kỹ cả 2 ảnh. Mình hiểu vấn đề rồi: OpenCode đã làm sai cả concept lẫn bố cục 3D, chứ không đơn thuần là sai màu sắc hay hiệu ứng.

Bạn muốn game NEON PULSE giống ảnh thứ nhất, nhưng sản phẩm hiện tại ở ảnh thứ hai lại đi theo một phong cách hoàn toàn khác.

## 1. Phân tích sự khác biệt

Ảnh 1 – Thiết kế mục tiêu

Đúng concept

Ảnh 2 – Sản phẩm hiện tại

Sai concept

### Ảnh 1 có 5 đặc điểm quan trọng

1. Camera góc nhìn thứ nhất (First Person): Người chơi nhìn thẳng vào một đường hầm 3D, có chiều sâu và điểm tụ ở chính giữa.
2. Neon Geometry Tunnel: Các khung hình học lớn dạng tam giác, đa giác, đường thẳng phát sáng, đan xen màu cyan, hồng và vàng.
3. Reflective Floor: Sàn đen bóng như mặt nước, phản chiếu ánh sáng neon.
4. Dancer Silhouette: Một ô nhỏ bên trái hiển thị hình bóng người nhảy màu trắng trên nền đen.
5. Four Dance Pads: Bốn ô hình thang viền trắng nằm phía dưới, phối cảnh theo chiều sâu.

### Ảnh 2 đang sai ở đâu?

| Thành phần | Lỗi |
| --- | --- |
| Background | Dùng vòng tròn đồng tâm thay vì neon polygon tunnel |
| Camera | Cảm giác như game chạy trên đường ray |
| Sàn | Sàn tím phẳng, thiếu phản chiếu |
| Nhân vật | Đưa nhân vật 3D lớn sang bên phải |
| Dance pads | Dùng 3 vòng tròn thay vì 4 ô hình thang |
| UI | Quá nhiều HUD và thông tin |
| Hiệu ứng | Thiên về arcade rhythm game thay vì neon dance experience |

Điểm quan trọng: Không nên tiếp tục sửa từng chi tiết của ảnh 2. Nên yêu cầu tái thiết kế lại toàn bộ composition của game scene theo ảnh 1, đồng thời giữ lại những module xử lý nhạc và gameplay đang hoạt động.

## 2. Kiến trúc hình ảnh mình đề xuất

Fantastic Neon Tunnel VJ Loop by OlScher - Stock Video | Motion Array

[motionarray.com](<https://motionarray.com/stock-video/fantastic-neon-tunnel-vj-loop-1106211/?utm_source=chatgpt.com>)

Bố cục phải được chia thành 3 khu vực:

A. Main 3D World – 80–90% màn hình

- Neon tunnel chiếm toàn bộ background.
- Camera chính giữa.
- Điểm tụ nằm ở tâm màn hình.
- Sàn phản chiếu ánh sáng.
- Các khung neon chuyển động tiến về phía camera.

B. Dancer Silhouette – góc trái

- Khung hình chữ nhật màu đen.
- Nhân vật dạng silhouette trắng.
- Hiển thị chuyển động nhảy đồng bộ với nhạc.
- Không sử dụng avatar 3D lớn ở bên phải.

C. Dance Pads – phía dưới

- Chính xác 4 ô.
- Viền trắng mảnh.
- Mặt ô tối, trong suốt.
- Phối cảnh hình thang.
- Có hiệu ứng phát sáng khi tương tác.

## 3. Prompt để yêu cầu OpenCode sửa lại

Mình viết prompt này theo hướng bắt OpenCode sửa kiến trúc hiển thị, không tự ý sáng tạo thêm giao diện.

Writing

# NEON PULSE – VISUAL REDESIGN DIRECTIVE

## CRITICAL: REBUILD THE VISUAL COMPOSITION

The current implementation does NOT match the intended visual reference.

The target is the FIRST reference image, not the current implementation.

Do not attempt to fix this by simply changing colors, materials, or CSS.

Rebuild the main 3D scene composition to match the reference image as closely as possible.

Preserve the existing functional systems wherever possible:

- Audio engine
- Beat detection
- Music loading
- BPM detection
- Scoring
- Combo system
- Game timing
- Difficulty settings
- Existing music controls

Do not rewrite working systems unnecessarily.

---

## 1\. MAIN VISUAL CONCEPT

Create a futuristic first-person NEON DANCE TUNNEL.

The entire game world should look like the first reference image.

Visual characteristics:

- Symmetrical futuristic tunnel.
- Deep perspective with a central vanishing point.
- Large geometric neon frames.
- Triangular and polygonal structures.
- Cyan, magenta, pink and yellow neon lighting.
- Dark atmospheric environment.
- Glossy black reflective floor.
- Strong reflections on the floor.
- Cinematic volumetric atmosphere.
- High contrast between dark environment and neon lights.

IMPORTANT:

Remove the concentric circular tunnel design.

Do not use a conventional rhythm-game highway.

Do not use a purple flat floor.

Do not make the environment look like a generic arcade game.

The tunnel must be built using geometric neon structures, not circular rings.

## 2\. CAMERA AND PERSPECTIVE

Use a centered first-person camera.

Camera requirements:

- Position at the center of the tunnel.
- Looking straight toward the vanishing point.
- Symmetrical left/right composition.
- Wide-angle cinematic perspective.
- Strong depth perception.
- Stable camera with no unnecessary rotation.

The tunnel must extend far into the distance.

Neon frames should gradually become smaller toward the vanishing point.

## 3\. NEON GEOMETRIC TUNNEL

Build multiple layers of geometric structures.

Include:

- Large triangular neon frames.
- Irregular polygon frames.
- Angular diagonal neon lines.
- Interconnected geometric structures.
- Multiple depth layers.

Color distribution:

- Cyan: primary lighting.
- Magenta: secondary lighting.
- Pink: accent lighting.
- Yellow: occasional highlights.

Use emissive materials and bloom carefully.

The neon structures should feel physically present in a 3D environment.

## 4\. REFLECTIVE FLOOR

This is a critical visual requirement.

Create a dark glossy floor resembling black water.

Requirements:

- Strong neon reflections.
- Cyan and pink reflection streaks.
- Subtle animated ripple distortion.
- Dark central walkway.
- Reflections should follow the positions of the neon structures.

Avoid a flat purple floor or a simple grid floor.

## 5\. DANCER SILHOUETTE PANEL

Position a small rectangular dancer panel on the LEFT side of the screen.

Requirements:

- Black background.
- White human silhouette.
- Full-body figure.
- Thin glowing white outline.
- Simple rectangular frame.

The silhouette should animate according to the music.

Do not place a large 3D avatar panel on the right side.

Do not allow the dancer panel to dominate the main scene.

If the existing avatar animation system can be reused, reuse its motion data to drive the silhouette.

## 6\. FOUR DANCE PADS

At the bottom center of the screen, create EXACTLY FOUR dance pads.

Requirements:

- Four trapezoidal perspective shapes.
- Thin white outlines.
- Dark transparent interiors.
- Symmetrical horizontal arrangement.
- Perspective matching the tunnel floor.

These must look like four physical interaction zones.

Do not use three circular targets.

Do not use circular rings.

Do not replace the four pads with another rhythm-game interface.

Each pad should illuminate when activated by gameplay.

## 7\. UI DESIGN

Keep the interface minimal.

The 3D environment is the main attraction.

Avoid:

- Large player panels.
- Oversized avatar windows.
- Excessive HUD elements.
- Large central song titles covering the tunnel.
- Unnecessary decorative controls.

Only retain essential information:

- Score.
- Combo.
- Song progress.
- BPM.
- Pause button.

Use small, discreet typography.

## 8\. TECHNICAL REQUIREMENTS

Before modifying anything:

1. Inspect the existing source code.
2. Identify the current scene renderer.
3. Identify the camera implementation.
4. Identify the existing tunnel geometry.
5. Identify the dance pad implementation.
6. Identify the dancer panel implementation.
7. Identify dependencies between visual modules and gameplay modules.

Then implement the redesign.

Prefer reusing the existing Three.js renderer and scene architecture.

Avoid introducing additional WebGL contexts unless absolutely necessary.

Optimize:

- Draw calls.
- Geometry reuse.
- Emissive materials.
- Bloom post-processing.
- Reflection performance.
- Animation loops.

Maintain stable performance.

## 9\. STRICT ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA

The redesign is NOT complete unless all the following conditions are satisfied:

\[ \] First-person centered camera.

\[ \] Large geometric neon tunnel.

\[ \] No concentric circular tunnel.

\[ \] Cyan, magenta, pink and yellow neon structures.

\[ \] Dark glossy reflective floor.

\[ \] Strong visible floor reflections.

\[ \] Small dancer silhouette panel on the left.

\[ \] Exactly four trapezoidal dance pads at the bottom.

\[ \] No three circular dance targets.

\[ \] No large dancer panel on the right.

\[ \] Minimal HUD.

\[ \] Music and beat detection still work.

\[ \] Scoring and combo still work.

\[ \] Pause and resume still work.

\[ \] Existing music synchronization remains intact.

## 10\. IMPLEMENTATION AND VERIFICATION

Do not declare success based only on a successful build.

Run the application and capture an actual gameplay screenshot.

Compare the screenshot with the target reference image.

If the composition is still different, continue correcting it.

Report:

- Files modified.
- Components rebuilt.
- Components preserved.
- Build and runtime test results.
- Performance observations.
- Screenshot of the final result.

IMPORTANT FINAL INSTRUCTION:

The FIRST reference image is the visual specification.

Do not reinterpret it into a different rhythm-game design.

Visual fidelity to the reference takes priority over adding new decorative features.

## 4. Cách mình muốn bạn triển khai

Mình khuyên không giao OpenCode sửa tất cả một lần. Chia thành 3 giai đoạn:

1. Giai đoạn 1 – Visual: Chỉ dựng tunnel, camera, sàn phản chiếu và bốn dance pads. Chưa cần nhân vật.
2. Giai đoạn 2 – Character: Đưa dancer silhouette vào bên trái, kết nối chuyển động với audio.
3. Giai đoạn 3 – Gameplay: Kết nối lại beat detection, điểm số, combo và hiệu ứng tương tác.

Lý do: nếu sửa cả đồ họa, animation và gameplay cùng lúc, OpenCode có thể làm hỏng những module đang chạy tốt.

## 5. Mình muốn xác nhận một điểm quan trọng

Mình thống nhất thiết kế mục tiêu:

- Nhân vật: A – Silhouette trắng.

I’ll start by mapping the current scene, camera, tunnel, pad and HUD implementations, and checking whether the target reference image exists in the workspace. Then I’ll rebuild the composition around the existing gameplay/audio systems and verify it in the running game.

## What changed

**Rebuilt**
- `src/world/NeonTunnel.js` — new geometric tunnel made from instanced neon beams, polygonal frames, angular braces, haze planes, vanishing-point glow, and a transparent reflective floor with animated caustic/ripple shading. No torus or circular tunnel geometry.
- `src/world/DancePads.js` — exactly four trapezoidal pads at the bottom center, white/neon outlines, dark transparent fills, pulse illumination, and lane-to-pad hit mapping.
- `src/world/NeonWorld.js` — new World implementation using the tunnel, pads, particle field, impacts, and bursts.
- `src/game/DanceSilhouette.js` — left-side black panel with an animated filled white dancer silhouette driven by the existing `AutoPoseProvider`.
- `src/core/CameraRig.js` and `src/config.js` — centered first-person camera looking straight down the tunnel, stable framing, no lane lean/sway.
- `src/ui/templates.js`, `src/ui/UIManager.js`, `src/styles/main.css` — minimal HUD, left silhouette panel, four-pad mobile touch layer, old right-side avatar panel hidden.
- `src/objects/NoteManager.js` — notes changed from glowing spheres to angular shards; circular note/hazard rings hidden so they don’t read as targets.

**Preserved**
- Audio engine, music loading, BPM/beat detection, chart generation, scoring/combo, timing windows, difficulty settings, pause/resume/seek/restart, and the studio/video modules. The gameplay chart still uses its three internal lanes; lane 1 is visually represented by the two center pads.

## Verification

- `npm run build` passes: 94 modules, JS 1,055.24 kB, CSS 64.66 kB.
- Dev server runtime loaded successfully with no console errors.
- Ran `demo:neon-pulse` on `medium`; audio clock advanced and HUD reported `128 BPM`.
- Pause/resume verified: state changed to `paused` and back to `playing`, with playback time continuing.
- Scoring/combo verified with a real note hit through `Gameplay.handleLane`: perfect judgement, score `76`, combo `1`, HUD updated.
- Four pads confirmed present and visible; silhouette panel and minimal HUD confirmed.
- One-frame render cost: **50 draw calls, ~13.7k triangles, 22 geometries, 15 textures**, high-quality post-processing. 60 FPS was observed while the preview was foregrounded; the later preview tab was background-throttled, so its low FPS reading was not representative.

## Limitations

The first reference image was not available as a file in this workspace or through the session tools, so I could not perform a direct side-by-side comparison. This pass follows the written visual specification; if you reattach the reference image, I can tune frame density, tunnel scale, floor reflection strength, and pad proportions against it directly.

The final WebGL capture is native 1280×720. The HUD image is a scaled preview capture because the preview surface is narrower than the game viewport; its scene detail is softer than the native image.

Tuy nhiên, mình chưa muốn cho qua Giai đoạn 1 ngay. Có một số chi tiết cần kiểm tra và tinh chỉnh trước khi chuyển sang nhân vật và gameplay.

## 1. Đánh giá ảnh hiện tại

Đúng hướng thiết kế

| Hạng mục | Đánh giá |
| --- | --- |
| Camera chính giữa | Đạt |
| Tunnel hình học neon | Đạt |
| Màu cyan, magenta, vàng | Đạt |
| Silhouette bên trái | Đạt vị trí |
| Sàn phản chiếu | Cần cải thiện |
| Bốn dance pads | Cần kiểm tra |
| Tỷ lệ phối cảnh | Cần tinh chỉnh |

## 2\. So sánh trực tiếp với ảnh mục tiêu

Mục tiêu

Hiện tại

Mình nhận thấy 5 điểm khác biệt:

1\. Tunnel

- Mục tiêu: đường hầm có kiến trúc rõ ràng, các khung neon tạo thành hành lang có chiều sâu.
- Hiện tại: quá nhiều đường tam giác chồng chéo, tạo cảm giác mạng lưới hình học hỗn loạn.

2\. Sàn phản chiếu

- Mục tiêu: mặt sàn giống nước, có những mảng phản chiếu cyan, hồng và vàng rất rõ.
- Hiện tại: sàn khá tối, chủ yếu thấy các vệt sáng ngang. Chưa có cảm giác sàn ướt.

3\. Dance pads

- Mục tiêu: bốn ô hình thang lớn, rõ ràng, nằm sát tiền cảnh.
- Hiện tại: có các hình đỏ và vàng che khuất khu vực pads. Cần phân biệt rõ vật thể gameplay với mặt pad.

4\. Silhouette

- Mục tiêu: hình người toàn thân, có viền trắng, kích thước tương đối lớn ở bên trái.
- Hiện tại: panel quá nhỏ, hình người chưa thể hiện rõ dáng toàn thân.

5\. Không gian

- Mục tiêu: có hai vách tunnel trái/phải, tạo cảm giác đang đứng trong một hành lang 3D.
- Hiện tại: cảm giác đang đứng giữa một không gian wireframe mở.

## 3\. Chưa chuyển sang Giai đoạn 2

Mình đề xuất thêm một vòng tinh chỉnh Visual trước.

Đây là prompt bổ sung, không yêu cầu viết lại toàn bộ hệ thống:

Writing

# NEON PULSE – VISUAL STAGE 1 REFINEMENT

The first visual rebuild is a good foundation, but it still does not sufficiently match the original reference image.

IMPORTANT: This is a refinement task, NOT a complete rewrite.

Preserve the current working implementation and all gameplay/audio systems.

## 1\. TUNNEL ARCHITECTURE

The current tunnel has too many overlapping wireframe triangles.

Make it look like a physical futuristic corridor instead of an abstract geometric wireframe.

Required:

- Clear left and right tunnel walls.
- Strong central vanishing point.
- Large polygonal neon frames distributed along the tunnel.
- More structured spacing between frames.
- A visible architectural corridor.
- Cyan, magenta and yellow neon accents.

Reduce unnecessary intersecting lines.

Use a combination of large geometric frames and smaller structures in the distance.

## 2\. WET REFLECTIVE FLOOR

Improve the floor to resemble the reference.

Required:

- Wide dark wet floor.
- Visible cyan and magenta reflections.
- Yellow reflection accents.
- Irregular reflective patches.
- Subtle water-like distortion.
- A dark central walkway.

The floor must visibly reflect the tunnel geometry.

Do not rely only on horizontal glowing lines.

## 3\. FOUR DANCE PADS

Keep EXACTLY FOUR trapezoidal pads.

Match the reference proportions:

- Wider pads.
- Larger visible surface area.
- Positioned close to the bottom foreground.
- Equal spacing.
- Thin white outlines.
- Dark transparent interiors.

IMPORTANT: Gameplay notes must not permanently obscure the pads.

For visual comparison, create an idle scene with no active notes or hazards.

Check that all four pads are clearly distinguishable.

Remove any unintended oversized red or yellow geometry covering the pad area.

## 4\. DANCER SILHOUETTE

Improve the left-side dancer panel.

Match the reference:

- Larger rectangular panel.
- Black background.
- Full-body human silhouette.
- White glowing outline.
- Clearly visible head, arms, torso and legs.
- Centered within the panel.

Keep it a 2D silhouette, not a large 3D avatar.

## 5\. CAMERA AND COMPOSITION

Maintain the centered first-person camera.

Adjust the camera and tunnel scale to make the corridor feel deeper and more immersive.

The pads should occupy the lower foreground.

The vanishing point should remain near the center of the scene.

## 6\. VISUAL REGRESSION CHECK

Before declaring completion:

Capture a clean 1280x720 screenshot with:

- No active gameplay notes.
- All four pads visible.
- Full tunnel visible.
- Full dancer silhouette visible.
- Floor reflections clearly visible.

Compare against the original reference image.

Do not modify audio, chart generation, scoring, combo, timing or input logic.

Report all modified files and provide the final screenshot.

1111111111111 giai đoạn 2
Lần này mình thấy OpenCode đã xử lý đúng những vấn đề mà chúng ta yêu cầu ở vòng tinh chỉnh thứ nhất. Đặc biệt, việc chuyển từ wireframe hỗn loạn sang kiến trúc hành lang có vách, khung portal và điểm tụ rõ ràng là một quyết định đúng.

## 1. Đánh giá vòng tinh chỉnh thứ hai

Tiến bộ tốtDựa trên ảnh mới và báo cáo triển khai:

| Hạng mục | Đánh giá |
| --- | --- |
| Kiến trúc hành lang | Tốt hơn |
| Vách tunnel | Đã bổ sung |
| Portal hình học | Đã có cấu trúc |
| Điểm tụ trung tâm | Đã cải thiện |
| Sàn phản chiếu | Đã cải thiện |
| Bốn dance pads | Đã mở rộng |
| Silhouette bên trái | Đã cải thiện |
| Gameplay/audio | Được giữ nguyên theo báo cáo |

Một điểm mình đánh giá cao là OpenCode đã tạo ảnh idle không có note và hazard. Đây là cách kiểm tra giao diện nền rất đúng, tránh nhầm lẫn giữa lỗi thiết kế và hiệu ứng gameplay.

## 2. Có 3 việc cần kiểm tra trước khi duyệt Visual

Mình chưa muốn yêu cầu OpenCode chỉnh thêm ngay, vì có thể nó đã đạt mức cần thiết.

- Sàn: Phản chiếu phải xuất phát từ các cấu trúc neon, không chỉ là các dải sáng ngang.
- Pads: Phải nhìn rõ cả bốn hình thang, có phối cảnh và khoảng cách hợp lý.
- Silhouette: Phải thấy rõ hình người toàn thân, không bị biến thành hình khối trắng khó nhận dạng.

Một lưu ý: báo cáo hiện tại chưa có số liệu FPS khi chơi thực tế. 63 draw calls và 6.5k triangles là khá gọn về mặt hình học, nhưng 23 shader programs và post-processing vẫn cần kiểm tra trên thiết bị thật.

## 3. Mình đề xuất chuyển sang Giai đoạn 2

Đề xuất tiếp theo## Giai đoạn 2 – Dancer Silhouette Animation

Music | Cataleya Music

Stream OG Telly - Na' Gangsta Walk by Memphis Jookin | Listen online for free on SoundCloud

Street Dance Girl Style - iClone/Motion - Reallusion Content Store

Mục tiêu bây giờ là làm cho hình bóng người bên trái thực sự trở thành một dancer.

Mình muốn giữ đúng 4 nguyên tắc:

1. Hình người màu trắng, nền đen.
2. Toàn thân luôn nằm trong khung hình.
3. Chuyển động liên tục, tự nhiên, không giật.
4. Chuyển động đồng bộ với nhạc đang phát.

### Điều đặc biệt quan trọng

Không nên chỉ làm nhân vật nhún lên xuống theo BPM.

Cần có các nhóm chuyển động:

- Idle / Groove.
- Upper body movement.
- Arm choreography.
- Hip movement.
- Leg movement.
- Dance transitions.
- Accent movement theo kick và snare.

Nếu hệ thống `AutoPoseProvider` hiện tại đã có dữ liệu chuyển động, hãy tái sử dụng và cải thiện nó thay vì viết lại từ đầu.

## 4\. Prompt Giai đoạn 2 cho OpenCode

Mình đề xuất gửi prompt này sau khi bạn quyết định duyệt Visual.

Writing

# NEON PULSE – STAGE 2: DANCER SILHOUETTE ANIMATION

The Stage 1 visual environment is now the baseline.

Do not modify or redesign:

- Neon tunnel
- Camera
- Reflective floor
- Four dance pads
- Main HUD layout

The next task is to develop the left-side white dancer silhouette into a convincing music-synchronized dancing character.

## 1\. VISUAL IDENTITY

Keep the existing design:

- Solid white human silhouette.
- Black background.
- Full-body visibility.
- Small rectangular panel on the left.
- Clear human anatomy.
- No facial details.
- No clothing textures.
- No colored avatar.

The silhouette should remain visually consistent with the original reference.

## 2\. MOVEMENT SYSTEM

Inspect and reuse the existing AutoPoseProvider.

Improve the animation system with distinct movement families:

- Idle groove
- Upper body movement
- Shoulder movement
- Arm choreography
- Hip movement
- Leg movement
- Body turns
- Dynamic dance transitions

Avoid repetitive vertical bouncing.

The character should look like a person dancing to music, not a mechanical object responding to BPM.

## 3\. MUSIC SYNCHRONIZATION

Use the existing audio engine and audio clock.

Synchronize movement using:

- BPM
- Beat phase
- Kick intensity
- Snare intensity
- Frequency energy
- Music sections and transitions where available

Movement should remain continuous between beats.

Use musical accents to emphasize important movements rather than restarting the entire animation on every beat.

## 4\. ANIMATION QUALITY

Requirements:

- Smooth interpolation.
- Natural transitions.
- Balanced body proportions.
- Stable feet.
- No sudden limb snapping.
- No impossible joint angles.
- No disappearing body parts.
- No clipping outside the panel.

Use deterministic movement based on the audio clock so seeking and restarting do not cause unpredictable animation states.

## 5\. PERFORMANCE

Keep the silhouette lightweight.

Avoid creating another WebGL renderer.

Prefer the existing rendering pipeline or a lightweight 2D canvas/SVG implementation if appropriate.

Do not increase GPU load unnecessarily.

## 6\. VERIFICATION

Test with at least three different musical characteristics:

- Slow music.
- Medium-tempo music.
- Fast music.

Check:

- Animation continuity.
- Beat synchronization.
- Natural movement.
- Pause/resume.
- Seek/restart synchronization.
- Full-body visibility.

Capture screenshots and a short gameplay recording if possible.

Do not declare completion based on build success alone.

Report modified files, preserved systems and actual runtime test results.

Mình muốn thống nhất một điều: NEON PULSE không nên chỉ là game bấm phím theo nhạc. Phần dancer silhouette phải tạo được cảm giác như một màn trình diễn dance thực sự, còn tunnel là sân khấu tạo chiều sâu cho màn trình diễn đó.

Bạn có thể gửi thêm ảnh native 1280×720 (`stage1-final-native.png`) nếu muốn mình duyệt sát hơn trước khi chuyển giai đoạn. Hiện tại mình nghiêng về việc giữ nguyên Visual và bắt đầu Giai đoạn 2, không tiếp tục chỉnh tunnel vô hạn.



I’ll keep the Stage 1 stage composition untouched and work only on the silhouette pipeline. I’m reading the existing auto-pose generator, its joint rig, the game’s silhouette update path, and analyzer fields before adding the music-driven movement director.

The existing provider already produces a solid base groove, so I’m adding a deterministic choreography layer on top of it rather than replacing it. That layer will select and crossfade movement families, add kick/snare accents and section-based emphasis, ground the support foot, and keep the pose tied to the song clock.

























The core animation now validates cleanly (no clipping across 225 sampled poses, stable feet, exact seek determinism). I found one remaining discontinuity at music-section boundaries, so I’m adding a short deterministic crossfade from the previous section before final capture.
















Stage 2 is implemented and running, building on the existing silhouette rather than replacing it.

## Animation changes

- Added `src/game/DanceChoreography.js`:
  - Seven movement families: idle groove, upper body, shoulders, arms, hips, legs and body turns.
  - Deterministic 8-beat family cycle with crossfades.
  - Section-aware emphasis and a 0.9-second section-change crossfade.
  - Kick and snare accents layered over the movement instead of resetting it.
  - Grounded support foot, knee rebuilding, arm-length limits and body-turn projection.
- Updated `src/game/DanceSilhouette.js`:
  - Uses `AutoPoseProvider` through the new choreography layer.
  - Accepts kick, snare, high and low analyzer inputs.
  - Full-body fit is now centred with safe panel margins.
  - Added readable foot strokes and kept the silhouette solid white.
- Updated `src/core/Game.js`:
  - Passes `kick`, `snare`, `high`, `low` and the previous section to the dancer.
  - Derives the previous section from chart structure so seeking is deterministic.
  - No scoring, audio, chart-generation, combo, timing or input logic changed.
- Smoothed seeded arm variation at bar/phrase boundaries in `src/studio/pose/AutoPoseProvider.js`.

## Preserved

The neon tunnel, camera, reflective floor, four pads and HUD layout/styles were not redesigned. The existing 2D canvas and game tick are still used; no second WebGL renderer was added.

## Runtime verification

- `npm run build` passed: **95 modules**, JS **1,068.02 kB**, CSS **64.66 kB**.
- Live game reported **60 FPS**.
- **225 sampled poses** across 70 BPM breakdown, 128 BPM drop and 170 BPM build-up:
  - Clipping: **0**
  - NaN joints: **0**
  - Anatomy/limb violations: **0**
  - Stable support ankle at **0.035 m**, ground drift: **0**
- Smoothness at 60 FPS sampling:
  - 70 BPM: max joint movement **0.01038 m**
  - 128 BPM: **0.01447 m**
  - 170 BPM: **0.02587 m**
- Pause: canvas and pose remained unchanged.
- Resume: playback advanced and animation resumed.
- Seek: seeking `8 → 20 → 8` produced an identical pose, delta **0**.
- Restart: re-entered the intro with the demo track loaded and a fresh silhouette.
- Section transition: crossfaded boundary movement **0.01855 m**, compared with **0.01687 m** normal movement just after the transition.

## Evidence

## Limitations

- The only built-in track is 128 BPM. Slow and fast validation used synthetic BPM/section/analyzer inputs against the real choreography renderer, not separate slow/fast audio files.
- The original reference image was not available in the workspace, so this preserves the established silhouette style rather than matching a reference pixel-for-pixel.
- The browser preview surface is 421 px wide; the native 1280 scene and silhouette canvases were captured directly. A full desktop HUD screenshot was therefore not produced.
- The supplied clip is generated from the actual silhouette canvas, not a full-screen gameplay recording.

The next natural pass is to push the arm silhouettes toward more recognisable dance gestures while keeping this timing and section system intact.

Nếu bạn muốn tiếp tục, đây là phần giao việc tiếp theo:

Writing

# NEON PULSE – STAGE 2.5: CHOREOGRAPHY QUALITY POLISH

The Stage 2 animation architecture is working correctly.

Do NOT rewrite DanceChoreography or AutoPoseProvider.

Do NOT modify the tunnel, camera, floor, dance pads or HUD.

The objective is to improve the visual quality and recognizability of dance movements.

## 1\. RECOGNIZABLE DANCE POSES

Review all seven movement families.

Make each family visually distinct.

Prioritize:

- Clear arm gestures.
- Arms crossing and opening.
- Overhead arm movements.
- Side extensions.
- Shoulder isolations.
- Hip shifts.
- Side steps.
- Weight transfers.
- Controlled body turns.
- Dynamic stance changes.

Avoid repetitive small joint oscillations.

## 2\. CHOREOGRAPHY VARIATION

Introduce meaningful pose variations across phrases.

Do not simply repeat the same eight-beat movement cycle indefinitely.

Create a coherent sequence:

- Intro groove.
- Main dance phrase.
- Strong accent phrase.
- Variation phrase.
- Recovery groove.
- Transition.

Keep all movement deterministic and seek-safe.

## 3\. MUSIC RESPONSIVENESS

Maintain smooth continuous movement.

Use:

- Beat phase for rhythmic timing.
- Kick for grounded accents.
- Snare for upper-body gestures.
- High frequencies for smaller gestures.
- Section transitions for choreography changes.

Do not trigger a complete pose reset on every beat.

## 4\. VISUAL QUALITY

Maintain:

- Solid white silhouette.
- Black background.
- Full-body framing.
- Clear limb separation.
- Natural body proportions.
- No clipping.

Make the silhouette readable even at small panel sizes.

## 5\. TESTING

Test using actual audio recordings with different tempos and musical characteristics, not only synthetic BPM values.

Capture a video demonstrating:

- At least three distinct movement families.
- Smooth transitions.
- Full-body movement.
- Kick and snare responsiveness.
- Pause/resume.
- Seek/restart.

Do not modify the Stage 1 environment or gameplay systems.

Report exactly what changed and include the animation video.

## 5. Kế hoạch NEON PULSE hiện tại

1. Giai đoạn 1 – Visual
   Đã hoàn thành theo báo cáoTunnel, camera, reflective floor, 4 pads, HUD.
2. Giai đoạn 2 – Character
   Đã hoàn thành nền tảngSilhouette, choreography, audio synchronization.

   Nên thực hiệnLàm động tác nhảy đẹp, đa dạng và có cá tính.
4. Giai đoạn 3 – Gameplay
   Tiếp theoKết nối hoàn chỉnh note, dance pads, hit detection, scoring, combo và hiệu ứng.

## What changed

### `src/game/DanceChoreography.js`

- Added a **124-beat phrase sequence**:
  - `intro-groove` — 16 beats
  - `main-dance` — 32 beats
  - `accent` — 16 beats
  - `variation` — 32 beats
  - `recovery` — 16 beats
  - `transition` — 12 beats
- Phrase bias blends continuously into the family selection and phrase shape, so it no longer repeats an eight-beat cycle.
- Extended the family span to **12 beats**.
- Added distinct per-family arm silhouettes:
  - `groove` — low, open arms
  - `upper` — crossed, chest-level arms
  - `shoulders` — wide shoulder isolations
  - `arms` — raised/overhead and side-opening arms
  - `hips` — hands travelling to the hips/waist
  - `legs` — low open arms with side steps and knee lifts
  - `turn` — low crossed arms framing a controlled shoulder/body turn
- Increased the arm-family blending sharpness while keeping it continuous.
- Strengthened hip shifts, chest counter-rotation, side steps, weight transfers, knee lifts and shoulder-line turns.
- Kept kick, snare and high-frequency accents as overlays, not full-pose resets.
- All motion remains a pure function of song time, BPM, seed, section and analyzer values, so seeks and restarts resolve deterministically.

### `src/game/DanceSilhouette.js`

- Kept the solid white silhouette and black background.
- Kept black separation strokes around overlapping limbs, but removed them from torso-chain edges so the body reads as one solid shape rather than segmented tiles.
- Added foot strokes for grounding.
- Added a conditional black halo around the head only when a wrist is raised above the head.
- Preserved full-body fitting, safe margins and centering for raised arms, turns and knee lifts.

### Validation added

- `qa/stage25-choreography-harness.mjs` — deterministic family/phrase, finite-joint, grounded-foot, panel-fit, smoothness and seek-safety checks.
- `qa-artifacts/stage25-choreography-validation.json` — latest results.

Nothing was changed in `AutoPoseProvider`, `Game.js`, the tunnel, camera, floor, dance pads or HUD.

## Actual-audio testing

| Chopin — Nocturne Op. 9 No. 2, 239.5 s | 124.5 | kick `0.36`, snare `0.10` | pause at `11.34 s` held exactly; resume to `11.65 s`; seek `31.65 → 31.86 s` |
| Clementi — Sonatina Op. 36 No. 1, 107.5 s | 175 | kick `0.16`, snare `0.065` at sampled peak | actual playback and silhouette updates verified |

## Verification

- `npm run build` passed:
  - 95 modules
  - JS `1,070.55 kB` (`293.02 kB` gzip)
  - CSS `64.66 kB`
- Harness passed at **528 samples per BPM** for Brahms, Chopin and Clementi:
  - all seven families present
  - all six phrases present
  - finite 17-joint full body
  - pose fits a `152×203` panel with `25.67 px` minimum margin
  - grounded foot minimum `0.035`
  - maximum sampled joint speed `2.61–4.15 m/s` depending on tempo
  - deterministic seek pose, family and phrase
- Final video inspected through extracted frame sheets; it is `28.000 s`, H.264/AAC, and the audio stream is present and non-silent (`mean_volume -30.1 dB`).

The browser console showed no JavaScript runtime errors. During the extended capture, the existing Stage 1 renderer emitted repeated `WebGL: INVALID_OPERATION: useProgram: program not valid` warnings; I did not alter that environment because this pass was scoped to choreography and silhouette quality.

## 2. Một vấn đề kỹ thuật cần lưu ý

Cần theo dõiOpenCode báo cáo:

`WebGL: INVALID_OPERATION: useProgram: program not valid`

Đây là cảnh báo đáng chú ý.

Nó xuất hiện trong quá trình capture kéo dài, thuộc renderer của Stage 1.

Mình không muốn bỏ qua lỗi này khi bước sang gameplay vì nó có thể liên quan đến shader hoặc WebGL program lifecycle.

Chưa thể kết luận nó ảnh hưởng đến gameplay, nhưng nên yêu cầu kiểm tra và xử lý trước khi phát hành.

Ngoài ra, tốc độ khớp tối đa 2.61–4.15 m/s trong harness là số liệu cần xem xét theo ngữ cảnh, không tự động chứng minh chuyển động trông tự nhiên.

## Gameplay Integration

Hexagroove: Tactical DJ - Ichigoichie Games

《音灵 INVAXION》X ilem 联动开启，限时折扣+ DLC限免奉上！\_新浪游戏\_手机新浪网

Save 50% on Dance Dash on Steam

Mục tiêu của giai đoạn này là kết nối toàn bộ hệ thống thành một trải nghiệm game thống nhất.

Mình muốn có 6 phần:

1. Note spawning: Nốt xuất hiện từ điểm tụ trong tunnel và di chuyển về phía người chơi.
2. Four-pad mapping: Bốn pads thực sự tương ứng với hệ thống input, không chỉ là hình trang trí.
3. Hit detection: Người chơi tương tác đúng thời điểm nhạc.
4. Scoring: Perfect, Great, Good, Miss.
5. Combo: Hiển thị chuỗi hit liên tục.
6. Visual feedback: Pads phát sáng, tunnel phản ứng và dancer có accent theo nhịp.

## 4. Prompt Giai đoạn 3 cho OpenCode

Mình đề xuất giao việc theo hướng bảo toàn các hệ thống đã hoàn thiện.

Writing

# NEON PULSE – STAGE 3: GAMEPLAY INTEGRATION

Stage 1 visual redesign and Stage 2/2.5 dancer choreography are the established baseline.

The objective is to integrate the complete rhythm gameplay experience with the new four-pad visual environment.

CRITICAL: Do not redesign the tunnel, camera, reflective floor, silhouette panel or HUD composition.

Do not replace the existing audio engine, chart generator, scoring engine or timing system without demonstrating a concrete technical necessity.

## 1\. AUDIT EXISTING GAMEPLAY

Before changing code, inspect:

- NoteManager.js
- DancePads.js
- Gameplay.js
- Game.js
- InputManager.js
- AudioEngine.js
- Chart generation
- Scoring and combo logic

Document how the existing three internal gameplay lanes map to the four visual pads.

Do not assume that three internal lanes can be mapped to four pads correctly without inspecting the current implementation.

Resolve the lane-to-pad mapping explicitly.

## 2\. NOTE VISUALIZATION

Notes should emerge from the tunnel vanishing point and approach the player.

Requirements:

- Strong perspective depth.
- Clear direction of movement.
- Distinguishable note shapes.
- Readable colors.
- Accurate timing against the audio clock.
- Notes must visually align with their corresponding pads.

Avoid excessive particle effects.

Notes must not obscure the four pads unnecessarily.

## 3\. FOUR-PAD GAMEPLAY

The four trapezoidal pads must become actual gameplay interaction zones.

Requirements:

- Exactly four visual pads.
- Each pad has a clearly defined input mapping.
- Correct lane-to-pad correspondence.
- Active pad illumination.
- Hit feedback.
- Miss feedback.
- Consistent desktop and mobile input behavior.

- Good

- Generate a restrained impact effect.
- Add a brief neon pulse.
- Trigger appropriate dancer accent behavior.

On misses:

- Do not introduce excessive screen shake.

The tunnel and floor should respond to musical events without reducing visual clarity.

## 6\. DANCER INTEGRATION

Keep the current DanceChoreography system.

The dancer should:

- Continue dancing during gameplay.
- React to music continuously.
- Maintain deterministic seek/restart behavior.
- Remain visually readable.
- Never reset its pose abruptly on a successful hit.

Investigate the previously observed:

WebGL: INVALID\_OPERATION: useProgram: program not valid

Identify its source and reproduce it if possible.

Do not suppress the warning without identifying its cause.

Fix only if the issue is reproducible and attributable to the application.

Stage 3 is implemented, verified against real audio, and the WebGL warning is traced to a real bug and fixed.

## 1. What the audit found

The app was genuinely a **three-lane game wearing four pads**:

| Before | Reality |
|---|---|
| `LANE_COUNT = 3`, `LANE_X = [-2.6, 0, 2.6]` | centre-lane notes at `x = 0` land between the two centre pads |
| `DancePads._padsForLane` | lane 0 → pad 0, lane 1 → **pads 1 and 2**, lane 2 → pad 3 |
| Charts / obstacles | lanes 0–2 only |
| Input | A/S/D + 3 numbers, 3 arrow keys |
| Touch DOM | `data-lane` 0, 1, 1, 2 (two zones fired the same lane) |

Three lanes cannot map onto four pads without one pad pair sharing a lane, so I made the chart **four real lanes** instead of keeping the workaround. Timing windows, scoring, combo, audio clock and the chart-generator architecture were kept; only the lane vocabulary and patterns changed.

## 2. Four-pad input mapping

| Lane | World X | Pad | Keyboard | Alternates | Touch zone |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | −3 | pad 1 (cyan) | **A** | ←, 1, Num1 | `.hud__pad-touch [data-lane="0"]` |
| 1 | −1 | pad 2 (magenta) | **S** | ↑, 2, Num2 | `[data-lane="1"]` |
| 2 | +1 | pad 3 (pink) | **D** | ↓, 3, Num3 | `[data-lane="2"]` |
| 3 | +3 | pad 4 (gold) | **F** | →, 4, Num4 | `[data-lane="3"]` |

`PAD_X` is now literally `LANE_X`, so pad centres and lane centres cannot drift. `_padsForLane()` returns `[lane]` — one pad per lane, no sharing.

## 3. Files modified

- `src/config.js` — 4 lanes, `LANE_X = [-3,-1,1,3]`, full key map, `LANE_KEYS`
- `src/audio/BeatMapGenerator.js` — imports `LANE_COUNT` from config, rewrote the easy/normal/hard/expert lane patterns over 4 lanes
- `src/objects/NoteManager.js` — 4 lane material sets, 4 lane colours, slightly larger shard/ring, less white wash so lane colour reads
- `src/world/DancePads.js` — `PAD_X = LANE_X`, 1:1 `_padsForLane`
- `src/world/NeonTunnel.js` — **WebGL fix** (below)
- `src/game/DanceSilhouette.js` — hit accent overlay + `dt`, cleared on seek
- `src/core/Game.js` — judge events drive the dancer accent, `dt` forwarded
- `src/ui/templates.js` — 4 HUD lane buttons (A/S/D/F), 4 touch zones, how-to copy, footer “4 LANES”
- `src/world/HitLine.js` — legacy judgement line now defaults to `LANE_COUNT`
- `qa-gameplay-harness.js`, `qa/auto-dance-difficulty-harness.mjs` — four-lane QA
- new: `qa-artifacts/stage3-gameplay-validation.json`

The tunnel, camera, reflective floor composition, silhouette panel and HUD layout are untouched. `DanceChoreography` is untouched — the accent is a canvas overlay drawn *after* the pose, so a hit can never reset it (verified: identical joint data before/after an accent, and identical pose for the same song time after seek).

## 4. The WebGL warning — reproduced and fixed

It was not a capture artifact. On a fresh load the console flooded within seconds:

- One `ShaderMaterial` had `diagnostics.runnable = false`, log: `Fragment shader is not compiled.` / `ERROR: 0:80: 'patch' : Illegal use of reserved word`.
- Source: the floor shader in `src/world/NeonTunnel.js` declared `float patch`. `patch` is reserved GLSL (tessellation), so the program never linked and every frame called `useProgram` on it.

Renamed to `wetPatch`. After that: **0 broken programs, `gl.getError() === 0`, and zero `useProgram` warnings** across a ~100 s gameplay session and on a fresh reload. Nothing was suppressed.

Side effect worth stating plainly: the floor shader had been silently failing, so this **restores the intended wet-floor shader** that Stage 1 designed. It is a visible change caused by fixing the bug, not a redesign.

## 5. Measurements (real audio, live build)

**Charts** — Clementi 175 BPM: 404 notes, lanes `[99,100,99,106]`; Chopin 124.5 BPM: 664 notes, `[169,164,168,163]`; procedural 96 BPM: `[6,8,6,6]`, 0 out-of-range lanes.

**Input, per lane** — A/S/D/F each judged exactly 1 note in their own lane and lit only their own pad `[0]/[1]/[2]/[3]`. All four touch zones did the same.

**Judgements (Hard: 210 ms window, 60 ms perfect)** — +5 ms → `perfect` 170 pts, +110 ms → `great` 128, +190 ms → `good` 85, +300 ms → `miss` 0. Combo built 1→2→3→4 then reset to 0 on a miss.

**No double hit / no duplicate score** — second press on an already-judged note: `scoreDelta 0`, `judgedDelta 0`, no judge event.

**No false hits** — pressing a lane at a moment with no note inside the window: 0 judged, 0 score, no event, only that pad pulsed (0.35 free-tap reward). Miss feedback: pad 2 only, `success = false`.

**Transport** — pause moved the clock 0.8 ms, resume advanced 0.46 s, seek 20 s landed at 20.16 s with the chart and note count preserved. Restart rebuilt the session at four lanes.

**Note/audio alignment** — worst sampled visual error 0.33 m at 31 m/s ≈ **11 ms, under one frame**; notes sat at `x = -3, -1, 1, 3`, exactly the pad centres.

**Runtime** — 120 frames: 16.598 ms average, p95 16.8 ms, **60.03 FPS** at 432×780; 140 draw calls, 14,274 triangles, 24 programs, 56 MB heap after 104 s and 411 judged notes. One 56 FPS reading occurred while the screen recorder was capturing.

## 6. Deliverables

![Stage 3 four-pad gameplay](screenshots/stage3-gameplay-desktop.png)

Desktop frame: all four pads lit, one colour per lane, live impact and HUD. `screenshots/stage3-gameplay-mobile.png` is the portrait capture, and the real-audio clip is `screenshots/stage3-gameplay.mp4` (26 s, H.264/AAC, 420×800, Clementi audio muxed from the recorded song position).

**Console report:** clean. No errors or warnings on a fresh load; no WebGL warnings during gameplay. Two unrelated `net::ERR_FILE_NOT_FOUND` resource 404s appeared in one long session and did not recur after reload — nothing to do with rendering.

**Build:** `npm run build` exit 0 — 95 modules, JS 1,072.07 kB (293.60 kB gzip), CSS 64.66 kB.

## 7. Limitations I did not hide

- **Portrait framing:** at ~421–432 px wide the fixed 70° camera only fits the two inner pads horizontally; all four are in frame from roughly 16:10 upwards. Fitting ±3.7 units at pad depth in portrait needs a ~118° vertical FOV or moving the pads — both forbidden by the brief, so I left the composition alone. Input still covers all four lanes on mobile.
- Automated input tests mute the audio element (autoplay policy); the video carries the real track audio, aligned to within ~0.3 s.
- Autoplay hits every note, so hit-FX density in the stills/video is heavier than human play would produce.
- The `.lanes` HUD buttons stay hidden by the existing Stage 1 CSS; mobile input is the invisible pad-touch grid, unchanged in design.
- Not checked: a human click-through of every screen, or phone hardware — everything above ran in the local browser at desktop and portrait viewport sizes.

If you want the outer pads visible in portrait as well, that is a camera/FOV or pad-placement decision rather than a gameplay one.

Mình đánh giá đây là một bước tiến rất lớn của NEON PULSE. Đặc biệt, OpenCode đã phát hiện và sửa được hai vấn đề quan trọng: game thực chất chỉ có 3 lane và shader sàn phản chiếu bị lỗi biên dịch.

Mình nghiêng về việc duyệt Stage 3 về mặt logic và tích hợp, nhưng chưa nên tuyên bố hoàn thành toàn bộ sản phẩm.

## 1. Những điểm đã làm rất tốt

Đạt theo báo cáo kiểm thử### Chuyển từ 3 lane sang 4 lane thực sự

Đây là thay đổi quan trọng nhất.

## Four-pad mapping

# 1

## A

X = −3

# 2

## S

X = −1

# 3

## D

X = +1

# 4

## F

X = +3

Mỗi lane tương ứng chính xác một pad, không còn chia sẻ lane giữa hai pad.

Việc sửa đồng bộ chart generator, NoteManager, DancePads và InputManager là cách làm đúng.

### Scoring

| Judgement | Timing | Điểm |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Perfect | +5 ms | 170 |
| Great | +110 ms | 128 |
| Good | +190 ms | 85 |
| Miss | +300 ms | 0 |

Các kiểm tra double-hit, false-hit và combo reset đều có kết quả tốt.

### Shader sàn phản chiếu

Đây là phát hiện đáng giá nhất ngoài phần gameplay.

Lỗi `patch` trong GLSL khiến shader không thể biên dịch. Việc đổi tên thành `wetPatch` đã khôi phục shader thực sự thay vì che giấu cảnh báo.

Điều này có nghĩa Stage 1 trước đây chưa từng hiển thị đúng hoàn toàn hiệu ứng sàn phản chiếu như thiết kế.

## 2. Ba vấn đề mình chưa muốn bỏ qua

### A. Portrait chưa hiển thị đủ bốn pads

Cần xử lýOpenCode xác nhận camera hiện tại chỉ hiển thị hai pad giữa ở màn hình điện thoại.

Đây là vấn đề UX quan trọng. Người chơi mobile phải nhìn thấy được cả bốn khu vực tương tác.

Mình không đồng ý với việc mặc định chấp nhận hạn chế này chỉ vì không muốn thay đổi camera.

Giải pháp nên thử:

- Responsive camera FOV riêng cho portrait.
- Điều chỉnh khoảng cách camera theo aspect ratio.
- Giữ nguyên phối cảnh và vị trí pads tương đối.
- Không thay đổi camera desktop.

### B. 140 draw calls

60 FPS là tốt, nhưng 140 draw calls và 24 shader programs vẫn cần theo dõi trên điện thoại cấu hình trung bình.

Chưa cần tối ưu vội. Cần benchmark trên thiết bị thật trước.

### C. Chưa có kiểm thử người chơi thực tế

Autoplay 75-perfect combo không chứng minh gameplay có cảm giác tốt.

Cần thử:

- Người chơi bấm bằng bàn phím.
- Người chơi chạm màn hình.
- Phản hồi pad có nhanh không.
- Note có dễ nhìn không.
- Timing window có hợp lý không.

## 1. Responsive camera framing

New `src/world/StageFraming.js` derives the whole playfield layout from the viewport aspect ratio, and `Game.resize()` applies it:

- **FOV opens as the viewport narrows** (70° → max 92°) — a uniform perspective widen, no stretching.
- **The pad row and judgement depth slide deeper** until the outermost pad corner fits with a 6% margin. Pad size, trapezoid proportions and lane spacing are untouched; only depth changes, using the existing perspective.
- **Wide viewports resolve to exactly the desktop numbers**, so `1280×720` and `1920×1080` are unchanged: fov 70, pads 7.2→3.8, hit line 0.
- The **vanishing point stays centred** (camera position and look-at are untouched).
- Notes are now judged at a depth *inside* the pad row on narrow layouts, so they land on their pad instead of stopping short of it.

| Viewport | FOV | Pad near→far Z | Judged Z | Outer pad NDC X | All four visible |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 360×800 | 90.4° | 2.09 → −1.31 | −0.63 | −0.938 … 0.938 | yes |
| 390×844 | 90.0° | 2.25 → −1.15 | −0.47 | −0.938 … 0.938 | yes |
| 430×932 | 90.0° | 2.24 → −1.16 | −0.48 | −0.938 … 0.938 | yes |
| 1280×720 | 70° | 7.2 → 3.8 | 0 | −0.82 … 0.82 | yes (unchanged) |
| 1920×1080 | 70° | 7.2 → 3.8 | 0 | −0.82 … 0.82 | yes (unchanged) |

`qa/stage35-framing-check.mjs` asserts this by projecting every pad corner through a real `PerspectiveCamera` — exit 0.

The invisible touch zones were pinned to the bottom of the screen, which no longer lined up with the pads, so they now project to the pad row (portrait: top 49.5%, height 18%). Desktop keeps the old bottom strip.

![Portrait gameplay at 360×800](screenshots/stage35-360x800.png)

## 2. Touch gameplay

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Four zones → four lanes | each emits its own lane, judges 1 note, lights only its own pad |
| Duplicate pointerdown (same pointer) | `judgedDelta 0`, `scoreDelta 0` |
| Multi-touch (two zones at once) | both zones pressed, lane events `[0, 3]` |
| Release outside the zone | pressed state cleared — added a window-level release net |
| Scrolling/gestures | `touch-action: none`, `user-select: none`, `overflow: hidden`, `scrollY 0` |
| Keyboard vs touch parity | identical 60 pts / 100% / Perfect ×1 at the same delta |

Pointer capture is now taken on press and released on up/cancel, so a finger sliding off a pad can't leave it stuck or drop the release.

## 3. Note readability

- Notes now use **exactly the four pad colours** (`LANE_COLORS` in config, shared by `DancePads` and `NoteManager`) instead of theme-derived hues that collapsed to cyan/blue/teal — lane colour is literally the pad colour.
- Shard 0.62, ring 0.90 with lower additive opacity (0.46 / 0.34 + pulse) so the halo no longer washes out the silhouette.
- Notes sit at `x = −3, −1, 1, 3`, i.e. the pad centres, and are judged inside the pad on narrow layouts.

One visible consequence: because notes and pads are fixed to pad hues, they no longer recolour with the 12 environments. Pads never did, so this makes the two layers consistent.

## 4. Manual input test

A 20-second session driven by **real keyboard events through `InputManager`** (not autoplay), all four lanes:

- 78 notes scheduled, **78 judged, 0 missed inputs**
- Perfect 78 / Great 0 / Good 0 / Miss 0
- Per lane: 27 / 21 / 24 / 22
- Mean |timing delta| 41 ms, max 58 ms (Medium window 280 ms, Perfect 80 ms)
- 16 misses appear **after** the scripted window ends, which drains health to 0 about 5 s later

The events were synthetic DOM `KeyboardEvent`s (`isTrusted=false`) — an attempt to send OS-level keys through the browser panel did not register, so I am not claiming physical-key or physical-device testing.

## 5. Performance (390×844, 60 Hz panel, DPR 1)

| Scene | Avg frame | p95 | FPS | Draw calls | Triangles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Idle (menu) | 16.622 ms | 16.8 | 60.16 | 126 | 13,026 |
| Active gameplay | 16.549 ms | 16.8 | 60.43 | 178 | 15,638 |
| Heavy effects (climax + 4 bursts + flare) | 16.629 ms | 16.8 | 60.14 | 179 | 15,752 |

Heap 48.9 MB during play, 54.0 MB at the menu; 24 shader programs; no quality reduction was needed or applied.

**Console:** zero WebGL warnings, zero broken shader programs (the `patch` fix holds), and no runtime errors. The only noise is `ERR_FILE_NOT_FOUND` on revoked `blob:` audio URLs when a track is restarted mid-playback — the media element briefly still references the old object URL. It's cosmetic and pre-existing; I left the audio engine alone.

## 6. Deliverables

- Screenshots: `screenshots/stage35-360x800.png`, `stage35-390x844.png`, `stage35-430x932.png`, `stage35-1280x720.png`, `stage35-1920x1080.png` (exact framebuffer captures at each target), plus `stage35-portrait-hud.png` with the live HUD.
- Recording: `screenshots/stage35-gameplay.mp4` — 27.4 s, 420×800, H.264 + AAC, real Clementi audio muxed from the recorded song position.
- Data: `qa-artifacts/stage35-validation.json`, `qa-artifacts/stage35-framing.json`.
- Build: `npm run build` exit 0 — 95 modules, JS 1,074.37 kB (294.41 kB gzip), CSS 64.71 kB.

**Files changed:** `src/world/StageFraming.js` (new), `src/config.js`, `src/core/Game.js`, `src/core/CameraRig.js`, `src/world/DancePads.js`, `src/objects/NoteManager.js`, `src/game/Gameplay.js`, `src/ui/UIManager.js`, `src/styles/main.css`, `qa/stage35-framing-check.mjs` (new).

## 7. Remaining limitations

The honest one: **on portrait the four pads fit but are small** — they occupy roughly 6% of frame height and sit just below the vertical centre. That is geometry, not a bug: lanes span ±3 units and the camera cannot pull back past the tunnel's near wall, so "all four visible" and "pads large at the bottom" cannot both hold without either compressing lane spacing on portrait (changes gameplay geometry) or extending the floor/walls to allow a camera outside the tunnel. I kept the constraints you set and left that call to you.

Also: 3D screenshots are framebuffer captures (no HUD) because the preview panel crops DOM screenshots to ~431 px wide; touch/key input was synthetic; the background tab freezes during chunked audio analysis, which stalls the loading bar.

Tuy nhiên, có một vấn đề mình muốn giải quyết trước khi tuyên bố NEON PULSE hoàn thiện: bốn dance pads tuy đã nằm trong màn hình điện thoại nhưng vẫn quá nhỏ và nằm quá cao.

Theo mình, đây là vấn đề đáng ưu tiên hơn việc bổ sung thêm hiệu ứng.

### Phương án mình chọn

Responsive Mobile Gameplay Layout

Giữ nguyên thế giới 3D và chỉ thay đổi cách bố trí gameplay trên mobile:

- Desktop: giữ nguyên camera, tunnel và bốn pads 3D.
- Mobile: giữ tunnel làm nền, tăng diện tích hiển thị pads.
- Pads vẫn có hình thang, màu neon và phối cảnh.
- Có thể sử dụng một lớp tương tác 2D phối hợp với vị trí 3D, nhưng không được tạo cảm giác hai bộ pads chồng lên nhau.
- Dancer panel thu gọn hợp lý để dành không gian cho gameplay.

Như vậy không cần phá vỡ thiết kế Stage 1.

## 3. Prompt tiếp theo cho OpenCode

Mình muốn giao một vòng cuối tập trung vào mobile UX, không yêu cầu viết lại engine.

Writing

# NEON PULSE – STAGE 3.6: MOBILE GAMEPLAY EXPERIENCE

Stage 3.5 is technically functional.

However, the four dance pads are still too small and positioned too close to the vertical center on portrait screens.

The objective is to improve actual mobile gameplay usability while preserving the established visual identity.

IMPORTANT: Do not rewrite the gameplay engine. Do not change the four-lane chart architecture. Do not change scoring, timing windows or audio synchronization. Do not redesign desktop mode.

## 1\. DESKTOP

Desktop must remain visually identical to the current Stage 3.5 composition.

Preserve:

- Camera FOV.
- Tunnel geometry.
- Pad positions.
- Pad proportions.
- Note positions.
- Dancer panel.
- HUD layout.

## 2\. MOBILE GAMEPLAY COMPOSITION

Create a responsive portrait-specific composition.

Requirements:

- All four pads must be large enough to recognize and interact with comfortably.
- Move the gameplay interaction area toward the lower portion of the screen.
- Preserve the centered vanishing point.
- Preserve tunnel depth and perspective.
- Avoid excessively wide camera FOV.
- Do not stretch the 3D scene.

Explore a hybrid presentation if necessary:

- Keep the existing 3D pads and tunnel as the visual environment.
- Use a perspective-aligned 2D interaction overlay only if it improves mobile usability.
- Never show two competing sets of pads.

## 3\. PAD SIZE AND TOUCH TARGETS

Prioritize comfortable finger interaction.

Requirements:

- Four equally sized interaction zones.
- Clear separation between adjacent zones.
- Adequate spacing from screen edges.
- No accidental browser scrolling.
- No touch-zone overlap.
- Each touch zone maps to exactly one lane.

## 4\. DANCER PANEL

On portrait screens:

- Reduce the dancer panel footprint if needed.
- Keep the full white silhouette recognizable.
- Avoid allowing the panel to compete with the four gameplay pads.

Desktop dancer composition must remain unchanged.

## 5\. REAL DEVICE VALIDATION

Do not claim physical mobile testing unless an actual device is used.

At minimum, provide:

- 360×800
- 390×844
- 430×932

For each viewport, report:

- Visible pad width and height in pixels.
- Touch-zone dimensions.
- Distance from screen edges.
- Whether all four touch zones are independently usable.

## 6\. REGRESSION TESTS

Verify:

- Four-lane mapping.
- Keyboard gameplay.
- Touch gameplay.
- Multi-touch.
- Perfect/Great/Good/Miss.
- Combo and score.
- Pause/resume.
- Seek/restart.
- Desktop composition unchanged.
- No WebGL errors.

## 7\. DELIVERABLES

Provide:

- Desktop screenshot.
- Three portrait screenshots.
- Pad dimensions in pixels.
- Touch-zone dimensions.
- Short gameplay video.
- Build result.
- Remaining limitations.

Do not optimize the mobile layout at the expense of desktop quality.

Picking this up from Stage 3.5. My plan: replace the "wide FOV + push pads deep" approach with a portrait-only *uniform scale + proximity* composition, so pads keep their exact proportions but land lower and much larger. Let me read the current layout code first.

The Stage 3.5 approach was geometrically capped: pads were 71 px wide but only 50 px tall and sat mid-frame. I'll replace it with a portrait-only *uniform row scale* (pads keep exact proportions, no stretching), FOV back to 70°, and a lower placement; the authored desktop path is untouched.
