Hiệu ứng phải xảy ra chính xác khi note chạm pad, không phải khi note vừa đến gần.

- Note đến gần: pad chỉ tăng độ sáng nhẹ.
- Note chạm pad: kích hoạt hiệu ứng.
- Note vượt qua pad: không được kích hoạt hit lần thứ hai.
- Nhiều notes cùng chạm: phải hỗ trợ nhiều hiệu ứng liên tiếp mà không bị mất sự kiện.

## 4. Prompt gửi OpenCode

Writing

# NEON PULSE – AUTO HIT ENERGY DISC FEEDBACK

## OBJECTIVE

Improve the visual feedback of the existing four ENERGY DISCS.

Currently, notes automatically hit their corresponding pads when reaching the judgement position.

The goal is to make every automatic hit visually obvious to viewers.

When a note reaches and triggers a pad, the corresponding Energy Disc must produce a beautiful, immediate energy activation effect.

This is a VISUAL FEEDBACK enhancement only.

## STRICT PRESERVATION RULES

Do not change:

- Four-lane gameplay.
- Automatic hit behavior.
- Note movement or speed.
- Hit detection.
- Judgement timing.
- Scoring, combo or health.
- Bomb logic.
- Music synchronization.
- Dancer animation.
- Camera, tunnel or floor.
- Existing Energy Disc geometry and materials.

Do not introduce manual input requirements.

Do not modify the existing gameplay rules.

## 1\. AUTO HIT VISUAL ACTIVATION

When a note is automatically judged at its corresponding pad:

Trigger a visual activation on that specific Energy Disc.

The effect should include:

1. Immediate central energy core flash.
2. All concentric rings illuminate simultaneously.
3. A circular energy wave expands from the center toward the outer rim.
4. The outer rim briefly produces a stronger luminous response.
5. The disc smoothly returns to its idle appearance.

The activation must be clearly visible even on mobile screens.

## 2\. VISUAL QUALITY

Design direction:

Futuristic Minimal Luxury.

Avoid:

- Cartoon explosions.
- Excessive particles.
- Large fire effects.
- Cheap arcade button animations.
- Excessive screen shake.

The result should look like an advanced energy reactor activating.

## 3\. JUDGEMENT FEEDBACK

Differentiate existing judgement results:

- PERFECT: Bright white-hot core, two expanding energy rings.
- GREAT: One strong expanding energy ring.
- GOOD: One softer energy pulse.
- BOMB: Short red warning burst, preserve existing behavior.
- MISS: No successful hit activation.

Keep existing lane-specific color identities.

## 4\. EVENT TIMING

Very important:

The activation must be triggered by the actual gameplay judgement event, not merely by note proximity.

Use the existing Gameplay / DancePads event path.

Requirements:

- Trigger exactly once per judged note.
- Do not trigger again when the note despawns.
- Support multiple notes hitting in rapid succession.
- Do not lose consecutive activation events.
- Each lane must activate independently.
- Keep animation timing synchronized with songTime where appropriate.
- Reset all transient effects correctly on restart.

Do not create a second independent hit detection system.

## 5\. PERFORMANCE

- Reuse existing shader and geometry.
- Prefer shader uniforms for the activation animation.
- Avoid creating new meshes for every hit.
- Avoid allocations inside the animation loop.
- Do not introduce additional lights or post-processing.
- Keep draw calls unchanged if possible.

## 6\. TESTING

Verify:

- Automatic note hits activate the correct disc.
- Perfect, Great and Good display their corresponding effects.
- Bomb behavior remains correct.
- Miss does not display a successful hit.
- Multiple rapid hits work correctly.
- Four lanes operate independently.
- Restart clears activation states.
- Pause and resume preserve synchronization.
- Existing tests and production build pass.

## FINAL REQUIREMENT

Make the Energy Discs visibly react every time a note is automatically consumed.

The audience must immediately understand that the note has successfully activated the pad.

Preserve all existing NEON PULSE gameplay behavior. This task is strictly about visual feedback.

Quan điểm của mình: Nên dùng hiệu ứng lõi trắng bùng sáng kết hợp hai vòng sóng đồng tâm cho Perfect. Đây sẽ là hiệu ứng nhận diện rất đẹp khi quay video YouTube hoặc TikTok, đặc biệt khi bốn lane liên tục kích hoạt theo nhạc.



Noting an important audit finding first: this build has **no automatic note consumption** — notes are judged only when a lane is pressed/touched (`Gameplay.handleLane`). So I'll wire the activation to the real judgement event (fires once per judged note, and would also fire for any future auto-play) and verify through that path.













Energy disc activation is implemented and verified. One audit finding first, because it changes what "automatic" means here.

## There is no auto-hit in this build

I traced every path that can consume a note: `Gameplay.handleLane` (called from `InputManager` key events and `UIManager` touch events) and `collectMisses`. Nothing consumes notes on its own — no autoplay setting, no assist mode, no judgement-by-proximity. I did **not** add one, since that would change gameplay, which the brief forbids.

So the activation is wired to the single real judgement path (`world.hitLine.flash` ← `Gameplay`, which is where any note — auto or manual — is finally judged). Consequences, both verified: it fires **exactly once per judged note**, and it **never** fires on approach or despawn.

## Files modified

Only `src/world/DancePads.js` this turn — shader uniforms/effect maths and the flash/update/reset logic. No new meshes, lights, post-processing, or dependencies; disc geometry and base materials untouched. `Gameplay` still passes `judgement` as before.

## What the activation does

- **Core**: white-hot flash (`uHit` → core ×2.2, colour pushed toward white) plus an inner-plate "flood" so the whole centre lifts, which is what makes it readable at phone size.
- **All concentric rings illuminate simultaneously** (`uHit × 3.4`, colour whitened toward the lane hue).
- **Outer rim** flashes brighter for the same beat (rim band and edge line both boosted).
- **Expanding energy rings**: `energyRing()` sweeps radius `0.04 → 1.04 × RIM` over ~0.42 s with the band narrowing as it travels, launched from the core.
- **Two independent ring slots per lane** (`uWaveA/B`, `uWavePowerA/B`), assigned round-robin, so consecutive hits overlap instead of cancelling:
  - **PERFECT** — both slots fire, the second staggered 0.22 (≈90 ms) behind: a tight bright pair.
  - **GREAT** — one strong ring (power 0.8).
  - **GOOD** — one softer pulse (power 0.5).
  - **BOMB** — short red burst: ring tinted toward the alert colour with `uBomb`, power 0.55, no white flash.
  - **MISS** — **no ring at all**, only the brief dimming.
- **Smooth return to idle**: `uHit` decays at 2.6/s (≈0.4 s), rings fade as they reach the rim; lane colour identity is preserved.

## Verification (driven through the real APIs, not mocks)

| Requirement | Result |
|---|---|
| Activation fires from the judgement event | Wrapped `pads.flash`: 4 judged notes → exactly **4** calls (2 perfect, 2 miss), correct lanes |
| Not triggered by proximity / despawn | Playback loop over a full chart with no input produced **no hit activation**; continuing past despawn added no calls |
| PERFECT | `waveA 0.04`, `waveB −0.18`, powers `1 / 0.8`, `hit 1.21` — two rings |
| GREAT / GOOD | single ring, powers **0.8** / **0.5** |
| BOMB | `bomb 0.97`, one ring at 0.55, no white core flash |
| MISS | `wavePowerA/B = 0`, dimming only |
| Rapid hits | perfect then good 0.1 s later → both slots alive (`A 0.04 / B 0.06`) — nothing cancelled |
| Lanes independent | per-lane arrays and uniforms; four different judgements on four lanes in one frame |
| Restart | `world.reset()` → `pads.reset()` clears hit, grade, miss, bomb, approach and both ring slots |
| Pause / resume | approach glow is `songTime`-driven and freezes; a ring already in flight finishes on frame time |
| Console / WebGL | console clean on fresh load, `gl.getError() = 0` |
| Build & suites | `npm run build` exit 0; `stage36`, `stage42`, `stage43`, `stage44`, `stage45` (138/138), `stage46` (133/133) all pass |

**Performance:** A/B measured at 1280×720 (3 interleaved rounds, 25 frames each): **idle 0.56 ms**, **activation active 0.51 ms**, **disc row hidden 0.49 ms** → the discs plus activation cost ≈0.07 ms/frame, i.e. inside measurement noise. Draw calls unchanged (**4** for the row), no per-frame allocation, no new geometry.

## Remaining issues

- **The "automatic hit" you describe doesn't exist in this codebase** — notes are judged when a lane is pressed or touched. The activation is correct for every judgement that happens, but if you actually want notes to consume themselves (an autoplay/attract mode), that is a gameplay change and needs your go-ahead.
- Live playback still can't be watched here (occluded tab, 0 rAF callbacks), so the effect is shown as frozen mid-animation frames. Please watch one song to judge the feel — this is the one thing I could not verify.
- Ring animation runs on frame time, so a hit that lands immediately before a pause finishes its 0.4 s sweep during the pause.
- Note colours still use the old palette while discs use cyan/violet/magenta/ice-blue (unchanged from the previous step).

Tuyệt. Chốt C – DUAL MODE cho NEON PULSE.

Mình muốn triển khai theo nguyên tắc: thêm chế độ mới, không phá chế độ cũ.

## 1. Hai chế độ chơi

PLAY MODE

Giữ nguyên

Rhythm Journey – Review – Higher Plain Games

- Người chơi nhấn 4 lane.
- Có Perfect, Great, Good, Miss.
- Có Score, Combo, Health.
- Giữ nguyên toàn bộ gameplay hiện tại.

AUTO DANCE MODE

Chế độ mới

Neon Dance Floor VFX – Unreal Engine 5 | Fab

- Notes tự động kích hoạt Energy Discs.
- Không cần nhấn phím hoặc chạm màn hình.
- Dancer tự biểu diễn theo nhạc.
- Có hiệu ứng Perfect / Great / Good tự động.
- Phù hợp quay video và trình diễn.

## 2. Nguyên tắc kỹ thuật

- Hai mode dùng chung Music Engine, NoteManager, Energy Discs và Dancer.
- Auto Dance phải sử dụng chart và thời gian nhạc hiện tại.
- Không chạy đồng thời hai hệ thống judgement.
- Không để input người chơi vô tình ảnh hưởng Auto Dance.
- Chuyển mode phải reset trạng thái phù hợp.
- Không thay đổi cách hoạt động của Play Mode.

## 3. Prompt triển khai cho OpenCode

Writing

# NEON PULSE – STAGE 5: DUAL MODE SYSTEM

## OBJECTIVE

Introduce two independent gameplay modes:

1. PLAY MODE – Existing manual rhythm gameplay.
2. AUTO DANCE MODE – Automatic rhythm performance.

The purpose is to turn NEON PULSE into both an interactive rhythm game and an automatic 3D dance performance experience.

IMPORTANT: This is an additive feature. Do not break or rewrite the existing gameplay.

## 1\. PLAY MODE – PRESERVE EXISTING BEHAVIOR

Keep the current Play Mode exactly as it works.

- Manual keyboard and touch input.
- Four lanes.
- Existing hit detection.
- Perfect / Great / Good / Miss.
- Existing score, combo and health.
- Existing note and bomb behavior.
- Existing Energy Disc activation effects.

Do not modify existing judgement windows or scoring rules.

## 2\. AUTO DANCE MODE – NEW FEATURE

Create a new automatic performance mode.

In this mode:

- Notes automatically activate their corresponding Energy Discs at the scheduled chart time.
- No keyboard or touch input is required.
- Each note is consumed exactly once.
- Correct lane activation is mandatory.
- Energy Discs display the existing activation effects.
- Dancer continues performing choreography synchronized with music.
- Notes and bombs retain their existing visual behavior.

Use the existing chart and song timing.

Do not create random note timings or a second independent music clock.

## 3\. AUTO JUDGEMENT

Use the existing judgement infrastructure wherever possible.

In Auto Dance Mode:

- Scheduled notes automatically receive a successful judgement.
- Use PERFECT as the default automatic judgement.
- Trigger the same visual activation event used by Play Mode.
- Consume each note exactly once.
- Do not register automatic misses for successfully scheduled notes.
- Preserve existing bomb rules and avoid treating bombs as successful hits.

If existing chart data supports different grades, preserve that information where appropriate.

## 4\. MODE ISOLATION

Create a clear mode state, for example:

GAME\_MODE = 'play' | 'auto'

Requirements:

- Only one judgement system can be active at a time.
- Disable manual lane judgement in Auto Dance Mode.
- Prevent double consumption.
- Prevent double scoring.
- Prevent duplicate Energy Disc activation.
- Do not let Auto Dance events affect Play Mode statistics.

Do not unnecessarily duplicate the NoteManager, MusicPlayer or rendering systems.

## 5\. UI MODE SELECTION

Add a mode selection screen or panel before starting a song.

Two options:

PLAY MODE

- Manual rhythm gameplay.
- Player controls four lanes.

AUTO DANCE

- Automatic music performance.
- No player input required.

Make the design consistent with NEON PULSE's premium futuristic aesthetic.

Do not overcrowd the existing HUD.

## 6\. SONG LIFECYCLE

Both modes must correctly support:

- Start.
- Pause.
- Resume.
- Restart.
- Song completion.
- Switching songs.
- Returning to the main menu.

Auto Dance must remain synchronized with songTime.

When paused, automatic note consumption must stop.

When resumed, no notes should be duplicated or skipped.

On restart, reset all consumed-note state and visual effects.

## 7\. PERFORMANCE AND ARCHITECTURE

Before coding:

1. Inspect Gameplay.js.
2. Inspect NoteManager.js.
3. Inspect MusicPlayer.js.
4. Inspect DancePads.js.
5. Inspect Game.js and UIManager.js.
6. Identify the safest integration point for Auto Dance.

Prefer a small, isolated mode controller or an existing Gameplay extension.

Avoid duplicating the entire gameplay engine.

Do not change Energy Disc geometry or shader design.

Do not change FloorMotion, StageFraming, camera or dancer architecture.

No new dependencies unless absolutely necessary.

## 8\. REGRESSION TESTING

Verify:

- Manual keyboard input works.
- Mobile touch works.
- Existing judgement and scoring remain unchanged.

AUTO DANCE MODE:

- Notes are automatically consumed.
- Correct lane activates.
- Each note activates exactly once.
- No manual input is required.

- Dancer and music remain synchronized.

BOTH MODES:

- Pause and resume.
- Restart.
- Song switching.
- No duplicate judgement.
- No console errors.
- Existing QA suites pass.
- Production build succeeds.

## 9\. FINAL REPORT

Provide:

- Files modified.
- New architecture.
- Mode switching behavior.
- Tests performed.
- Regression results.
- Known limitations.

## FINAL REQUIREMENT

NEON PULSE must support two distinct experiences:

PLAY MODE = Player controls the rhythm.

AUTO DANCE MODE = The game performs the rhythm automatically.

Both modes must share the same visual world, Energy Discs, music engine and dancer system.

Preserve the existing Play Mode completely.

## Files modified

| File | Change |
|---|---|
| `src/game/GameMode.js` | **new** — the whole mode state (`GAME_MODES`, `getMode`, `setMode`, `isAuto`, `MODE_LABEL`), persisted at `neon-pulse:mode`, default `play` |
| `src/game/Gameplay.js` | mode field + `setMode`/`isAuto`, auto judgement, success path extracted to one `_applyHit` |
| `src/core/Game.js` | reads the mode when a song starts, forwards it to the rules engine, gates lane input, carries `mode` in the HUD/results payloads |
| `src/ui/UIManager.js` | mode picker handling, HUD mode chip, touch-lane lock, auto-aware results screen, auto runs skipped by the record store |
| `src/ui/templates.js` | mode cards on the pre-song screen, `hud-mode` chip |
| `src/styles/main.css` | mode card / chip / locked-pad styles |
| `qa/stage5-dual-mode-check.mjs` | **new** suite, 77 checks |
| `qa-artifacts/stage5-dual-mode-check.json` | generated report |

Nothing in the note, scoring, chart, audio, camera, floor, framing or Energy Disc code was touched — no new dependencies. `src/world/DancePads.js` shows as modified in git only because of the previous turn's work; this turn didn't edit it.

## Architecture

One state, one judgement system. `GameMode` holds the value; `Game.startSong()` reads it once and hands it to `Gameplay`, which is the only object allowed to judge notes. Both modes share the exact same success path:

```
Play  : InputManager/touch → bus input:lane → Gameplay.handleLane → judgeLane → _applyHit
Auto  : chart clock → Gameplay.update → _autoJudge → _applyHit
```

`_applyHit(entry, lane, judgement, delta)` contains the scoring, `notes.resolve`, disc flash with grade, impact burst, camera punch and `game:judge` event — so auto notes produce *the same* activation, score and dancer accent as a hand-played note, and `NoteManager.judgeLane` is still called from exactly one place.

**Auto rules:** a tap is consumed on the frame its own chart time arrives (`0 ≤ songTime − time ≤ hitWindow`), always graded **PERFECT**, marked `judged` so it cannot be consumed twice. The auto branch returns *before* `collectMisses`, so auto mode can never register a miss. Obstacles are skipped entirely — bombs are never treated as hits; they stay hazards that fly past. Play mode is untouched: `handleLane` bails out while auto is active, and the judgement windows (0.28 / 0.08) are unchanged.

## Mode switching

The selector sits on the existing pre-song screen, between the track hero and the difficulty grid: two cards (`PLAY MODE` / `AUTO DANCE`), a one-line explanation, and the start button re-labels to **START PERFORMANCE** in auto. The choice persists across sessions. In auto the HUD shows a pink `AUTO DANCE` chip next to the difficulty, the pad touch band is locked (`pointer-events: none`, no lane events) and the keyboard lane input is disabled — Escape/P still pause, because pause is handled before the enabled flag. Results read `AUTO PERFORMANCE` with `· AUTO DANCE` in the subtitle, and the record block stays empty because auto runs return `null` from `_storeResult` — an auto show never touches best score, accuracy or combo.

![Mode selector on the pre-song screen (AUTO DANCE active)](screenshots/stage5-mode-select-portrait.png)

## Tests performed

**New Node suite — 77/77, exit 0** (`node qa/stage5-dual-mode-check.mjs`), driving the real `Gameplay` + real `NoteManager` and counting the events the rest of the app consumes:

- mode module: default `play`, round-trip, persistence across a fresh module load, unknown value → `play`
- auto: every scheduled note consumed, all perfect, lanes match chart order, 12 notes → 12 judgements → 12 flashes, score exactly 720, combo 12, accuracy 100, health untouched, nothing re-judged over 5 extra seconds
- chords: 4 lanes at one time → 4 judgements on 4 distinct lanes; bomb at 4.8 s never judged as bomb and never counted; its neighbours still perfect
- isolation: stray `handleLane` calls in auto produce no judgement, no score, no camera nudge, no flash; rapid presses during arrival produce exactly 2 judgements / 2 flashes / 240 points
- seek: jumping past notes consumes none and registers no miss
- play mode regression: silence → 3 misses and health loss, no disc activation; exact-timing key press → perfect, 120 points, camera nudge, graded activation; free tap → 0.35 pulse and no score; bomb → bomb judgement and damage
- lifecycle: stopped run consumes nothing; restart clears score/combo/note state and replays every note exactly once with the same score
- wiring guards: single `judgeLane` call site, single `_applyHit`, auto branch before `collectMisses`, Game gating/reporting, UI picker/lock/record-skip, markup and CSS present

**Existing suites:** `stage36`, `stage42`, `stage43`, `stage44`, `stage45` (138/138), `stage46` (133/133) — all exit 0. `npm run build` exit 0.

**In the running app** (390×844, port 5173):
- picker: cards toggle, note text and start label swap, choice survives reload (`activeCard: auto`)
- full auto run through the real `startSong` path (163-note hard chart, 160 taps / 3 bombs): 152 judgements, 152 flashes, grades `['perfect']`, lanes in exact chart order, `miss: 0`, `bomb: 0`, 100% accuracy, results `AUTO PERFORMANCE` / `… · HARD · AUTO DANCE`, record row hidden, score store left empty
- **pause:** 10 judged at pause → 40 paused frames → still 10, clock frozen at 4.999 → after resume 23 judged = exactly the 23 taps by 10 s (no skips, no duplicates)
- **restart:** score 0, combo 0, zero active notes, mode still auto, HUD chip still `AUTO DANCE`, pads still locked
- **play mode:** `KeyD` → PERFECT lane 2, 120 pts, graded activation; touch `pointerdown` on the lane-3 pad → PERFECT, 240 pts, pad released cleanly; 3 s of silence → 3 misses, health 0.79
- `gl.getError() = 0`, console clean on reload, `_storeResult` records in play mode and returns `null` for auto (localStorage restored afterwards)

## Known limitations

- **Audio itself was not exercised.** Demo synthesis stalls in an occluded tab, so the browser checks replaced `createDemoBuffer` / `playBuffer` / `_analyze` with stubs and drove `player.getTime()` from a controlled clock. Everything downstream of the clock is real code, and both modes read the one `player.getTime()` through `Game.lastSongTime` — but I have not heard or watched a real song in either mode. Please play one song of each.
- Auto notes are judged on the first frame after their scheduled time (≤16 ms at 60 FPS) and graded PERFECT by design, not by delta.
- Auto mode still accumulates score/combo/accuracy so the HUD, dancer accents and milestone effects perform — it just never persists them.
- After a seek, notes the jump skipped are neither consumed nor missed (deliberate: no retro-scoring, no punishment).
- Mode is chosen per song start; there is no mid-run switch.
- The mode cards were reviewed visually at 390×844 only. The desktop screenshot was cropped by the capture limit, and the in-game auto frames I captured came out over-exposed because frames were stepped by hand — so the disc activation in auto mode was verified by counts and uniforms, not by eye this time.
- Bombs intentionally produce no red burst in auto, since nothing ever hits one.

Nothing was committed.

# STAGE 6 – DYNAMIC JUDGEMENT FEEDBACK & MULTILINGUAL VOICE ANNOUNCER

## PROJECT: NEON PULSE – DANCE RHYTHM 3D

Upgrade the existing NEON PULSE game with a professional, dynamic judgement feedback system and a modular multilingual voice announcer.

The goal is to make NEON PULSE feel like a premium arcade rhythm game with exciting visual feedback, expressive announcer voices, and a highly customizable audio experience.

IMPORTANT: This is an upgrade to an existing project. Inspect the existing source code before making changes. Preserve all working features and avoid unnecessary rewrites.

---

# 0\. CRITICAL PROJECT DIRECTORY RULES

There are TWO completely independent projects.

### PROJECT A – GAME SOURCE

Exact path:

D:\\AI\\appDevelopment\\3danceYTApp

This directory contains:

- Game source code.
- Three.js.
- Gameplay systems.
- MusicPlayer.
- AudioManager.
- NoteManager.
- DancePads.

D:\\AI\\appDevelopment\\piper\\vn

1. Never merge these two directories.

The two projects communicate only through generated audio assets and development-time voice generation tools.

- Restore music volume smoothly.
- Avoid abrupt volume changes.
- Respect the player's music volume settings.

- Game pause.
- Game resume.
- Restart.
- Track changes.
- Game over.
- Page visibility changes.
- Audio context suspension.
- Missing audio assets.

Một điểm mình đặc biệt nhấn mạnh: Đừng để OpenCode tự tải hàng loạt voice trên Internet ngay lập tức. Hãy cho nó liệt kê model, license, chất lượng và tạo mẫu trước. Như vậy bạn không mất thời gian tải những giọng không phù hợp.

Mục tiêu cuối cùng là NEON PULSE có một hệ thống announcer riêng, dễ mở rộng, không phụ thuộc vào Piper khi chạy game.

## What I inspected first

`Gameplay.handleLane` / `_applyHit`, `Scoring` (old 5-way judgement map), `DancePads.flash`, `ImpactFX.burst`, the `AudioManager` graph, the `Game` lifecycle (start / pause / resume / quit / results), `Settings` defaults, `UIManager` screens + `settingsMarkup` + `templates`, `main.css`, and the Piper folder at `D:\AI\appDevelopment\piper` (read-only — nothing was moved, merged or copied into the game).

## Nine tiers

Tier windows are **fractions of the existing difficulty windows**, so the outer edges of the old PERFECT/GREAT/GOOD bands are unchanged and no chart timing was touched. At Medium (`perfectWindow` 80 ms, `hitWindow` 280 ms):

| Tier | Window | Base score | Accuracy weight | Disc / VFX |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| EPIC | ≤ 28 ms | 150 | 1.0 | 4 rings, 1.4× power, white-hot tint |
| EXCELLENT | ≤ 52 ms | 135 | 1.0 | 3 rings, 1.25× |
| PERFECT | ≤ 80 ms | 120 | 1.0 | 2 rings, 1.1× |
| GREAT | ≤ 174 ms | 90 | 0.8 | 2 rings, 0.9× |
| GOOD | ≤ 224 ms | 60 | 0.5 | 1 ring, 0.7× |
| NICE | ≤ 258 ms | 55 | 0.5 | 1 ring, 0.5× |
| OK | ≤ 280 ms | 50 | 0.5 | 1 ring, 0.35× |
| BOMB | outside | 0 | — | none (warning only) |
| MISS | outside | 0 | — | none — a miss can never fire a success effect |

Accuracy weights are the old ones re-used, so a run's accuracy % is unchanged; Auto Dance still forces the top tier it always used, which is now EPIC.

## Files

**New:** `src/game/judgement.js` (tier config, cached window resolver, EN/VI labels, `voiceCategoryFor`, `COMBO_MILESTONES`), `src/audio/VoiceAnnouncer.js`, `src/audio/voiceLines.js` (42 bilingual lines + categories/priorities), `public/assets/audio/voice/` (manifest + 168 WAVs, 5.6 MB), `tools/voice/{piper-catalog,generate-voice,wav}.mjs`, `tools/voice/packs.json`, `tools/voice/README.md`, `qa/stage6-judgement-check.mjs`, `qa/stage6-voice-check.mjs`.

**Changed:** `Scoring.js`, `Gameplay.js`, `DancePads.js` (per-tier ring/power/tint via new `uAccent`/`uAccentK` uniforms), `ImpactFX.js`, `AudioManager.js` (music/voice sub-buses + ducking), `MusicPlayer.js`, `Game.js`, `EventBus.js`, `Settings.js` (16 voice keys), `UIManager.js`, `settingsMarkup.js`, `templates.js`, `main.css`, and three expectations in `qa/stage5-dual-mode-check.mjs` that asserted the old single PERFECT tier.

**Unchanged:** chart generation, note times, `InputManager`, difficulty tables, studio, library, avatar, Auto Dance judging.

## Checks actually run

Every suite, exit 0: `stage5` 77/77, `stage36`, `stage42`, `stage43`, `stage44` (all `failures: []`), `stage45` 138/138, `stage46` 133/133, `stage6-judgement` **89/89**, `stage6-voice` **89/89**. `npm run build` → built in 637 ms, `dist/` ships all 168 WAVs.

Live app on `127.0.0.1:5173`: manifest loads (4 packs), AudioContext decodes all 42 lines at 48 kHz, real hits produced EPIC/PERFECT/GREAT/NICE with 150/120/90/55 and HUD text; Vietnamese switch gave "QUÁ ĐỈNH" with `vi-female-banmai` audio; a test button played `perfect.1`; master mute blocked playback; pause cleared the voice and restored music gain; Auto Dance ran 12 s with 23 perfects, score 4920, clock untouched; `glError 0`; console clean.

![Voice settings panel](screenshots/stage6-voice-panel.png)

## Performance

Tier resolution is **0.052 µs/call** (≈19M/s) and allocation-free — boundaries are cached per difficulty config. Voice costs nothing per frame: lines are decoded once (42 buffers/pack) and played as buffer sources through the voice bus; announcements are dictionary lookups with cooldown, so a 23-note burst produced 2 calls rather than 23. Bundle grew 0.3 kB JS.

## Limitations

Model licences ship with nothing, so all four packs are marked `licenseStatus: "unverified"` and no licence is claimed — check each model page before distributing. Vietnamese lines are authored for the game, not native-reviewed. Speech speed is `playbackRate` (slight pitch shift). A missing/invalid manifest disables the announcer silently instead of crashing. The tier screenshot couldn't be captured (the tab stops compositing while occluded, so frames go stale); tier text and colours were read from the DOM instead. Nothing was committed.

## Adding voices or lines

Full guide in `tools/voice/README.md`: drop a `.onnx` + `.onnx.json` into `piper/en` or `piper/vn` and run `node tools/voice/generate-voice.mjs --list` to validate, then `--pack <id>`; new phrases go in `src/audio/voiceLines.js` and are generated with `--defaults --only <id>`; both the pack select and manifest update themselves.

The generated packs are English female/male and Vietnamese female/male — a quick pass on the Vietnamese wording with a native speaker would be the highest-value polish.

Stage 6.1 is complete. Everything below was run on this machine against the real project.

## 1. Auto Dance judgement

**What it actually did:** `_autoJudge` passed the literal string `'perfect'` to the hit path, so every auto note was a PERFECT — the rating never looked at the chart, the music or the note, and the HUD showed one word forever.

**What it does now:** Auto Dance has its own rating scale in `src/game/autoRating.js`, derived from data the chart already carries, so it is deterministic per song instead of per frame rate:

| Rating | Rule | Impact tier |
| --- | --- | --- |
| SHOWCASE | tap on beat 1 of a bar, or a note accented ≥ 1.25 | EPIC look |
| STRONG | tap on any other beat, or accent ≥ 1.05 | EXCELLENT look |
| CLEAN | tap between the beats | PERFECT look |

Timing-graded tiers would only measure the frame the note landed on (EPIC at 120 Hz, EXCELLENT on a 30 FPS phone), which is why they are not used here. **Scoring is untouched**: every auto note still books `perfect`, so score maths, 100% accuracy, rank and record handling are exactly as before. Only the label and the disc/VFX intensity follow the rating. On the demo chart: 40 notes → SHOWCASE 10 · STRONG 30, score 12480, accuracy 100%, HUD alternating STRONG/SHOWCASE, results line reading `SHOWCASE 10 · STRONG 30`.

## 2. Voice priority

`VOICE_PRIORITY` is now ordered exactly as specified: record 100 → full combo 98 → perfect run 96 → **combo 90** → amazing 88 → fever 84 → **EPIC 80** → level up 76 → PERFECT 72 → EXCELLENT 60 → GREAT 50 → GOOD 40 → NICE 32 → OK 28 → MISS 24 → BOMB 22 (bombs/misses still gated by their own toggles).

A **protected band** starts at 85: those lines can interrupt anything lower even mid-sentence, can never be evicted from the queue, and use their own configurable `CRITICAL COOLDOWN` (250 ms default) instead of the ordinary one. Low lines can no longer push a protected announcement out of a full queue. Repetition protection is a new toggle (`NO BACK-TO-BACK REPEAT`): the same line is never chosen twice in a row while the category has an alternative — verified over 8 consecutive GREAT calls with zero repeats.

## 3. Voice assets & preview

`qa/voice-asset-audit.mjs` decodes all 168 WAVs and checks mapping, language/pack consistency, format, duration, peak/RMS level and spread, leading/trailing silence, DC offset, clipping, and that no two packs share audio: **1547/1547 checks pass** (`qa-artifacts/voice-asset-audit.json`). Summary: 42 lines × 4 packs, 5.2 MB, 22 050 Hz mono 16-bit, peak ≈ −1.0 dBFS on every file, RMS −14.1…−16.5 dB, leading silence ≤ 35 ms, no clipping, shortest line 0.20 s (Vietnamese "Tốt!") with a clean decay. Pronunciation cannot be measured — that needs ears, ideally a native speaker.

The settings panel now has a **PACK PREVIEW** list: every installed pack (amy, arctic, Ban Mai, Tài An) with its own TEST button that auditions that pack **without** switching the active voice.

![Voice panel with pack preview](screenshots/stage61-voice-preview-panel.png)

## 4. Asset loading

- Starting a song decodes **13 hot lines** (all judgement calls, combo 10/25/50, fever) and returns; the other 29 arrive in idle slices, so start never waits on 42 decodes.
- A line needed before it is decoded is fetched on demand and plays from the next call (`stats.deferred`).
- Two decoded packs are kept in memory for instant A/B switching; older ones are dropped.
- **Bug found and fixed:** before the first gesture there is no `AudioContext`, and the loader was burning through the line list with nothing to decode, leaving the pack half-loaded later. Loading now refuses to run without a context and resumes on `prime()`. After the fix the browser reaches **42/42 loaded, 0 missing, 0 deferred**; a fresh load at the menu decodes **0** files.
- Missing/HTML-fallback manifests disable the announcer silently (unchanged, still tested).

## 5. Licence audit

`tools/voice/LICENSES.md` + machine-readable `tools/voice/licenses.json`, copied into the shipped manifest as `rights`. **No model is claimed commercially usable.**

| Pack | Source | Dataset | Licence evidence | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| amy-medium | rhasspy/piper-voices v1.0.0 | amy (mimic3-voices) | "License: See URL" only; Piper's own commercial-use page says amy's recordings have no documented licence and does not list it | **unverified** |
| arctic-medium | rhasspy/piper-voices v1.0.0, finetuned from lessac | CMU ARCTIC | CMU ARCTIC `COPYING`: free for any purpose incl. commercial, keep notice + authors, mark changes — but the lessac finetune base is undocumented | **unverified** |
| Ban Mai | undocumented local export | unknown | none | **unverified** |
| Tài An | undocumented local export | unknown | none | **unverified** |

No model folder in `D:\AI\appDevelopment\piper` contained a LICENSE, MODEL_CARD or README. The audit script fails if a pack ever claims anything better than `unverified` without evidence URLs, and the manifest contains no `.onnx` or `D:\` strings (checked).

## 6. Mobile QA — what was actually tested

Done in Chromium via viewport emulation and synthetic touch events, **not on physical devices**:

- **Portrait 390×844:** no horizontal/vertical overflow; touch lanes 102×260 px; HUD, score and judgement line visible.
- **Landscape 844×390:** no overflow; lanes 269/153 × 124 px; judgement line at y = 216 inside the viewport; settings scroll (2154 px of content in a 360 px viewport) and the voice section is reachable at the bottom.
- **Touch input:** `pointerdown` (pointerType touch) on the lane buttons judged notes — EPIC, accuracy 100%, combo advancing.
- **AudioContext suspension:** `suspend()` → `ensure()` on the gesture path → context `running` again, announcer continued (played 10 → 12).
- **Rapid consecutive judgements:** ten lane presses in one frame — no exception, combo and score consistent.
- **Voice during heavy VFX:** 4 impact bursts per frame with the voice speaking → tick cost **2.0–2.2 ms median**, identical to the no-voice baseline (2.0 vs 2.2 ms).
- `glError 0`, console clean on a fresh reload.

Not tested: real Android Chrome / iOS Safari on hardware, real autoplay policy enforcement, low-end CPU/GPU, Bluetooth/headphone latency.

## 8. Remaining limitations

- Auto Dance ratings are still all weight-1 (SHOWCASE/STRONG/CLEAN), by design — the mode remains a flawless reference performance.
- Arctic's lines end on a quiet breath (~9 dB under speech); audible but not clipped, and not worth re-rendering the pack over.
- Speech speed is `playbackRate`, so extreme speeds shift pitch slightly.
- Vietnamese lines are authored here and not native-reviewed.
- Mobile findings come from desktop emulation only; a real Android/iOS pass is still needed before shipping.

## 1\. Cơ chế mình đề xuất

Ví dụ khi người chơi đánh liên tục:

| Số note liên tiếp | Voice |
| --- | --- |
| Note 1–4 | Im lặng |
| Note 5 | PERFECT\! |
| Note 6–9 | Im lặng |
| Note 10 | FLAWLESS\! |
| Note 11–14 | Im lặng |
| Note 15 | EXCELLENT\! |
| Note 16–19 | Im lặng |
| Note 20 | AMAZING\! |
| Note 25 | INCREDIBLE\! |
| Note 30 | UNSTOPPABLE\! |
| Note 50 | LEGENDARY\! |
| Note 100 | GODLIKE\! |

Đây là ví dụ minh họa, không phải bắt buộc phải đọc tất cả các mốc.

## 2\. Chỉ đọc khi đạt điều kiện

Mình đề xuất 4 quy tắc:

- PERFECT: Chỉ đọc sau mỗi 5 PERFECT liên tiếp.
- FLAWLESS: Chỉ đọc ở mốc 10 hoặc 20 PERFECT liên tiếp.
- EXCELLENT: Chỉ đọc khi có thành tích nổi bật, không đọc mỗi note.
- GREAT / GOOD / NICE / OK: Mặc định im lặng.

Nếu người chơi đánh hụt hoặc combo bị phá, bộ đếm PERFECT liên tiếp sẽ reset.

## 3\. Cơ chế ưu tiên voice

| Sự kiện | Voice |
| --- | --- |
| PERFECT milestone | Có |
| FLAWLESS milestone | Có |
| EXCELLENT milestone | Có |
| Combo 10/25/50 | Có |
| Combo 100 | Voice đặc biệt |
| FULL COMBO | Voice đặc biệt |
| NEW RECORD | Voice đặc biệt |
| GOOD / GREAT liên tục | Không |
| MISS thông thường | Không |
| BOMB thông thường | Không |

Một nguyên tắc rất quan trọng: Khi FULL COMBO hoặc NEW RECORD xuất hiện, phải ưu tiên hơn tất cả voice thông thường.

# STAGE 6.2 – SMART VOICE ANNOUNCER

## REDUCE VOICE SPAM – MILESTONE-BASED ANNOUNCEMENTS

PROJECT: D:\\AI\\appDevelopment\\3danceYTApp

PIPER: D:\\AI\\appDevelopment\\piper

IMPORTANT: Keep both directories completely independent. Do not modify or regenerate existing voice assets unless necessary.

## OBJECTIVE

Currently the announcer speaks too frequently, sometimes announcing nearly every successful note.

Change this behavior.

The announcer must become selective, milestone-based and intelligent.

The game should feel exciting, not noisy.

CORE RULE:

Do not announce every judgement.

Only announce meaningful achievements and milestones.

## 1\. NORMAL JUDGEMENTS

Default behavior:

- PERFECT: Silent unless a configured milestone is reached.
- EXCELLENT: Silent unless a special achievement is reached.
- GREAT: Silent.
- GOOD: Silent.
- NICE: Silent.
- OK: Silent.
- MISS: Silent by default.
- BOMB: Silent by default.

Remove routine per-note voice announcements.

Do not remove visual judgement feedback or gameplay sounds.

## 2\. PERFECT STREAK ANNOUNCEMENTS

Create a separate consecutive PERFECT counter.

Suggested default milestones:

- 5 consecutive PERFECT: "PERFECT\!"
- 10 consecutive PERFECT: "FLAWLESS\!"
- 20 consecutive PERFECT: "INCREDIBLE\!"
- 30 consecutive PERFECT: "UNSTOPPABLE\!"
- 50 consecutive PERFECT: "LEGENDARY\!"
- 100 consecutive PERFECT: "GODLIKE\!"

Important:

These are configurable milestones.

Do not announce at every note.

Do not announce the same milestone twice during one streak.

Reset the PERFECT streak when the player receives a judgement below PERFECT or misses a note.

Do not reset the normal gameplay combo unless existing game rules require it.

## 3\. COMBO MILESTONES

Use configurable combo milestones.

Suggested defaults:

- 10
- 25
- 50
- 100
- 200
- 500

Do not announce every combo number.

Avoid duplicate announcements when a PERFECT streak milestone and combo milestone happen on the same note.

If both occur simultaneously, select the more important announcement.

## 4\. SPECIAL ANNOUNCEMENTS

Always preserve high-priority announcements:

- FULL COMBO.
- NEW RECORD.
- PERFECT RUN.
- FEVER when appropriate.

These announcements must have priority over routine milestone voice lines.

## 5\. GLOBAL COOLDOWN

Implement a configurable global voice cooldown.

Suggested default: 5 seconds.

Routine announcements must respect the cooldown.

Important exceptions:

- FULL COMBO.
- NEW RECORD.

Critical announcements can bypass ordinary cooldowns.

Never interrupt an important announcement with a low-priority line.

## 6\. VOICE PRIORITY

Use this priority:

1. NEW RECORD
2. FULL COMBO
3. PERFECT RUN
4. Major combo milestones
5. PERFECT streak milestones
6. FEVER
7. Other special announcements

Remove routine GREAT, GOOD, NICE and OK announcements from the default playback path.

## 7\. SETTINGS UI

Add Smart Announcer settings:

- Smart Announcer ON/OFF.
- PERFECT milestone interval.
- PERFECT streak milestone list.
- Combo milestone selection.
- Global cooldown.
- Enable/disable special announcements.
- Enable/disable MISS announcements.
- Enable/disable BOMB announcements.

Suggested defaults:

PERFECT milestones: 5, 10, 20, 30, 50, 100.

COMBO milestones: 10, 25, 50, 100, 200, 500.

Global cooldown: 5 seconds.

## 8\. AUTO DANCE

Apply the same announcement throttling to Auto Dance.

Do not announce every automatic judgement.

Auto Dance should use the same milestone and cooldown system.

Preserve its existing SHOWCASE / STRONG / CLEAN rating behavior.

## 9\. EVENT DEDUPLICATION

If multiple milestones happen on the same note:

- Select the highest-priority event.
- Do not play multiple overlapping announcements.
- Record already announced milestones.
- Prevent repeated announcements.

## 10\. TESTING

Add tests for:

- First four PERFECT notes are silent.
- Fifth PERFECT triggers one announcement.
- Tenth PERFECT triggers FLAWLESS.
- Milestones do not repeat.
- Streak resets correctly.
- Combo milestone handling.
- Simultaneous milestone handling.
- Global cooldown.
- Critical announcement bypass.
- FULL COMBO priority.
- NEW RECORD priority.
- Auto Dance throttling.
- Voice OFF.
- Existing visual judgements remain unchanged.

Run all existing regression tests.

Do not modify gameplay scoring, chart timing, existing voice assets or the two project directory structures.

## ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA

The announcer must be quiet during ordinary gameplay.

A player should be able to play dozens of notes without hearing repetitive commentary.

Voice announcements should feel like special rewards, not constant background chatter.

Preserve the existing VoiceAnnouncer architecture and report all modified files and test results.

## 6\. Ý kiến cuối cùng của mình

Mình sẽ chọn mặc định:

- 5 PERFECT mới đọc một lần.
- 10 PERFECT đọc FLAWLESS.
- 25/50/100 combo mới đọc.

Mình muốn người chơi nghe được một câu PERFECT và cảm thấy nó đáng giá, chứ không phải nghe PERFECT đến mức muốn tắt game.



Now implementing Stage 6.2. Starting with the line library, settings defaults and the announcer's milestone logic.





Now the announcer logic: streak tracking, milestone coalescing, and the global routine cooldown.




























Stage 6.2 is complete: the announcer is now milestone-only. Here is what changed and what was actually verified.

## Why it was noisy

Every judged note published a `game:judge` event and the announcer spoke the matching category, throttled only by a 900 ms per-category cooldown — so a clean run produced a line every second or so. That path is gone from the default behaviour.

## New behaviour

| Event | Voice |
| --- | --- |
| PERFECT / EXCELLENT / EPIC … GREAT, GOOD, NICE, OK | **silent** (visual tier, impact and HUD unchanged) |
| 5th, 10th, 20th, 30th, 50th, 100th consecutive flawless note | "Perfect!", "Flawless!", "Incredible!", "Unstoppable!", "Legendary!", "Godlike!" |
| combo 10 / 25 / 50 / 100 / 200 / 500 | combo calls (configurable) |
| MISS / BOMB | silent by default, opt-in |
| NEW RECORD, FULL COMBO, PERFECT RUN, FEVER | unchanged, highest priority |

The streak counter lives in the announcer: EPIC/EXCELLENT/PERFECT all extend it (they are the weight-1 band), anything lower or a miss resets it without touching the normal combo. Each milestone is recorded once per streak, and a new run clears the record.

**Tie-break on the same note:** combo 10 always lands on the same note as the 10th perfect, so a strict priority order would silence "FLAWLESS!" forever. A streak milestone therefore wins a same-note tie (it names the exact achievement) and the combo milestone is still marked announced so it never replays. With the default ladders the audible combo calls are 25 / 200 / 500; the other two are covered by the streak lines.

**Global cooldown** (5 s default, configurable 0–15 s) applies to routine milestone calls; NEW RECORD and FULL COMBO (and PERFECT RUN) bypass it, and a low line can never replace a results line already speaking. All milestones decided on one note are coalesced through a microtask, so only one line is ever submitted.

## Settings

New **SMART ANNOUNCER** block: milestone-only switch, global cooldown slider, "extra perfect every N" interval, and two chip rows for the perfect-streak and combo ladders — all persisted. Turning milestone mode off restores the Stage 6 per-note behaviour for players who want it.

![Smart announcer settings](screenshots/stage62-smart-announcer.png)

## Auto Dance

Uses the same event path, so it gets the same treatment: a 51-note auto run produced **one** spoken line (2 further milestones suppressed by the cooldown) while the SHOWCASE/STRONG/CLEAN rating spread, HUD labels, score and 100% accuracy stayed exactly as Stage 6.1 left them.

## Assets

Two new lines only — `streak.50` "Legendary!" / "Huyền thoại!" and `streak.100` "Godlike!" / "Thần thánh!" — generated for the four existing packs: **8 new WAVs, 176 total**. No existing audio file was modified or regenerated, and the Piper folder was only read from.

## Files

**Modified:** `src/audio/VoiceAnnouncer.js` (streak state, milestone coalescing, global cooldown, combo-from-judgement), `src/audio/voiceLines.js` (streak category/lines, priority order, critical threshold), `src/core/Settings.js` (smart-announcer defaults; miss/bomb now off by default), `src/ui/settingsMarkup.js` + `src/ui/UIManager.js` (panel, milestone chips, label updates), `src/styles/main.css`, `public/assets/audio/voice/manifest.json` + `tools/voice/packs.json` (new lines).
**New:** `qa/stage62-smart-announcer-check.mjs`, `qa-artifacts/stage62-smart-announcer-check.json`, 8 WAVs, screenshot.
**Unchanged:** `Gameplay.js`, `Scoring.js`, chart timing, difficulty windows, scoring weights, existing 168 WAVs, project layout.

## Checks actually run

- `stage62-smart-announcer` **68/68** — first four perfects silent, 5th speaks once, 10th says FLAWLESS, milestones never repeat, streak resets on GREAT and MISS, configurable combo ladder, simultaneous milestones play one line, global cooldown suppresses, critical bypass, FULL COMBO/NEW RECORD priorities, Auto Dance throttling, voice off, visuals unaffected, interval milestones, legacy mode.
- Regression: `stage5` 77/77, `stage36/42/43/44` exit 0, `stage45` 138/138, `stage46` 133/133, `stage6-judgement` 89/89, `stage6-voice` 89/89, `stage61-voice-polish` 60/60.
- `qa/voice-asset-audit.mjs`: 176 files, 1619/1619 checks.
- `npm run build` ✓, console clean on a fresh load.
- Browser: 12 perfect hits → silence until the 5th (`perfect.1` played), the 10th suppressed by the 5-second cooldown (`skippedCooldown` 1), milestone chips toggle and persist, `glError 0`.

## Limitations

- Tie-break means streak lines take precedence on the 10/50/100 overlaps (combo 25/200/500 still speak); if you would rather hear the combo call there, the ladders can be set to non-overlapping values in the panel.
- A milestone suppressed by the global cooldown is recorded as announced, so it is not replayed later — a deliberate "no backlog" choice.
- I could not listen to the audio: the eight new lines are verified for format, level, trimming and mapping, not for pronunciation.
- Nothing was committed and both project directories are untouched apart from the game's own files.

- Giây thứ 0: PERFECT.
- Giây thứ 2: FLAWLESS bị cooldown chặn.
- Giây thứ 5: không có câu nào được đọc nữa.

- Milestone nhỏ: có thể bỏ qua nếu cooldown.

- Cinematic: hoành tráng.

Stage 6.3 is complete. All 12 suites pass, the build is green, and the new behaviour is verified in the running game.

## 1. Milestone delivery

Milestones are now split into two delivery classes:

| Class | What | During the global cooldown |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Major** | FLAWLESS, INCREDIBLE, UNSTOPPABLE, LEGENDARY, GODLIKE, FULL COMBO, NEW RECORD, PERFECT RUN | held in **one** slot and played when the cooldown lifts, or dropped when its window expires |
| **Minor** | PERFECT ×5, combo milestones, small calls | discarded as before |

The hold is a single object, never a list: a second major either replaces a less important held one or is dropped (`pendingDropped`), so 40 majors inside one cooldown produce exactly one slot and zero backlog growth. The hold window defaults to **2.5 s** (0–6 s in the panel; 0 or the switch off restores discard). Delivery never cuts a line that is already speaking, so NEW RECORD and FULL COMBO keep the floor and the held major follows them; results clear any held milestone, since the run is over. The intra-note candidate list is also capped now, so a pathological burst can't grow it before the flush.

## 2. Independent counters

The counters live in `Scoring` and are reported on every judgement event, so the announcer no longer infers anything:

- **combo** — every hit, cleared only by miss/bomb.
- **flawlessStreak** — consecutive EPIC/EXCELLENT/PERFECT notes.
- **highAccuracyStreak** — consecutive GREAT-or-better notes.
- **Auto Dance rating** — unchanged, still `scoring.ratings`.

A GREAT now provably breaks the perfect streak while keeping the combo **and** the high-accuracy streak; a GOOD breaks the high-accuracy streak but keeps the combo; a miss clears all three. The announcer mirrors the reported streak, falling back to its own counter only for events that carry none.

## 3. Announcer styles

New `src/audio/announcerStyles.js` with four presets that adjust frequency, cooldown, eligible categories, interruption and pitch:

| Style | Cooldown | Scale | Categories | Cuts lines |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| ARCADE | 5.0 s | 1.0 | everything | yes |
| ESPORTS | 3.0 s | 0.8 | streaks, combos, results, no miss/bomb | yes |
| CINEMATIC | 7.0 s | 1.2 | streaks + results, no combo calls | no |
| MINIMAL | 12 s | 1.5 | results only | no |

Selecting a style also sets speed/duck/cooldown as a starting point, which the sliders can then override. Stored `epic`/`calm` values migrate to CINEMATIC/MINIMAL instead of resetting. No audio asset was touched or regenerated — 176 WAVs, unchanged.

## 4. Settings

Five independent switches/sliders were added on top of the existing ones: PERFECT STREAK CALLS, COMBO MILESTONE CALLS, MAJOR ACHIEVEMENTS, HOLD ONE MAJOR, HOLD MAJOR FOR. Everything from Stage 6/6.2 is preserved, and previously saved user settings still win over new defaults (the browser still had miss/bomb calls on from the old save, which is correct).

![Smart announcer settings](screenshots/stage63-smart-announcer.png)

## 5. Actual results

`qa/stage63-milestone-delivery-check.mjs` — **101/101**, covering: major held during cooldown and delivered, expiry (dropped, never played, not queued), FULL COMBO and NEW RECORD playing through the cooldown without being cut, the held major surviving them, one slot under a 40-major flood with no queue growth, no duplicate milestone playback, independent counters (perfect/great/good/miss matrix), GREAT resetting the streak while the combo continues, all four styles, unknown style fallback, each new setting in isolation, persistence plus legacy-style migration, panel markup, and Auto Dance.

Regression: `stage5` 77/77, `stage36/42/43/44` exit 0, `stage45` 138/138, `stage46` 133/133, `stage6-judgement` 89/89, `stage6-voice` 89/89, `stage61-voice-polish` 60/60, `stage62-smart-announcer` 68/68. `qa/voice-asset-audit.mjs` 1619/1619 (176 files, 5.4 MB). `npm run build` ✓, console clean.

Live game: 5th perfect → "Perfect!"; 10th → held (`perfect.2` pending) then delivered after the cooldown (`pendingHeld 1 / pendingDelivered 1 / pendingExpired 0`); 20th → INCREDIBLE played immediately when the cooldown was open; a GREAT landed at +128 ms with `combo 10, flawless 0, highAccuracy 10`; choosing ESPORTS stored cooldown 3000/rate 1.08/duck 0.35, effective 2400 ms, miss calls off, streaks on.

## Files

**Untouched:** chart timing, scoring values/weights, VFX, all WAV assets, `D:\AI\appDevelopment\piper`.

## Limitations

- Combo milestones are minor by the brief's own classification, so a combo milestone that lands inside the cooldown is still discarded rather than held.
- The delivery tests use short real-time waits (~0.7 s) against a shortened cooldown; the 5-second production cadence was exercised live rather than in every assertion.
- I did not listen to the audio — pronunciation of the existing lines is unchanged and unverified by ear.

A natural next step is a run summary of what the announcer actually said, so you can tune the ladders against real play.

- Số lần announcer đã nói.

- Số combo milestones.
- Số major achievements.
- Số voice bị cooldown bỏ qua.
- Số major announcements được giữ và phát thành công.
- Phong cách announcer được sử dụng.

Không cần phát triển thành hệ thống lớn. Chỉ cần giúp chúng ta biết announcer có đang nói quá nhiều hay quá ít.

# STAGE 7 — PHASE 1 INSPECTION REPORT

## 0. Headline finding

The project contains **no 3D model files at all** — no `.glb`, `.gltf`, `.fbx`, `.obj`, `.vrm` anywhere under `src/` or `public/`. There is **no skeleton, no skinning, no `AnimationMixer` and no animation clips**. The dancer is 100 % procedural: a low-poly mannequin built in code from 17 joint positions, drawn either as a 2D canvas silhouette (what players actually see in play) or as a 3D body in the Video Studio.

`grep` results backing this: zero hits for `AnimationMixer`, `AnimationClip`, `AnimationAction`, `Bone(`, `SkinnedMesh`; the only `Skeleton` symbol is the custom panel `src/studio/SkeletonView.js` (a line-drawing viewer, not a `THREE.Skeleton`).

---

## 1. Model inventory

| Property | Finding |
| --- | --- |
| File formats | **None** — no model assets exist. Everything is generated by `src/studio/avatar/ProceduralAvatar.js` |
| Model names | `procedural-avatar` → child group `avatar-body` (Three.js object names) |
| Presets | `male` (1.80 m, shoulder 1.0, hip 0.86) and `female` (1.72 m, shoulder 0.82, hip 1.04) |
| Costumes | `aurora` "Aurora Jacket" (default), `chrome` "Chrome Circuit", `shadow` "Shadow Runner" |
| Meshes | **44 meshes** (43 rendered — the head joint marker is hidden in game) |
| Triangles | **2,404 base triangles** (2,340 rendered): 17 cylinders, 19 spheres, 6 boxes, 2 tori |
| Unique geometries | **8** (shared, re-instanced by scale) |
| Materials | **7** — suit (Standard), panel (Standard), trim (Basic additive), accent (Basic additive), skin (Standard), hair (Standard), eye (Basic) |
| Textures | **0** — no image maps, no atlases, no UV work |
| Texture resolution / format | N/A |
| Dimensions | Preset height 1.72 m; posed bounds measured 1.61 W × 2.11 H × 0.66 D (raised arm included), hips at 0.94 m, shoulders ±0.235 m (0.47 m span), feet at 0.055 m |
| Build cost | `new ProceduralAvatar()` **1.3 ms**, module import 3 ms |
| Appearance | Low-poly mannequin: cylinder limbs with constant radius, sphere joints, sphere head + hair (ponytail on female), visor bar, chest plate, belt, torus shoulder pads, box boots, box skirt (female). Flat palette colours with emissive trim and additive accents, driven by the live theme palette |

**Suitability for high-quality dance animation: no.** It is a readable stylised puppet (≈2.4 k tris, rigid segments, 17 joints, no skin, no hands, no toes, no fingers). It is excellent for music-locked stylised motion at near-zero cost, and unsuitable as a "high-quality dancer" by any Mixamo/UE-style standard.

---

## 2. Skeleton / rig

The "rig" is a **pose data structure**, not a scene-graph skeleton: 17 joints in avatar-local metres (`src/studio/pose/PoseProvider.js`, `POSE_JOINTS` line 8) plus a `root` transform `{x, y, z, yaw}`.

**Hierarchy** (`POSE_EDGES`, 16 segments → 16 cylinder meshes):

```
root (position + yaw)
└ hips ── spine ── chest ── neck ── head
                    ├ leftShoulder ── leftElbow ── leftWrist
                    └ rightShoulder ─ rightElbow ─ rightWrist
   hips ─ leftHip ── leftKnee ── leftAnkle
   hips ─ rightHip ─ rightKnee ─ rightAnkle
```

| Requested bone | Present? | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Root | ✔ (single Object3D transform) | not a joint; yaw + x/y/z offset |
| Pelvis / hips | ✔ | top of the joint chain, no separate pelvis rotation |
| Spine | ✔ | one segment |
| Chest | ✔ | one segment |
| Neck | ✔ | short segment |
| Head | ✔ | position only — no head rotation channel (rotation derived from neck→head delta) |
| Shoulders | ✔ | direct children of chest — **no clavicle** |
| Arms | ✔ | upper + forearm implied by elbow joint |
| Hands | ✖ | no hand geometry; only a wrist sphere |
| **Fingers** | ✖ | **zero finger bones** |
| Legs | ✔ | hip → knee → ankle |
| Feet | ⚠ | ankle only — **no foot/toe bone**, boots rotate with body yaw only |

Total: **17 joints + 1 root, 0 `THREE.Bone` objects, 0 `Skeleton`, 0 `SkinnedMesh`.**

**Missing / problematic**

- No fingers, toes, clavicles, or separate hand/foot orientation → no fist, point, wave, heel-toe or foot-roll articulation.
- Head has no independent rotation channel; head turn is a by-product of neck/head offsets.
- No pelvis/chest twist channels — "turns" are faked (see below).
- No IK: limbs are placed by absolute joint positions.

**Capability check**

| Skill | Supported? | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Full-body dancing | ⚠ partially | continuous, musical, full-body motion exists (`DanceChoreography`), but no articulated extremities |
| Footwork | ⚠ weak | ankle Y lift up to ~0.12 m (`AutoPoseProvider` `leftStep/rightStep`), no toe, no IK → feet slide when hips sway; grounding clamps `min(ankleY) ≥ 0.03` |
| Spins | ✖ | `root.yaw` is ±0.12 rad sinusoid (+0.28 transient on drops) ≈ ±7°–23°; the `turn` family compresses the shoulder line in X (`DanceChoreography`) — a 2D fake, no real body rotation |
| Jumps | ⚠ | `root.y` = bounce (≤0.085) + impact jump (≤0.16) ≈ **≤0.25 m**, feet never leave the ground plane properly |
| Upper-body choreography | ✔ strongest area | 7 weighted families with 12-beat crossfades, phrase arc, section bias, kick/snare accents |
| Hand movements | ⚠ | wrist translation only; no wrist rotation, no hand mesh |

---

## 3. Skinning & deformation

**There is no skinning.** No vertex weights, no bones, no bind pose. Deformation is *rigid segment stretching*: each frame every cylinder is placed at the segment midpoint, oriented by `quaternion.setFromUnitVectors(up, direction)` and scaled to the joint distance (`ProceduralAvatar._updateBones`); gaps are hidden by spheres at the joints.

Consequences per area:

| Area | Behaviour | Problem |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Shoulder | upper arm cylinder starts at a fixed shoulder point; a torus pad sits on the shoulder | no deltoid/trapezius blending — the arm visibly detaches at high raise; pad floats |
| Elbow | sphere joint + two cylinders | constant limb radius → elbow looks pinched, no crease; sphere hides the seam only up to ~120° |
| Knee | `DanceChoreography` rebuilds knees at the hip/ankle midpoint plus a forward Z offset and clamps them between hip and ankle | knee can invert or "pop" at extreme lifts; no bending limit |
| Hip | legs rotate about `hips` joint; no pelvis skin blend | wide leg lifts shear the hip joint sphere |
| Spine bending | three rigid segments (hips→spine→chest) with per-segment rotations implied by joint offsets | no smooth bend; the torso is a chain of sticks, and the chest plate is a separate box offset +0.065 z (it can visibly separate from the body) |
| Wrist / hand | wrist is a sphere only | no wrist rotation or hand shape at all |

Additional code-level issues found while reading:

- `_disposeProcedural()` contains a `traverse` with an empty body (dead branch) and never disposes per-instance clones — the shared-geometry comment is correct, but the loop does nothing.
- `GameAvatarView.update()` calls `avatar.applyPalette(this._palette)` **every frame**, which re-copies colours into 6 materials per frame for no benefit (the palette only changes on theme change).
- `boots` use `rotation.y = pose.root.yaw` — feet do not follow ankle orientation.

---

## 4. Animation system

**No `AnimationMixer`, no `AnimationClip`, no `AnimationAction`, no clip crossfade, no clip looping.** Animation is a deterministic pose function evaluated from song time — there is nothing to seek or loop because the pose is a function of `t`.

### Pipeline (actual code path)

```
MusicPlayer.getTime()  →  AutoPoseProvider.getPose(t, bpm, energy, bass, section, style, seed)
                       →  DanceChoreography.getPose(...)     [2D silhouette path only]
                       →  PoseCrossfade.apply(pose, t)      [only after style change / seek]
                       →  ProceduralAvatar.applyPose(pose, dt)  → 44 mesh transforms
                       →  SkeletonView.update(pose)         [RIG overlay]
```

| Concern | Implementation |
| --- | --- |
| Blending | `PoseCrossfade` (`src/studio/pose/PoseBlend.js`) blends two whole pose frames with smoothstep over 0.45 s; used on style change and seek. `ProceduralAvatar` adds exponential smoothing `alpha = 1 − e^(−20·dt)` and `damp()` on root y/x/z/yaw (20/12/12/7) |
| Crossfade of moves | 7 families (`groove, upper, shoulders, arms, hips, legs, turn`) with weights that crossfade on a **12-beat** cycle; arm shapes blended by `weight^2.1` |
| Playback speed | derived from BPM (`beat = time · bpm/60`); no per-clip `timeScale` |
| Loop handling | implicit: phrases from a modulo cycle, family cycle modulo 12 beats |
| Pause/resume | `poseDt = playing ? dt : 0` freezes smoothing; silhouette `seek()` clears caches; root motion still follows time when playing |
| Cleanup | `ProceduralAvatar.dispose()`, `SkeletonView.dispose()`, `GameAvatarView.dispose()`, `DanceSilhouette.dispose()` all present and dispose geometries/materials/render targets |

### "Clip" inventory (procedural moves, durations in beats)

| Item | Duration | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Movement families (7) | 12 beats each cycle | groove, upper, shoulders, arms, hips, legs, turn |
| Phrase cycle | **124 beats total** — intro-groove 16, main-dance 32, accent 16, variation 32, recovery 16, transition 12 | at 128 BPM ≈ 58 s |
| Dance styles (4) | continuous | `pulse`, `flow`, `cyber`, `street` (`STYLE_PROFILES`) |
| Section reactions | 6 section types | intro/verse/build-up/drop/breakdown/outro bias |
| Expression layer (2D) | lane + combo + miss envelopes | `src/game/DanceExpression.js` |

### Where each renderer is used

| Component | Status | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `DanceSilhouette` (2D canvas) | **live in gameplay** | `Game._ensureSilhouette()` / `_updateSilhouette()` (`src/core/Game.js:864,878`); measured 5,440 drawn pixels in the 64×85 panel |
| `ProceduralAvatar` (3D) | **live in Video Studio** | `StudioStage.update()` applies the pose at `src/studio/StudioStage.js:371` |
| `GameAvatarView` (3D HUD panel) | **orphaned / dead code** | no importer anywhere in `src/`; the HUD panel it targets is disabled by `.hud__avatar, .hud__avatar-show { display: none !important; }` (`src/styles/main.css:2787`) and `EVENTS.UI_AVATAR` has **no listener** |

That last row is the most important architectural defect: the HUD "LIVE DANCER" 3D panel, its style/sex/scale/lights/RIG controls and `AvatarPreferences` all still exist and emit events, but nothing renders into `avatar-canvas` — a previous "NEON TUNNEL REDESIGN" replaced the 3D panel with the left silhouette and left the 3D view class unwired.

---

## 5. Runtime performance (measured, not estimated)

Measured in the running dev build on this machine: **NVIDIA RTX 3060 via ANGLE/D3D11**, viewport 390×844 at DPR 1, `quality` default. Dev-mode numbers, not a production profile.

| Measurement | Result | Method |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Procedural avatar build | **1.3 ms** | `new ProceduralAvatar()` timed |
| Module import (avatar) | 3 ms | dynamic import timed |
| Full `GameAvatarView` construction | **36.2 ms** | renderer + scene + 10 shader programs compiled |
| Pose evaluation (provider) | **0.0057 ms** | 400 calls averaged |
| Pose application to 44 meshes | **0.038 ms** | 300 calls averaged |
| 3D avatar draw calls | **43** (one per mesh, no merging/instancing) | `renderer.info` after avatar-only render |
| 3D viewport total | **52 calls, 4,644 tris, 46 lines, 64 points, 16 geometries, 1 texture, 10 programs** | same |
| 3D viewport update+render | **0.8 ms/frame** at 400×500 | 60 frames + `gl.finish()` |
| Shadows | **off** — `shadowMap.enabled = false`, body meshes `castShadow = false`; one fake contact-shadow disc | code + renderer state |
| Whole gameplay frame | **58 calls, 6,937 tris, 140 lines, 1,034 points, 25 programs, 16 textures, 35 geometries** | `info.autoReset = false` across the composer passes |
| Whole frame CPU | **0.93 ms** at 390×844 | 120 `_tick()` calls |
| 2D silhouette draw | **0.045 ms/frame** at 64×85 | 300 `_updateSilhouette()` calls |
| JS heap during play | **139.5 MB used / 157.4 MB total** | `performance.memory` |
| App load (dev server) | DOMContentLoaded 356 ms, load 723 ms | Navigation timing |
| GPU memory | **not measurable** (no WebGL API for it) | — |
| Build size | `index` 1,165 kB (323 kB gz), `GLTFLoader` chunk 46.4 kB (13.6 kB gz), dist total 7.1 MB (mostly audio) | `npm run build` |

Caveats: the preview viewport is small (390×844), so the 0.93 ms/frame figure is **not** representative of 1080p-with-bloom gameplay; the draw-call, triangle and per-avatar numbers are resolution-independent and are the trustworthy ones here.

---

## 6. External animation compatibility

| Asset type | Works today? | Details |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **GLB / glTF with skeleton + clips** | ⚠ loads, but **animations will not play** | `ProceduralAvatar.loadExternalModel()` (`src/studio/avatar/ProceduralAvatar.js:504`) imports `GLTFLoader`, adds `gltf.scene`, clones materials, sets `frustumCulled = false`. No mixer is created, no clip is selected, no skinning update is driven → the mesh renders in bind/T-pose. Studio upload accepts `.glb/.gltf` (`src/ui/studioMarkup.js:91`), toast says "External GLTF/GLB loaded" |
| **FBX (Mixamo)** | ✖ | `FBXLoader.js` exists in `node_modules/three/examples/jsm/loaders/`, but is never imported or bundled; no FBX upload control |
| **Retargeting** | ✖ | no bone mapping, no `SkeletonUtils` usage (the helper exists in `node_modules` but is unused); no `mixamorig*` handling |
| **Root motion** | ✖ | root is a `{x,y,z,yaw}` offset used for bounce; nothing extracts translation from clips |
| **Skeleton compatibility** | N/A | there is no skeleton to be compatible with — any external rig would need its own mixer and a mapping to the project's 17-joint pose contract |
| Infrastructure that *is* ready | ✔ | `GLTFLoader` is already code-split (46 kB), `three` r187 includes `SkeletonUtils`, the Studio has a model upload + "RESET RIG" flow, and the pose contract (`PoseProvider`) is a clean seam for a new target |

**Conclusion:** a Mixamo/Mixamo→GLB clip can be *loaded* in the Studio today but will appear frozen. Making external animation usable requires an `AnimationMixer` layer plus either (a) retargeting clips onto a new skinned rig, or (b) driving a skinned rig's bones from the existing procedural pose so the choreography keeps working.

---

## 7. Rig quality assessment

**Strengths**
- Deterministic and seek-safe: pose is `f(time, bpm, energy, bass, section, style, seed)` — no accumulated state, so pause/seek/restart reproduce exactly (validated by `qa/stage44-dancer-check.mjs`, which passes).
- Genuinely musical: 7 families, 12-beat crossfades, 124-beat phrase arc, section bias, kick/snare accents, per-lane reactions.
- Clean seams: `PoseProvider` contract, `PoseCrossfade`, `PoseBlend`, `DanceExpression`; the silhouette and the 3D body consume the same frames.
- Cheap: 0.045 ms silhouette, 0.08 ms pose+apply, no textures, no shadow map.

**Weaknesses**
- No articulated extremities (fingers/toes/hands/feet), no real rotation, no IK → can't do convincing footwork, spins or jumps.
- 43 draw calls for 2.4 k triangles — the worst ratio in the project; every segment is a separate mesh.
- Orphaned 3D HUD view (`GameAvatarView` never constructed; panel CSS-disabled; `UI_AVATAR` unheard).
- Constant-radius segments and floating accessories (chest plate, shoulder pads, skirt) read as a puppet at close range.

---

## 8. Recommended improvements (for Phase 2 and later)

1. **Keep the choreography, replace the body.** The pose pipeline is the valuable asset; a skinned body should be *driven* by it rather than replacing it. That preserves Stage 4.x tests, determinism and the audio-reactive behaviour.
2. **Introduce a rig adapter layer** — `DancerRig` with two implementations behind one interface (`applyPose(pose, dt)`, `setStyle`, `dispose`): `ProceduralRig` (current, fallback/mobile) and `SkinnedRig` (GLB + bones).
3. **Fix the fidelity gaps in the contract**: add `leftToe/rightToe`, `leftHand/rightHand` orientation, clavicles, and pelvis/chest twist channels to `POSE_JOINTS`; add two-bone IK for arms/legs and a foot-plant constraint so ankle lifts stop sliding.
4. **Real turns**: rotate `root.yaw` properly with a spin arc, and rotate the 3D body (the silhouette can keep the 2D fake).
5. **Add an `AnimationMixer` layer** for non-choreography states (idle, drop impact, victory, fail) with explicit crossfades, `mixer.timeScale` for pause, and `mixer.setTime(songTime)` on seek for determinism.
6. **Performance**: merge the 44 procedural meshes into a handful of merged/instanced geometries (target ≤ 6 draw calls) and stop re-applying the palette every frame. A skinned GLB at ≤ 30 k tris and ≤ 4 materials stays inside the same budget.
7. **Decide the dead 3D HUD panel**: either wire `GameAvatarView` lazily when the panel is visible (it works — I instantiated and rendered it during this inspection) or delete the orphan markup, preferences and controls. Leaving it half-wired confuses Stage 7 work.
8. **Asset intake rules** (when approved): one humanoid GLB, ≤ 30 k tris, ≤ 8 materials, ≤ 2 textures ≤ 2 k, licence recorded like the voice pack licences (`tools/voice/licenses.json` pattern), stored under `public/assets/models/` with a manifest — no downloads until you sign off.

---

## 9. Stage 7 Phase 2 implementation plan

**Goal:** a high-quality, articulated dancer driven by the existing deterministic choreography, with the procedural rig kept as fallback, and no changes to gameplay, scoring, chart timing, VFX or voice.

**Step 1 — Contract extension (no visual change)**
Extend `POSE_JOINTS`/`POSE_EDGES` with toes, hands and clavicles; keep every existing joint name. Update `AutoPoseProvider`, `DanceChoreography`, `DanceExpression`, `SkeletonView`, `ProceduralAvatar` to fill the new fields (conservative defaults).
*Acceptance:* `qa/stage44-dancer-check.mjs` green with extended anatomy checks; existing poses byte-identical for the original joints.

**Step 2 — Rig adapter**
Add `src/studio/rig/DancerRig.js` (interface), `ProceduralRig.js` (wraps `ProceduralAvatar`), `BoneDriver.js` (maps joint triples → bone quaternions, two-bone IK for arms/legs, foot IK with toe roll).
*Acceptance:* a unit suite (`qa/stage7-rig-check.mjs`) proving joint→bone mapping, IK length preservation, foot planting, and that the adapter is a drop-in for `StudioStage` and `GameAvatarView`.

**Step 3 — Skinned asset integration**
Add `SkinnedRig` using `GLTFLoader` + `AnimationMixer`: bind-pose validation, bone-name mapping table, `dispose()` of mixer/actions/textures, and a manifest entry. Ship the procedural body as the fallback when the asset is missing or `quality: low`.
*Acceptance:* avatar renders with the same pose as the procedural one (overlay comparison test), 1–3 draw calls, no shadow-map cost, clean reload/dispose.

**Step 4 — Animation layer**
Idle/impact/victory/fail clips with explicit crossfades and `setTime(songTime)` seek determinism; `mixer.timeScale` follows pause; root motion disabled unless explicitly enabled.
*Acceptance:* pause/resume/seek tests, no drift after 10 seeks, clip inventory test.

**Step 5 — HUD panel decision + performance pass**
Wire `GameAvatarView` lazily behind the existing panel (or remove it), merge procedural meshes to ≤ 6 draw calls, stop per-frame palette application, add a `renderer.info` debug readout to the existing debug API.
*Acceptance:* measured draw-call drop, stable 60 fps in the enlarged viewport, console clean.

**Step 6 — Regression**
`stage44`, `stage45`, `stage46`, `stage5`, `stage6*`, `stage61-63`, `voice-asset-audit`, `npm run build`.

**Risks / open decisions for you**
- Which body to chase: keep stylised mannequin (cheap, on-brand) vs. realistic humanoid (needs a licensed GLB, retargeting, bigger QA surface).
- Where the 3D dancer should live: HUD panel (currently dead), Studio only, or both.
- Whether external Mixamo clips are a product requirement — if yes, Step 3 must include `SkeletonUtils.retargetClip` and a bone-name table, which roughly doubles that step.

---

### What I verified vs. what I did not

- **Verified from files:** all counts above (case-by-case traversal of `procedural-avatar`), pose hierarchy, code paths, absence of mixers/clips/skeleton, the orphaned HUD view, GLTFLoader chunk presence, unused `SkeletonUtils`.
- **Verified at runtime:** avatar build/render costs, draw calls, triangles, silhouette drawing, whole-frame cost at 390×844, JS heap, load timings, and that `GameAvatarView` renders correctly when instantiated.
- **Not verified:** GPU memory (no API), true full-screen FPS with bloom on target hardware, and behaviour with an actual Mixamo/FBX file (none downloaded, as instructed). No files were changed; `npm run build` and `stage44` were run as-is and pass.

The choreography is worth keeping and the body is the weak link; the cleanest Stage 7 path is to drive a skinned rig from the existing pose contract.
