Phase 2 — architecture plus tests, with no external asset. First, the exact integration points.

























Now the automated test suite for the rig architecture.










Most failures are test-harness expectations, but two look real. Debugging those before touching the suites.

Found it: the driver used the *parent's* bind orientation instead of the bone's own, which discards a bone's authored roll. Fixing the bind math.







Two real bugs found: the model node was inside the bone chain (history-dependent bones), and clip seeks drifted. Fixing both, then the test expectations.










Stage 7 Phase 2 is implemented and verified: the rig seam exists, both rigs implement it, and the Studio runs on it with no behaviour change.

## Architecture

```
AutoPoseProvider ─┐
DanceChoreography ─┴─> pose frame (17 joints + root) ──> DancerRig.applyPose()
                                                          ├── ProceduralRig  (ProceduralAvatar, no clips)
                                                          └── SkinnedRig     (bones + ClipPlayer)
                                                                  ├── BoneMap   joint → bone resolution
                                                                  ├── BoneDriver pose → bone rotations (+IK, foot plant)
                                                                  └── ClipPlayer AnimationMixer wrapper
                                                          createDancerRig() picks one, always with a fallback
```

**New modules** (`src/studio/rig/`):

| File | Responsibility |
| --- | --- |
| `DancerRig.js` | The contract (`kind`, `group`, `supportsClips`, `clipInventory`, `applyPose`, `setOrigin`, `setPalette`, `playClip`, `setClipTime`, `update`, `dispose`) plus `isDancerRig()` and `RIG_KINDS` |
| `ProceduralRig.js` | `ProceduralAvatar` behind the seam — identical rendering, no clips. Also the mobile/low-quality fallback |
| `BoneMap.js` | Alias table (v2) for Mixamo/Blender/generic humanoids, prefix+separator normalisation, `buildBoneMap`, `isBoneMapComplete`, `buildRetargetRemap`, extras for toes/fingers. Positional rule: a joint maps to the bone whose **head** sits at that joint |
| `BoneDriver.js` | `bindRig(bones, {space})`, `applyPoseToBones()` (delta pass + residual correction), `solveTwoBoneIK()` (analytic, pole-vector), `computeFootPlant()`/`shiftPose()`, `JOINT_CHILD`/`JOINT_ORDER` |
| `ClipPlayer.js` | `AnimationMixer` wrapper: inventory, play with crossfade, deterministic `setTime`, pause/resume via `timeScale`, `stopAll`, `dispose` |
| `SkinnedRig.js` | Skinned implementation: bone map + driver + clips + placement stage, palette rim tint, optional foot plant, diagnostics |
| `createDancerRig.js` | Factory with quality gate (`low` → procedural), `hasSkinnedMesh`, `inspectModelForRig` pre-flight |

**Modified:** `src/studio/StudioStage.js:11,68` and `src/game/GameAvatarView.js:4,85` now construct `ProceduralRig` instead of `ProceduralAvatar`. Both are drop-in subclasses, so behaviour is bit-identical (a test proves it).

## Two real bugs the tests caught

1. **Bone roll was discarded.** The driver used the *parent's* bind orientation, so a rig whose bones encode limb direction in their own rest quaternion (every Blender rig, most Mixamo exports) came out rotated by that roll — measured 1.56–2.79 rad. Fixed by binding the bone's own world orientation and adding a residual pass that rotates by the measured remaining error, parents first. The rolled synthetic rig now aligns to **0.000000 rad**.
2. **The model node sat inside the bone chain.** `SkinnedRig` first placed the pose's position/yaw on the model itself, so each frame's bones inherited the previous frame's transform — replay drifted 0.106 rad. Fixed with a dedicated placement stage and space-scoped binding (`bindRig(..., { space })`), so the skeleton is driven in avatar-local space. Replay now matches to 1e-9.

Also fixed: `ClipPlayer.setTime` drifted 4e-4 rad per repeated seek because the mixer inherited interpolation state; actions are now reset before evaluation, making seeks exact.

## Testing (actual results)

`qa/stage7-rig-check.mjs` — **90/90**, no external asset: a humanoid skeleton is built in code (Mixamo names, arbitrary rest rotations, toe bones) with a real `SkinnedMesh` + `Skeleton`, and clips are built with `QuaternionKeyframeTrack`.

Covered: interface/guard (raw avatar rejected, rig accepted), procedural parity over 20 choreography frames (position Δ 0, quaternion Δ 0), bone mapping (mixamo, Blender, missing-bone reporting, coverage, extras, retarget remap), driving (12 orientable bones, 5 leaves skipped, alignment < 1e-4, no bone stretch < 1e-6, deterministic, NaN-free, rolled rigs), IK (reach exact, lengths preserved, unreachable clamped, degenerate target, pole steering), foot planting, ClipPlayer (inventory, play/fade, deterministic seek, wrap, pause/resume, dispose), SkinnedRig (origin/yaw, diagnostics, clip playback, replay and seek determinism, rejection of an incomplete skeleton), factory (defaults, skinned, quality gate, non-skinned model, fallback on unmappable), and integration (both consumers import the seam, choreography determinism, skinned replay from song time).

Regression: `stage44` dancer ✓, `stage45` 138/138, `stage46` 133/133, `stage5` 77/77, `stage36/42/43` ✓, `stage6-judgement` 89/89, `stage6-voice` 89/89, `stage61` 60/60, `stage62` 68/68, `stage63` 101/101. **`npm run build` ✓** (1,165.87 kB / 323.30 kB gz — unchanged).

Browser: Studio opens and renders with `ProceduralRig` (kind `procedural`, 33 meshes, 17 pose joints, clip inventory 0, skeleton panel present, hips moving between frames), console and network clean.

## Deliberately not done

- **No character asset** downloaded or replaced; the procedural dancer is still what renders everywhere.
- **IK and foot-plant are implemented, tested and exported but not wired into the live pose path** — doing so would change the existing dance, which this phase must not do. They are ready for `SkinnedRig` limbs once an asset is approved.
- **Mixamo FBX is still unsupported** (GLB only; `FBXLoader` remains unbundled), and retargeting is name-level (`buildRetargetRemap`) without `SkeletonUtils.retargetClip`.
- The HUD 3D dancer panel is still CSS-disabled and its `GameAvatarView` still has no consumer — that decision belongs to a later phase; changing it was out of scope here.
- Draw calls for the procedural body are unchanged (43 meshes → 43 calls); merging them is Phase 3 work.

## Changed files

**New:** `src/studio/rig/{DancerRig,ProceduralRig,BoneMap,BoneDriver,ClipPlayer,SkinnedRig,createDancerRig}.js`, `qa/stage7-rig-check.mjs`, `qa-artifacts/stage7-rig-check.json`.
**Modified:** `src/studio/StudioStage.js`, `src/game/GameAvatarView.js`.
**Untouched:** gameplay/scoring/chart/judgement/voice/audio, `AutoPoseProvider`, `DanceChoreography`, `ProceduralAvatar`, all WAV assets, and `D:\AI\appDevelopment\piper`.

The seam is ready for a real skinned character: dropping a mapped GLB into `createDancerRig({ model, clips })` switches bodies without touching the choreography.



11111111111111 song duration
Được\! Mình đồng ý nên ưu tiên sửa việc này trước Stage 7 Phase 3.

Đây là lỗi quan trọng của gameplay: bài nhạc phải có điểm kết thúc thực sự, không được chạy vô tận hoặc để dancer tiếp tục nhảy sau khi nhạc đã hết.

Mình đề xuất làm một Stage riêng: Stage 7.1 – Song Duration & Playback Lifecycle.

## 1\. Hành vi cần đạt được

Ví dụ bài nhạc dài 3 phút 25 giây:

- 00:00 – 03:25: Nhạc phát, chart chạy và dancer biểu diễn.
- 03:25: Nhạc kết thúc đúng thời điểm.
- Dừng chart spawning và gameplay clock.
- Dừng dancer hoặc chuyển sang animation outro.
- Chuyển sang Results Screen.
- Hiển thị điểm, accuracy, combo và judgement statistics.

Không được phụ thuộc vào việc người chơi có đánh hết các note hay không.

## 2\. Những trường hợp phải kiểm tra

- Pause giữa bài: thời gian phải dừng, không tự kết thúc.
- Resume: tiếp tục đúng vị trí.
- Seek đến cuối bài: kết thúc đúng một lần.
- Restart: reset thời gian và kết quả.
- Auto Dance: cũng phải kết thúc theo nhạc.
- Song lỗi hoặc duration không xác định: không được tự kết thúc sai.
- Bài ngắn: không để chart hoặc note chạy vượt quá thời lượng bài.
- Không phát voice milestone mới sau khi kết thúc.

Nên dùng audio playback làm nguồn thời gian chuẩn, không dùng `setTimeout(duration)` làm đồng hồ chính. Nếu dùng HTMLAudioElement thì xử lý `ended`; nếu dùng Web Audio buffer thì xử lý thời điểm kết thúc nguồn và đồng bộ playback clock.

MDN

+2

## 3\. Prompt cho OpenCode

Bạn gửi nguyên prompt này:

Writing

# STAGE 7.1 — SONG DURATION & PLAYBACK LIFECYCLE

Project: D:\\AI\\appDevelopment\\3danceYTApp

Piper: D:\\AI\\appDevelopment\\piper

IMPORTANT: These are two separate directories. Never move, merge or reorganize them.

## OBJECTIVE

Fix the song playback lifecycle.

Currently, songs can continue running indefinitely. Every song must stop at its actual media duration and transition cleanly to the Results screen.

## PHASE 1 — INSPECTION

Inspect:

- MusicPlayer.js
- AudioManager.js
- Game.js
- Gameplay.js
- Chart playback and note spawning
- Game clock and song clock
- Pause/resume/restart
- Results screen transition
- Auto Dance
- Song library metadata and duration handling

Identify the authoritative playback clock and actual audio duration source.

Do not assume chart duration equals audio duration.

## PHASE 2 — SONG END HANDLING

Implement a reliable song-end lifecycle.

Requirements:

1. Read the actual audio duration.
2. Start the song clock from actual playback.
3. Detect the true end of playback.
4. Stop gameplay at song end.
5. Stop spawning new notes.
6. Prevent new judgements after the song ends.
7. Stop or transition the dancer to an outro.
8. Stop routine voice announcements.
9. Trigger Results exactly once.
10. Release playback resources correctly.

## PHASE 3 — SYNCHRONIZATION

The following must use the same authoritative song timeline:

- Music playback.
- Gameplay clock.
- Note spawning.
- Note judgement.
- Auto Dance.
- DanceChoreography.
- Dancer animation.
- Progress bar.

Do not use independent timers that drift away from audio playback.

## PHASE 4 — EDGE CASES

Test:

- Normal playback to completion.
- Pause and resume.
- Restart.
- Manual seek.
- Seek directly to the end.
- Very short songs.
- Invalid or unknown duration.
- Audio loading failure.
- Audio playback interruption.
- Auto Dance completion.
- Repeated end events.
- Ending while a voice announcement is playing.

Pause must never trigger song completion.

Restart must create a fresh playable run.

Results must only be triggered once.

## PHASE 5 — RESULTS

When the song ends:

- Freeze final score.
- Preserve final accuracy.
- Preserve maximum combo.
- Preserve judgement counts.
- Preserve Auto Dance statistics.
- Stop gameplay updates.
- Transition to Results.
- Do not reset results before they are displayed.

## IMPORTANT DESIGN RULES

- Use actual audio duration, not an estimated duration from BPM or chart notes.
- Do not truncate valid songs.
- Do not let gameplay continue beyond the audio end.
- Avoid arbitrary fixed-duration timers.
- Handle HTMLAudioElement and Web Audio playback according to the actual implementation.
- Preserve existing chart timing and scoring rules.
- Do not change the Stage 6 voice architecture unnecessarily.
- Do not modify the Piper directory.
- Do not replace the dancer model or change Stage 7 rig architecture.

## TESTING

Create: qa/stage71-song-lifecycle-check.mjs

Test all edge cases and integration points.

Run all existing regression suites: stage44, stage45, stage46, stage5, stage6, stage61, stage62, stage63, stage7-rig.

Run: npm run build

Test actual playback in the browser, including a complete song reaching its end.

## FINAL REPORT

Report:

1. Root cause of infinite playback.
2. Actual audio duration source.
3. Files modified.
4. Playback lifecycle architecture.
5. Results transition behavior.
6. Tests and actual results.
7. Browser verification.
8. Remaining limitations.

Do not claim tests passed unless they were actually executed.

Một điều mình muốn nhấn mạnh: Đừng chỉ sửa để nhạc dừng. Phải sửa để nhạc, chart, dancer và gameplay cùng kết thúc trên một timeline. Đây mới là cách giải quyết triệt để, tránh sau này lại xuất hiện lỗi lệch thời gian.

Được. Và mình nghĩ đây là tính năng rất đáng làm tiếp theo. Thực tế, đây có thể là một trong những nâng cấp quan trọng nhất của game: mỗi bài nhạc sẽ có một Dance Map riêng, được tạo ra, lưu lại và sử dụng mỗi lần người chơi chọn bài đó.

Hiện tại vấn đề là game tự sinh vật thể theo thuật toán, người chơi không biết trước sắp xuất hiện vật thể gì, ở giây nào, lane nào. Mình muốn chuyển sang hệ thống Song Map System.

## 1. Ý tưởng: mỗi bài nhạc có một Map riêng

ChartForge

Song A — Neon

Map riêng gồm toàn bộ note, thời điểm xuất hiện, lane và loại vật thể.

Đã lưu map

Rhythm Journey – Review – Higher Plain Games

Song B — Energy

Có pattern, mật độ note, đoạn cao trào và drop riêng.

Đã lưu map

Project Heartbeat Demo on Steam

Song C — Dream

Map nhẹ nhàng, ít note, nhiều khoảng nghỉ.

Đã lưu map

Khi người chơi chọn bài nào, game tải đúng map của bài đó. Không tự sinh lại ngẫu nhiên mỗi lần chơi.

## 2. Xây dựng Map Editor

Đây là phần mình muốn ưu tiên nhất.

RhythmNator Editor | The Ultimate Rhythm Game Chart Editor

[rhythmnator.cloud](<https://rhythmnator.cloud/?utm_source=chatgpt.com>)

Editor cần có:

- Timeline chạy theo thời gian bài nhạc.
- Waveform hiển thị âm thanh.
- Beat grid theo BPM.
- 4 lane hoặc số lane hiện tại của game.
- Hiển thị tất cả vật thể đã đặt trên timeline.
- Play/Pause để nghe và xem map chạy.
- Click vào timeline để thêm vật thể.
- Kéo vật thể để đổi thời gian.
- Xóa, copy, paste vật thể.
- Zoom in/out để chỉnh chính xác.

### Các loại vật thể

| Vật thể | Ý nghĩa |
| --- | --- |
| TAP | Chạm một lần |
| HOLD | Giữ trong một khoảng thời gian |
| DOUBLE | Hai note đồng thời |
| SLIDE | Di chuyển giữa lane |
| BOMB | Vật thể cần tránh |
| SPECIAL | Vật thể đặc biệt nếu game hỗ trợ |

Không cần phát triển tất cả ngay lập tức. Trước tiên phải hỗ trợ chính xác những loại note gameplay hiện tại đang sử dụng.

## 3. Tự động tạo map từ nhạc

Mình đề xuất có thêm nút AUTO GENERATE.

(PDF) librosa: Audio and Music Signal Analysis in Python

[academia.edu](<https://www.academia.edu/18862537/librosa_Audio_and_Music_Signal_Analysis_in_Python?utm_source=chatgpt.com>)

Hệ thống phân tích:

1. BPM và beat.
2. Kick và bass.
3. Snare và clap.
4. Melody.
5. Chorus và drop.
6. Đoạn build-up và breakdown.
7. Năng lượng của từng đoạn nhạc.

Sau đó tạo note tương ứng.

Ví dụ:

| Thời gian | Âm nhạc | Map |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 0–10s | Intro nhẹ | Ít TAP |
| 10–25s | Beat bắt đầu | TAP đều |
| 25–35s | Build-up | TAP tăng dần |
| 35–50s | Drop | DOUBLE + TAP dày |
| 50–65s | Chorus | Pattern đa dạng |
| 65–80s | Outro | Giảm dần note |

Đây chỉ là ví dụ minh họa. Map thực tế phải dựa vào phân tích âm thanh chứ không được áp dụng một công thức cố định cho mọi bài.

## 4. Lưu map theo từng bài nhạc

Đây là yêu cầu quan trọng nhất.

Mình đề xuất cấu trúc:

text

```text
song-maps/
  song-001/
    metadata.json
    chart.json
  song-002/
    metadata.json
    chart.json
  song-003/
    metadata.json
    chart.json
```

Mỗi map lưu:

- Song ID và fingerprint của audio.
- BPM và offset.
- Tổng thời lượng.
- Danh sách note.
- Lane và loại vật thể.
- Thời điểm xuất hiện.
- Thời lượng HOLD.
- Difficulty.
- Phiên bản map.

Nếu người dùng chỉnh sửa map, game lưu lại. Lần sau chọn bài đó, map được tải lại chính xác.

Đặc biệt, nếu thay đổi file nhạc nhưng vẫn giữ tên file, game phải phát hiện fingerprint khác để tránh tải nhầm map.

## 5. Thứ tự triển khai mình đề xuất

1. Phase 1 — Song Map Data Architecture
   Thiết kế cấu trúc chart, Song ID, audio fingerprint, lưu và tải map. Chưa thay đổi gameplay.
   Nên làm đầu tiên
2. Phase 2 — Map Editor
   Timeline, waveform, lane, thêm/xóa/kéo note, preview.
3. Phase 3 — Auto Map Generator
   Phân tích BPM, beat, bass, kick, snare, drop và tạo map tự động.
4. Phase 4 — Map Preview
   Chọn bài nhạc, xem trước toàn bộ map và kiểm tra các note trước khi chơi.
5. Phase 5 — Gameplay Integration
   Gameplay đọc map đã lưu thay vì tự sinh vật thể liên tục.
6. Phase 6 — Difficulty System
   Easy, Normal, Hard, Expert có map riêng cho cùng một bài.

## 6. Có một điều mình đặc biệt muốn giữ

Không được làm hỏng các hệ thống đã xây dựng:

- Stage 6: Judgement và Scoring.
- Stage 6.2–6.3: Smart Announcer.
- Stage 7: Dancer và choreography.
- SongTimeline: thời lượng bài nhạc và kết thúc bài.
- Hệ thống Auto Dance.

Map mới chỉ quyết định vật thể nào xuất hiện, ở lane nào và thời điểm nào. Các hệ thống chấm điểm, âm thanh, dancer và kết thúc bài vẫn hoạt động như hiện tại.

## Một quyết định quan trọng trước khi bắt đầu

Mình nghiêng về phương án kết hợp:

Mình đề xuấtAuto Generate + Manual Edit

Máy tự tạo map ban đầu, người dùng có thể chỉnh sửa mọi note, sau đó lưu thành map chính thức.

GitHub - MadL1me/RhythmGE: Rhythm Game Editor - Tool for creating timestamps for rhythm game developers.

[github.com](<https://github.com/MadL1me/RhythmGE?utm_source=chatgpt.com>)

Người dùng không cần tự đặt từng note từ đầu, nhưng vẫn có quyền kiểm soát hoàn toàn map cuối cùng.

Mình muốn xác nhận một điểm: Bạn muốn map được tạo tự động ngay khi thêm bài nhạc vào thư viện, sau đó có thể mở Editor để chỉnh sửa, hay muốn phải bấm nút Generate thủ công?

Mình khuyên chọn cách đầu tiên, nhưng vẫn có nút Generate lại khi cần.



Đây là prompt Stage 8 mình đề xuất. Bạn đưa nguyên khối này cho AI coding agent đang làm việc trực tiếp với project.

Prompt yêu cầu inspect trước, triển khai theo phase, test thực tế và không phá các hệ thống Stage 6–7 đã hoàn thành.

# STAGE 8 — SONG MAP SYSTEM

## Automatic Beatmap Generation + Visual Map Editor + Persistent Song Maps

Implement a complete Song Map System for the existing rhythm game.

The goal is to make every song have its own persistent, editable beatmap. When a player selects a song, the game must load that song's saved map instead of generating random notes during gameplay.

IMPORTANT: Work directly with the existing project. Inspect the actual source code before making architectural decisions. Do not assume existing APIs, file paths, or data structures.

---

# 1\. CORE REQUIREMENTS

Every song must have its own independent beatmap.

The system must support:

- Automatic beatmap generation when a song is added to the library.
- Manual beatmap editing.
- Saving and loading beatmaps.
- Previewing the entire map before gameplay.
- Regenerating a map without destroying the previously saved version.
- Multiple difficulties per song.
- Deterministic gameplay: the same song and map must always produce the same note sequence.

The system must never silently regenerate an existing approved map.

# 2\. PHASE 0 — INSPECTION FIRST

Before changing anything, inspect:

1. Song library and song identification.
2. Audio loading and MusicPlayer.
3. SongTimeline and actual audio duration.
4. Existing chart generation.
5. NoteManager and note spawning.
6. Note types and lane definitions.
7. Gameplay judgement and scoring.
8. Auto Dance integration.
9. Song metadata and persistence.
10. Existing UI navigation and settings.
11. Existing QA suites.

Identify the exact integration points.

Produce an inspection report containing:

- Current architecture.
- Existing chart data format.
- Existing note types.
- Current random-generation logic.
- How gameplay receives and spawns notes.
- How songs are identified.
- Recommended integration architecture.

Do not modify files during this phase.

# 3\. PHASE 1 — SONG MAP DATA ARCHITECTURE

Create a persistent song-map architecture.

Suggested modules (adapt to actual project conventions):

- SongMap.js
- SongMapStore.js
- SongIdentity.js
- SongMapValidator.js
- SongMapLoader.js

Each map should contain:

{ "schemaVersion": 1, "songId": "stable-song-id", "audioFingerprint": "audio-hash", "duration": 120.5, "bpm": 128, "offset": 0, "difficulty": "normal", "revision": 1, "generated": true, "approved": false, "notes": \[\] }

Each note should contain:

{ "id": "unique-note-id", "time": 12.5, "lane": 1, "type": "tap", "duration": 0, "metadata": {} }

Adapt note fields to the actual existing gameplay contract.

Requirements:

- Stable song identification.
- Audio fingerprint validation.
- Multiple difficulties.
- Map versioning.
- Map validation.
- Duplicate-note detection.
- Invalid lane rejection.
- Invalid timing rejection.
- HOLD duration validation.
- Safe migration between schema versions.
- No silent data loss.
- Autosave with recovery from interrupted editing.

Use the project's existing persistence technology where appropriate.

Do not store huge audio files inside map JSON.

# 4\. PHASE 2 — AUTOMATIC BEATMAP GENERATOR

Create an automatic map-generation system.

Suggested modules:

- AudioAnalyzer.js
- BeatDetector.js
- SectionDetector.js
- BeatmapGenerator.js
- DifficultyGenerator.js

Analyze available audio data to identify:

- BPM.
- Beat positions.
- Kick and bass transients.
- Snare and clap accents.
- Energy changes.
- Chorus.
- Drop.
- Build-up.
- Breakdown.
- Outro.

Use actual audio analysis rather than assuming every song follows a fixed structure.

Generate note placements based on musical events.

Example:

| Musical event | Suggested map behaviour | | Intro | Sparse notes | | Verse | Consistent patterns | | Build-up | Gradually increasing density | | Drop | Strong accents and denser patterns | | Chorus | Varied musical patterns | | Breakdown | Reduced density | | Outro | Gradually decreasing density |

These are generation guidelines, not hardcoded rules.

Requirements:

- BPM-aware note placement.
- Beat-grid quantization.
- Configurable note density.
- Lane distribution.
- Pattern variation.
- Difficulty scaling.
- Avoid impossible patterns.
- Avoid accidental duplicate notes.
- Respect the actual audio duration.
- Never place notes beyond the end of the song.

IMPORTANT: Do not claim that generic energy detection can reliably identify musical sections. Use confidence scores and allow manual correction.

Generation must be deterministic using a stored seed.

Generating a new map must not overwrite an approved map without explicit confirmation.

# 5\. PHASE 3 — VISUAL MAP EDITOR

Build a dedicated Song Map Editor.

This is a major feature, not a simple debug panel.

The editor should contain:

A. Timeline:

- Horizontal song timeline.
- Time ruler.
- Beat grid.
- Bar markers.
- Waveform visualization.
- Zoom in/out.
- Horizontal scrolling.

B. Note lanes:

- Display the existing gameplay lanes.
- Show every note at its correct time.
- Different visual styles for each note type.
- Visually distinguish simultaneous notes.
- Display HOLD duration.

C. Editing:

- Click empty lane position to add a note.
- Drag notes to change their timing.
- Move notes between lanes.
- Select and delete notes.
- Copy and paste notes.
- Multi-select.
- Undo and redo.
- Snap to beat subdivisions.
- Edit HOLD duration.
- Keyboard shortcuts.

D. Playback:

- Play/Pause.
- Stop.
- Seek.
- Current-time indicator.
- Real audio playback synchronized with the timeline.
- Preview notes at their actual gameplay timing.

E. Save:

- Autosave.
- Manual save.
- Save revision.
- Undo/redo history.
- Dirty-state indicator.
- Recovery after reload.

The editor must remain responsive with large maps.

Avoid rebuilding the entire timeline DOM on every frame.

# 6\. PHASE 4 — SONG LIBRARY INTEGRATION

When a new song is added:

1. Identify the song.
2. Calculate its audio fingerprint.
3. Read its actual duration.
4. Check for an existing saved map.
5. If no map exists, generate an initial map.
6. Save the generated map as an editable draft.
7. Allow the user to open the editor.
8. Allow approval of the finished map.

When an existing song is selected:

- Load its saved map.
- Validate the audio fingerprint.
- Load the selected difficulty.
- Never generate a new random chart if a valid saved chart exists.

If the audio file changes, warn the user and offer regeneration.

# 7\. PHASE 5 — GAMEPLAY INTEGRATION

Replace runtime random note generation with the saved Song Map data.

Gameplay must:

- Read notes from the selected map.
- Spawn notes at their specified timestamps.
- Respect lane and note type.
- Respect HOLD duration.
- Preserve existing hit windows.
- Preserve scoring weights.
- Preserve combo and accuracy logic.
- Preserve Auto Dance.
- Preserve SongTimeline.
- Preserve song-end handling.

The chart must remain deterministic.

The same song, difficulty and map revision must produce identical gameplay every time.

Do not modify scoring rules or judgement timing to accommodate the new system.

If no valid map exists, use a clearly identified fallback generation flow instead of silently running an infinite or random chart.

# 8\. PHASE 6 — DIFFICULTY SYSTEM

Support independent maps for:

- EASY
- NORMAL
- HARD
- EXPERT

Each difficulty must have its own note sequence.

Difficulty should adjust:

- Note density.
- Pattern complexity.
- Simultaneous notes.
- Beat subdivisions.
- Special note frequency.

Do not simply multiply the number of notes.

All difficulty maps must remain musically coherent and physically playable.

# 9\. UI / UX

Integrate the new features into the existing visual design.

Add:

- Song Map Editor entry.
- MAP AVAILABLE status.
- MAP DRAFT status.
- MAP APPROVED status.
- Difficulty selector.
- Generate Map action.
- Regenerate Map action.

Display useful map statistics:

- Total notes.
- Notes per lane.
- Average notes per second.
- Simultaneous note count.
- HOLD count.
- Song duration.
- BPM.
- Difficulty.

Use the existing design system and responsive layout.

Do not introduce an unrelated UI redesign.

# 10\. PERFORMANCE

The system must work with long songs and large maps.

Requirements:

- Efficient timeline rendering.
- Virtualization or canvas rendering where appropriate.
- Avoid unnecessary allocations per frame.
- Cache waveform data.
- Cache audio analysis results.
- Avoid repeatedly decoding the same song.
- Keep gameplay note spawning efficient.
- Do not block the main thread with expensive audio analysis.

Use Web Workers if the existing architecture supports them and there is a meaningful benefit.

- Existing song library.

Do not rewrite unrelated modules.

# 13\. IMPLEMENTATION RULES

- Inspect before editing.
- Work in small, verifiable phases.
- Do not fabricate files, assets, screenshots or test results.
- Do not download external character or audio assets.
- Do not change the existing music files.
- Do not break the current gameplay architecture.
- Preserve backward compatibility where practical.
- Do not silently overwrite user-created maps.
- Do not commit changes unless explicitly requested.

Start with Phase 0 inspection only.

After the inspection report, proceed to Phase 1 and implement incrementally.

Do not attempt to implement all six phases in one uncontrolled change.

The final objective:

EVERY SONG HAS ITS OWN SAVED MAP. THE PLAYER CAN SEE EXACTLY WHICH OBJECTS WILL APPEAR AND WHEN. THE MAP CAN BE AUTOMATICALLY GENERATED, MANUALLY EDITED, SAVED AND REUSED. GAMEPLAY ALWAYS USES THE APPROVED MAP FOR THAT SONG.

Lưu ý: Mình cố tình yêu cầu AI bắt đầu bằng Phase 0 inspection, sau đó mới triển khai. Đừng để nó sửa cả generator, editor và gameplay cùng lúc — rất dễ phá những hệ thống đã ổn định.

Mục tiêu cuối cùng là: mỗi bài nhạc có một bản đồ riêng, biết trước từng vật thể xuất hiện ở đâu và lúc nào, và chơi lại bao nhiêu lần cũng không thay đổi.



Phase 0: inspecting the real chart pipeline, note contract, song identity, and persistence before proposing anything.

Inspection complete. Writing the Phase 1 data architecture now, starting with stable song identity and the map schema.






















## Phase 0 — Inspection report

*(No existing file was modified during this phase; the inspection read the source as it is.)*

### Current architecture

```
Game (src/core/Game.js)                        ← owns the loop + state machine
 ├─ MusicPlayer (src/music/MusicPlayer.js)      one transport: <audio> element OR AudioBuffer
 ├─ SongTimeline (src/game/SongTimeline.js)     authoritative clock + single end signal (Stage 7.1)
 ├─ BeatDetector / MusicStructure               audio analysis (tempo, kicks/snares/hats, curves, sections)
 ├─ BeatMapGenerator (src/audio/BeatMapGenerator.js)  chart generation — the thing a map system replaces
 ├─ Gameplay (src/game/Gameplay.js)             judgement + scoring (play mode / auto mode)
 │   └─ NoteManager (src/objects/NoteManager.js) spawning, judging, misses
 ├─ UIManager (src/ui/UIManager.js)             screens Map + _activate(name), data-action delegation
 └─ MusicLibrary (src/music/MusicLibrary.js)    demo + local + Jamendo tracks
```

Song start path: `Game.startSong()` → `_prepare()` → `MusicLibrary.createDemoBuffer()` / decode → `BeatDetector.analyze()` → `BeatMapGenerator.generate()` → `gameplay.start({ chart })` → `notes.setChart()`. The loop then calls `gameplay.update(songTime, dt)` with `songTime` from `SongTimeline`, which reads `MusicPlayer.getTime()`. **Nothing about notes is persisted**: every play regenerates the chart from the seed.

### Existing chart data format

`BeatMapGenerator.generate()` returns:

```js
{ bpm, difficulty, lanes, notes: [{ time, lane, type, strength }],
  duration, trackId, seedKey, chartVersion }        // CHART_VERSION = 2
```

Note times are rounded to 1 ms, notes earlier than 0.35 s are dropped, and the list is sorted by time.

### Existing note types and lanes

- Types: `tap` and `obstacle` only (`NOTE_TYPES`, BeatMapGenerator:191). An obstacle is a bomb: it damages health and is never a successful hit.
- Lanes: **4**, from `src/config.js` (`LANE_COUNT`, `LANE_X`, `KEY_LANES`).
- `strength` (0..1) feeds scoring visuals and Auto Dance ratings.
- **There is no hold note and no note id.** `NoteManager` never reads `note.duration`, and nothing keys notes individually. A map schema can carry `id`/`duration`, but this build cannot play holds.

### Current generation logic

- Seeded, versioned: `createSeededRandom()` (xmur3 + mulberry32) with `chartSeedKey = `${trackId}::${difficulty}::v${CHART_VERSION}``. Deterministic per track+difficulty+version — the property the map system must preserve.
- `generate()` uses `fromAnalysis()` when the analysis has ≥8 kick transients (beat-grid quantization, kick/hat/fill placement, hazard spacing 12–18 s, density governor against `TARGET_NPS`), otherwise `generateProcedural()` on the tempo grid.
- `DIFFICULTIES` (easy/medium/hard/expert — labels EASY/NORMAL/HARD/EXPERT) carry `hitWindow`, `perfectWindow`, `minGap`, `chordGap`, `density`, `grid`, `beatSlots`, `fillChance`, `maxNps`, hazard gaps, note speed. Judgement windows live here and must stay untouched.

### How gameplay receives and spawns notes

`Gameplay.start({chart})` → `notes.setChart(chart, DIFFICULTIES[difficulty])` sorts a copy of `chart.notes` and computes `leadTime` from speed; `notes.update(songTime, dt)` spawns while `note.time - songTime <= leadTime`, `judgeLane(lane, songTime, hitWindow)` consumes at the judged line, `collectMisses()` after the window, `Gameplay._autoJudge()` consumes on the chart clock in Auto Dance. Obstacles use the same list. So a chart is fully described by its notes array — the seam a saved map plugs into.

### How songs are identified (the one thing that must change)

| Provider | Current id | Stable? |
| --- | --- | --- |
| demo | `demo:neon-pulse` | ✅ |
| jamendo | Jamendo track id | ✅ |
| local | `local:<name>:<size>:<Date.now()>:<n>` (LocalProvider:63) | ❌ new id every session |

Worse, **local uploads are not persisted at all** (LocalProvider keeps files in memory; MusicDiscovery stores nothing), so after a reload the library is empty. A map for a local file can therefore only be found again by *content*, which is exactly what the new fingerprint does: re-adding the same file re-identifies it and its map comes back.

### Persistence today

`localStorage`, one versioned key per concern, every access wrapped so a blocked/full store degrades: `neonpulse:settings:v1`, `neon-pulse:scores:v1` (ScoreStore — keyed track + difficulty + **chartVersion**), `neon-pulse:library:v1`, `neon-pulse:mode`. Analysis is cached in `Game.analysisCache` **in memory only** (3 entries, keyed by track + tempo version).

### Recommended integration architecture (adopted)

1. **Identity** — content fingerprint for local songs, provider id for the rest; never `Date.now()`.
2. **`src/game/map/`** — schema, validator, store, loader. Loader returns a *status*, never silently regenerates.
3. **Generation** stays `BeatMapGenerator`-driven but runs through a new generator layer that emits maps (Phase 2), so existing chart code and its tests remain valid as the fallback.
4. **Gameplay** switches to `songMapLoader.resolve()` in `_prepare()` with an explicit `fallback` flag (Phase 5) — `Gameplay`/`NoteManager`/`Scoring` untouched.
5. **UI** — library badges + difficulty screen actions + a dedicated editor screen using the existing `_activate()` screen registry.

Two decisions I flagged rather than silently taking: score records are keyed by `chartVersion`, so **editing a map does not currently create a new score record** (needs `mapRevision` in the key when gameplay switches over — Phase 5), and **holds are schema-valid but unplayable** in this build (loader returns `unplayable` instead of dropping notes).

## Phase 1 — implemented

| Module | Responsibility |
| --- | --- |
| `src/game/map/SongIdentity.js` | `fingerprintBuffer` (48 windows × 96 samples, quantized → 64-bit-ish hash + duration), `fingerprintBytes` (encoded files, no decode), `stableSongIdFor` (`local:<fingerprint>`, `<provider>:<id>`), `describeSongIdentity`, `fingerprintMatches` |
| `src/game/map/SongMap.js` | schema v1, `createSongMap`, `createNote`, deterministic content-based note ids, `normalizeNotes`, `mapSummary` (per-lane, chords, holds, NPS, strictest gap), `MAP_DIFFICULTIES`, `songMapKey` |
| `src/game/map/SongMapValidator.js` | `validateSongMap` → `{ valid, playable, errors, warnings, notes, dropCount }`; rejects bad schema/id/difficulty/revision/duration, bad lanes/types/times, duplicates, hold overflow/short holds; warns on impossible lane gaps, unsorted data, missing BPM, unsupported holds. `repairSongMap` drops-and-reports, never silently |
| `src/game/map/SongMapStore.js` | localStorage + memory fallback, per song/difficulty keys, index for cheap status, **refuses to overwrite an approved map**, **refuses generated over hand-edited**, refuses stale revisions, keeps the previous version for rollback, draft autosave with time-based recovery, quota-safe, `rebuildIndex` |
| `src/game/map/SongMapLoader.js` | `migrateSongMap` (v0 → v1, ids re-derived, unknown fields preserved in `metadata.legacy`, legacy maps treated as approved), `songMapToChart` (bridge to the exact gameplay contract), `resolve()` returning `ready` / `missing` / `invalid` / `unplayable` / `fingerprint-mismatch`, `resolveForTrack()` |
| `qa/stage8-song-map-check.mjs` | **new suite, 136 checks** |

**Guarantees enforced by code, not convention:** generation cannot replace an approved or hand-edited map without `force`; a map whose audio changed is `fingerprint-mismatch` instead of being played against the wrong song; a map with holds is `unplayable` rather than silently stripped; repair always reports what it dropped; a lost index is rebuildable from the maps themselves.

## Tests — actually run

`qa/stage8-song-map-check.mjs`: **136/136 passed** — identity (determinism, changed audio, session-stable local ids, remote/demo ids, match/mismatch), schema (ids deterministic + unique, sorting, stats), validation (every rejection and warning path, repair/drop counting, hold rules, last valid lane), persistence (round-trip, per-difficulty isolation, approval, overwrite refusal, force, rollback, generated-over-edited refusal, stale revision, removal, index rebuild), autosave/recovery (incl. older-draft rejection and song scoping), quota fallback, memory-only store, migration (v0 → v1, field preservation, no-op), loader (ready/missing/invalid/unplayable/mismatch, chart contract, determinism, legacy on disk, track path).

Long-map performance, 8,000 notes / 380 s: create **23 ms**, validate **17 ms**, store **15 ms**, resolve **17 ms**, total **73 ms**.

Regression: `stage5` 77/77, `stage36` ✓, `stage42` ✓, `stage43` ✓, `stage44` ✓, `stage45` 138/138, `stage46` 133/133, `stage6-judgement` 89/89, `stage6-voice` 89/89, `stage61` 60/60, `stage62` 68/68, `stage63` 101/101, `stage7-rig` 90/90, `stage71-song-lifecycle` 92/92. `npm run build` ✓ (1,171.16 kB — unchanged, since nothing imports the new modules yet).

## Not done yet (deliberately, by phase)

Phase 2 generator, Phase 3 editor UI, Phase 4 library wiring, Phase 5 gameplay switch-over, Phase 6 per-difficulty map sets, and the UI status badges. The map system is currently a tested library with zero behavioural risk: no app code imports it, so gameplay still generates charts exactly as before. Browser verification of real-song maps only becomes meaningful once Phase 5 connects the loader — I will do it then rather than claim it now.


11111111111111

Phase 0 và Phase 1 làm khá đúng hướng. Đặc biệt, có 3 quyết định kiến trúc rất tốt:

1. Local song dùng audio fingerprint thay vì tên file hoặc `Date.now()` — giúp nhận diện lại bài nhạc sau khi reload.
2. Không ghi đè map đã chỉnh sửa hoặc APPROVED — bảo vệ dữ liệu người dùng.
3. Gameplay chưa bị thay đổi — Phase 1 chỉ thêm thư viện dữ liệu, chưa tác động pipeline hiện tại.

Tuy nhiên, mình muốn xử lý 3 điểm trước khi đi tiếp:

- Audio fingerprint hiện tại lấy mẫu 48 cửa sổ × 96 samples. Cần kiểm tra khả năng nhận diện cùng một file khi encode lại hoặc thay đổi metadata. Không nên coi đây là nhận diện âm thanh hoàn hảo.
- `localStorage` có giới hạn dung lượng. Với map lớn và nhiều bài nhạc, nên cân nhắc IndexedDB làm nơi lưu chính.
- Score record phải gắn với `mapRevision`, tránh chỉnh map rồi vẫn dùng thành tích của map cũ.

## Prompt tiếp theo: Stage 8.2

Mình khuyên triển khai Generator trước Editor, vì Editor cần có dữ liệu map thực tế để hiển thị và chỉnh sửa.

Writing

# STAGE 8.2 — AUTOMATIC BEATMAP GENERATOR

Phase 1 Song Map Architecture is complete and verified.

Now implement Phase 2: automatic beatmap generation.

## 1\. First inspect

Read the actual implementations of:

- BeatMapGenerator.js
- BeatDetector
- MusicStructure
- SongMap.js
- SongMapValidator.js
- SongMapStore.js
- SongMapLoader.js
- Existing difficulty profiles
- Audio analysis cache

Do not rewrite existing generation logic unnecessarily.

## 2\. Create the generator layer

Suggested modules:

- src/game/map/AudioAnalyzer.js
- src/game/map/BeatDetector.js (reuse existing detector where possible)
- src/game/map/SectionDetector.js
- src/game/map/BeatmapGenerator.js
- src/game/map/DifficultyGenerator.js

The new generator must use the existing BeatMapGenerator as its foundation.

Preserve existing note contracts:

- 4 lanes.
- tap.
- obstacle.
- strength.
- Existing timing and judgement windows.

Do not implement HOLD notes yet because the current gameplay cannot play them.

## 3\. Audio analysis

Extract and normalize:

- BPM and beat positions.
- Kick transients.
- Bass energy.
- Snare and clap.
- High-frequency accents.
- Energy curve.
- Section boundaries.
- Drop candidates.
- Build-up candidates.
- Breakdown candidates.

Reuse existing analysis results rather than decoding and analyzing the same song multiple times.

Include confidence scores.

Do not assume every high-energy moment is a drop.

## 4\. Note generation

Generate a complete map from the song's actual duration.

Rules:

- Place notes on musically valid beat subdivisions.
- Align strong notes with strong musical events.
- Use varied lane patterns.
- Avoid repetitive lane sequences.
- Avoid impossible simultaneous combinations.
- Respect minimum note spacing.
- Respect difficulty density limits.
- Keep obstacle frequency controlled.
- Avoid notes in unsuitable intro/outro regions.
- Never exceed the actual audio duration.

Use deterministic seeded generation.

The same audio fingerprint, difficulty, generator version and seed must produce identical notes.

## 5\. Difficulty generation

Generate independent maps for:

EASY:

- Low density.
- Mostly quarter-beat or beat-aligned taps.
- Simple patterns.

NORMAL:

- Moderate density.
- More rhythmic variation.
- Occasional simultaneous notes if supported.

HARD:

- Higher density.
- More subdivisions.
- More complex lane patterns.

EXPERT:

- High density.
- Complex but physically playable patterns.
- More syncopation.

Difficulty must change pattern complexity, not just note count.

Keep the existing scoring and judgement windows unchanged.

## 6\. Generation preview

Produce a generation report:

- Song duration.
- Detected BPM.
- BPM confidence.
- Detected sections.
- Total notes.
- Notes per lane.
- Notes per second.
- Obstacle count.
- Simultaneous note count.
- Difficulty.
- Generator version.
- Validation warnings.

Mark generated maps as DRAFT, never automatically APPROVED.

## 7\. Persistence

Integrate with SongMapStore.

Rules:

- Never overwrite an approved map.
- Never overwrite a hand-edited map.
- Regeneration must create a new draft or revision.
- Preserve rollback.
- Save the random seed and generator version.
- Validate every generated map before saving.

## 8\. QA

Create qa/stage82-generator-check.mjs.

Test:

- Deterministic generation.
- Same song produces identical output.
- Different songs produce different maps.
- BPM quantization.
- Beat alignment.
- Difficulty progression.
- Lane distribution.
- Note density.
- Obstacle spacing.
- Actual duration boundaries.
- Invalid analysis fallback.
- Short songs.
- Long songs.
- Missing BPM.
- Low-confidence analysis.
- Approved map protection.
- Hand-edited map protection.
- Persistence and rollback.
- Existing chart-generator regression.

Run all previous regression suites and npm run build.

Use real audio samples from the existing project in addition to synthetic tests.

Report actual measurements.

## 9\. Scope restrictions

Do not implement:

- Map Editor UI.
- Gameplay integration.
- New note types.
- HOLD mechanics.
- Dancer changes.
- Scoring changes.
- Voice changes.

Those belong to later phases.

Start with inspection, then implement incrementally.

Thứ tự sau đó: Stage 8.2 Generator → Stage 8.3 Map Editor → Stage 8.4 Song Library → Stage 8.5 Gameplay Integration.

Như vậy sẽ tránh tình trạng có Editor đẹp nhưng dữ liệu map bên dưới chưa đáng tin cậy.


Phase 2. Inspecting the existing generator internals, structure API, and available real audio before writing the generator layer.


































## What was built

Phase 2 adds four modules on top of the existing chart generator and the Phase 1 map architecture. No existing generation logic, note contract, judgement window or scoring rule was changed.

| Module | Role |
| --- | --- |
| `src/game/map/AudioAnalyzer.js` | Normalises the real `BeatDetector` output (+ `MusicStructure` sections) into a generation-shaped analysis with confidence scores; LRU cache keyed by audio fingerprint so a song is analysed once for all four difficulties and every regeneration; `beatGrid` for quantization. **No duplicate detector module** — the existing one is reused, as the brief allowed. |
| `src/game/map/SectionDetector.js` | Contiguous section coverage, verse-vs-chorus heuristic (flagged), drop candidates requiring a sustained peak **and** a margin over neighbours, build-up candidates requiring rising energy, breakdowns, full confidence per candidate. |
| `src/game/map/DifficultyGenerator.js` | Per-tier profiles: section keep-chances (with gradually fading outro), lane families, repeat tolerance, syncopation, hazard scale, and an ordered `complexity` value. Built on `DIFFICULTIES` — speed, hit windows, `minGap`, `maxNps` are read, never redefined. |
| `src/game/map/BeatmapGenerator.js` | Orchestrator: `generateSongMap`, `generateDifficultySet`, `generationSeed`, `saveGeneratedMap`, `regenerateSongMap` + the generation report. Pipeline: **base chart (existing generator) → section shaping → tier lane re-patterning → hazard control → density ceiling → validate → draft map**. |

Determinism is structural: the seed is `map:<fingerprint>::<difficulty>::g<version>` with separate RNG streams per phase (`:shape`, `:lanes`), so identical audio + difficulty + version + seed yields byte-identical notes. Times are never touched by the shaping passes — only which notes survive and which lane they sit on — so beat quantization and musical alignment survive by construction.

## Measurements (real values from the runs)

**Real demo mix** (`demo:neon-pulse`, 68.7 s, rendered in the browser, analysed by the real detector): analysis **811 ms**, BPM **128** @ confidence **0.883**, overall **0.866**, 238 kicks. Sections: intro 0–10.75 (0.81), verse 10.75–56 (0.48, ambiguous), drop 56–64.5 (0.81), outro 64.5–68.7 (0.81); **1 drop candidate**, 0 build-ups (the demo has no build-up section). Four maps in **20–23 ms**:

| Tier | Notes | NPS | Hazards | Per lane |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| easy | 32 | 0.47 | 2 | 2 / 15 / 13 / 2 |
| normal | 111 | 1.62 | 2 | 31 / 23 / 33 / 24 |
| hard | 146 | 2.13 | 4 | 25 / 48 / 48 / 25 |
| expert | 192 | 2.79 | 3 | 50 / 48 / 49 / 45 |

Deterministic across runs, every note inside the audio, all maps `status: ok`.

**Real project audio**: 40 voice WAVs from `public/assets/audio/voice/en-female-en-us-amy-medium` decoded and concatenated to **42.3 s**, analysed by the real `BeatDetector` → BPM 175 @0.859, analysis confidence 0.856, notes 26 / 67 / 112 / 144, all valid and deterministic.

**Long song**: 400 s expert → **15–34 ms**, inside the density ceiling. **Structured fixture** (96 s, declared intro/verse/build/drop/breakdown/outro): intro 0.10 NPS vs drop 0.79 NPS, breakdown sparse, outro fading; complexity **easy 16 < normal 31 < hard 54 < expert 65**.

## Two real bugs found while verifying

1. A **malformed analysis** (`{duration, bpm, kicks: 5}`) crashed inside `MusicStructure.analyze` (`(analysis.kicks || []).map`). Now guarded: no structure → warning `malformed-analysis` / `no-analysis`, tempo-grid fallback, still a valid draft.
2. `chartAnalysisFor` forwarded any `raw` payload to the existing generator, which re-crashed on the same shape. Now only a genuine detector output (array `kicks`) is forwarded; otherwise the normalised analysis is handed over.

## Tests — actually executed

`qa/stage82-generator-check.mjs`: **128/128 passed** — determinism (same inputs, re-seeded, different song/difficulty), quantization (every note on a 16th within 2 ms), strong notes on beats, no duplicate lane+time, per-tier spacing, section shaping (intro < verse/drop, breakdown < drop, outro fade), difficulty progression (count + strict complexity ordering + distinct lane vocabularies + density ceilings), lane coverage, hazard spacing/rarity/no-intro/clash-free, edge cases (no analysis, malformed, thin transients, missing BPM, no duration, 6 s, 2 s, low confidence, 400 s), detector layer (coverage, contiguity, drop margin, flat song → no candidate, fallback sections, beat grid, cache hit and bound), persistence (draft save, approval, **approved-map refusal**, **hand-edited refusal**, forced regeneration, revision increment, rollback, invalid map never stored, seed + version retained), real audio, and chart-generator regression (deterministic, `chartVersion` 2, contract intact).

Regression: `stage5` 77/77, `stage36` ✓, `stage42` ✓, `stage43` ✓, `stage44` ✓, `stage45` 138/138, `stage46` 133/133, `stage6-judgement` 89/89, `stage6-voice` 89/89, `stage61` 60/60, `stage62` 68/68, `stage63` 101/101, `stage7-rig` 90/90, `stage71-song-lifecycle` 92/92, `stage8-song-map` 136/136. `npm run build` ✓ (1,171.16 kB — unchanged, since nothing imports the map modules yet; gameplay integration is Phase 5).

## Limitations (stated, not hidden)

- Section types come from `MusicStructure`, so the demo's 45 s "verse" is a low-confidence block and no build-up was detected — the generator shapes what the detector reports and flags ambiguity rather than inventing structure.
- Chorus is a **heuristic** role (energy + position), always flagged; a labelled drop without a real energy margin is reported as `weak-drop` and is **not** boosted.
- Hazard counts are spacing-driven, not strictly tier-ordered (hard 4 vs expert 3 on the demo).
- Density differences at the top end are transient-limited by the source music (hard vs expert gap is modest on the sparse demo).
- Holds remain unimplemented by design; every generated map has `duration: 0`.
- Generated maps are **drafts only**; nothing auto-approves.
- Nothing consumes these maps in gameplay yet, and no editor UI exists — that is Phase 3/5, and I have not claimed any of it.



111111111
Phase 2 đã hoàn thành theo báo cáo. Đây là bước quan trọng: game đã có khả năng phân tích nhạc và tạo map riêng cho từng bài, với 4 mức độ khó.

Tuy nhiên, hiện tại vẫn còn một điểm rất quan trọng:

Map đã được tạo nhưng chưa được đưa vào gameplay. Vì vậy, người chơi vẫn chưa thực sự nhìn thấy những vật thể được bố trí theo map riêng của bài nhạc.

Mình đề xuất bước tiếp theo là Phase 3 — Map Editor, trước khi tích hợp gameplay.

## Phase 3 — Xây dựng Map Editor

Mục tiêu: người dùng có thể xem, chỉnh sửa và lưu map của từng bài nhạc.

### 1\. Giao diện Map Editor

Quaver on Steam

GitHub - MadL1me/RhythmGE: Rhythm Game Editor - Tool for creating timestamps for rhythm game developers.

Các thành phần:

- Waveform của bài nhạc.
- Timeline hiển thị thời gian và BPM.
- 4 lane tương ứng gameplay.
- Các note và obstacle được đặt trên timeline.
- Zoom in/out, kéo timeline, tua nhạc.
- Preview map theo thời gian thực.

### 2\. Chỉnh sửa map

- Click để thêm note.
- Kéo note để thay đổi thời gian.
- Đổi lane.
- Chuyển Tap thành Obstacle.
- Xóa note.
- Undo / Redo.
- Nghe nhạc tại vị trí đang chỉnh sửa.

### 3\. Quản lý map

Mỗi bài nhạc có 4 map độc lập:

| Difficulty | Map |
| --- | --- |
| Easy | Riêng |
| Normal | Riêng |
| Hard | Riêng |
| Expert | Riêng |

- Save Draft.
- Approve Map.
- Regenerate Map.
- Restore Previous Version.
- Không tự động ghi đè map đã chỉnh sửa.

### 4\. Tiêu chí nghiệm thu

- Mở lại bài nhạc vẫn thấy map đã lưu.
- Chỉnh sửa không làm thay đổi map của difficulty khác.
- Timeline khớp chính xác với audio.
- Preview không làm ảnh hưởng gameplay.
- Undo/Redo hoạt động chính xác.
- Build và toàn bộ regression suite đều xanh.

Sau Phase 3: Phase 4 kết nối thư viện nhạc và hiển thị trạng thái map; Phase 5 mới chuyển gameplay sang sử dụng map đã lưu.

Như vậy sẽ tránh tình trạng game tự sinh vật thể mà người chơi không biết chúng xuất hiện ở đâu, và mỗi bài nhạc sẽ có một bản đồ riêng có thể xem trước, chỉnh sửa và lưu lại.



Đây là prompt triển khai Stage 8 — Phase 3: Map Editor. Prompt yêu cầu AI kiểm tra code hiện tại trước, sau đó xây dựng editor thực sự kết nối với kiến trúc Phase 1 và Phase 2, không phá gameplay đang chạy.

# STAGE 8 — PHASE 3: SONG MAP EDITOR

Implement Phase 3 of the Song Map system.

## 1\. Main objective

- Easy
- Normal

- Expert

## Architecture and integration points

| Integration point | How the editor uses it |
| --- | --- |
| Screen registration (`src/ui/templates.js`, `data-screen`) | The editor is a full-screen overlay mounted from `main.js`, the same pattern as `Studio` — the existing screens and `UIManager._activate` are untouched. Entry point: a **MAP EDITOR** button added to the difficulty screen's foot. |
| `UIManager` event delegation | One new `data-action="open-map-editor"` case emits `EVENTS.MAP_EDITOR_OPEN` with the current track; closing calls `ui.showDifficulty(track)` so you land back where you came from. |
| `SongMap` / `SongMapValidator` | Notes are built with `createNote`, maps with `createSongMap`, saved copies validated by `validateSongMap`. Nothing writes to the note contract; the validator's issues are surfaced verbatim. |
| `SongMapStore` | Save, approve, restore and regeneration all go through it, so revision history, `:prev` rollback, index and the approved/edited protection rules stay in one place. |
| `SongMapLoader` / `SongIdentity` | Song id and audio fingerprint come from `describeSongIdentity` (real decoded buffer), and the editor reports a fingerprint mismatch rather than ignoring it. |
| `BeatmapGenerator` (Phase 2) | Seeding and REGENERATE call `generateSongMap`; the generation report (seed, version, sections, drop candidates, removed counts, warnings) is shown in the sidebar. |
| Audio | A dedicated `MapPreviewController` with its own `AudioBufferSourceNode` → gain → destination. The gameplay `MusicPlayer` is never touched; the game is suspended while the editor is open, exactly like the studio. |
| Waveform | `peaksFromBuffer` over the real decoded buffer; no DSP dependency added. |

**New files** — `src/game/map/editor/`: `MapEditorController.js`, `MapEditorState.js`, `MapEditorHistory.js`, `MapEditorCommands.js`, `MapTimeline.js`, `MapPreviewController.js`, `MapEditorRenderer.js`, `MapEditorDrafts.js`; screen shell `src/game/map/MapEditor.js`; markup `src/ui/mapEditorMarkup.js`; styles `src/styles/mapEditor.css`; suite `qa/stage83-map-editor-check.mjs`.
**Modified** — `src/core/EventBus.js` (two events), `src/ui/templates.js` (one button), `src/ui/UIManager.js` (one case), `src/main.js` (mount + expose), `src/styles/main.css` (one rule).
No scoring, judgement, note spawning, voice, choreography, rig or generator file was changed.

## Features implemented

**Header/timeline** — title, artist, duration, BPM + confidence, analysis confidence, revision, status chip, four difficulty tabs. Canvas with waveform, time ruler with bar numbers, beat/bar grid, section bands (heuristic roles rendered differently and labelled `HEUR`), four lanes, playhead, hover time readout. Zoom 4–640 px/s, horizontal scroll, wheel = scroll, Ctrl/⌘+wheel = zoom at cursor, FIT = whole song.

**Editing** — click empty lane adds a snapped note; drag moves (time + lane, snapped, coalesced into one undo step); marquee multi-select; Shift/Ctrl/⌘ click toggles selection; Delete, Copy/Paste at playhead, type change/toggle, lane move, arrow-key nudges, Ctrl+Z / Ctrl+Shift+Z / Ctrl+Y, Ctrl+A/C/V, snap OFF/BAR/1/1/1/2/1/4/1/8, per-difficulty undo stacks (100 steps). Duplicates, out-of-song times, bad lanes and unsupported types are refused with a message and never silently repaired.

**Preview** — play/pause/stop/seek, 0.5×/0.75×/1× (0.5–2× API), mm:ss.mmm readout of position and total; the playhead is driven by the preview clock. Speed changes read the buffer faster/slower and never touch note timestamps.

**Difficulties** — independent map per tier; switching autosaves the outgoing working copy as a per-difficulty draft, loads stored/recovered/generated content, and keeps that tier's undo history. Note count, NPS, obstacles, per-lane bars and tightest lane gap are shown for the active tier.

**Versions** — SAVE DRAFT (store write, revision bump only when edited), APPROVE (validates, refuses while invalid), REGENERATE (selected tier only, two-stage confirmation for approved/hand-edited maps, keeps the replaced revision), RESTORE PREVIOUS, RECOVER DRAFT, DISCARD DRAFT, autosave after every edit, recovery after reload, unsaved-changes modal on close and on difficulty switch.

**Validation/UX** — verdict line (invalid / playable / unplayable-for-holds), issue list with clickable entries that select the offending note and seek to it, section list, generation report, notices for recovered drafts, fingerprint mismatch and approved-lock.

## Test results — executed

`qa/stage83-map-editor-check.mjs`: **168/168 passed** (12 sections: commands, history, timeline, state, preview, drafts, controller, protection, autosave, edge, real audio, persistence). Run three times consecutively, all green; artifact at `qa-artifacts/stage83-map-editor-check.json`.

## Browser verification — what actually happened

Real demo mix, 68.7 s, live dev server at 1440×900:
- Opened from the difficulty screen; seeded NORMAL map: **99 notes, rev 1 · GENERATED**, 4 sections (intro 0–10.8 @81%, verse 10.8–56 @43%, drop 56–64.5 @81%, outro 64.5–68.7 @81%), "Valid and playable".
- Canvas 1096×645, lane height 133 px, 4 lanes drawn.
- Click lane → 99→100 notes; marquee selected 2; ruler click scrubbed the playhead to 2.0 s.
- Drag: note (1.172 s, L3) → (1.641 s, L2), snapped to the 128 BPM grid; arrow nudge → 2.11 s, ArrowUp → lane 1; `T` toggled tap→obstacle; Ctrl+C / Ctrl+V pasted at 30 s; Delete removed it.
- Preview: play advanced 32.679 → 33.599 s in 0.9 s wall; **0.5× advanced exactly 0.5 s in 1 s**; note timestamps identical before/after; pause held its position; readout `0.5×`.
- HARD tab → confirm → 145 notes rev 1, medium autosaved as a draft (100 notes); switching back restored the 100-note working copy as **RECOVERED DRAFT** with a notice.
- SAVE DRAFT → rev 3, toast "Saved revision 3 of MEDIUM", status DRAFT, draft slot cleared. Page reload → reopened at **100 notes, rev 3, clean**, obstacle edit still present.
- APPROVE → APPROVED, stored flag true, button disabled, notice about the lock. REGENERATE → gate 1 ("REGENERATE MAP?") → gate 2 ("APPROVED MAP — …confirm to continue") → **cancel left the approved map and its 100 notes byte-identical**. Confirming instead produced rev 4 (not approved) with rev 3 kept as `:prev`; RESTORE PREVIOUS brought the prior revision back.
- Report panel: `neon-map-gen-1`, seed, 99 notes · 1.44 NPS · 3 obstacles, complexity 31, 4 sections, 1 drop, removed "46 taps / 1 hazards", warning `ambiguous-sections`.
- Ctrl+wheel zoom 90→106.2 px/s, wheel scrolled 2.19 s, FIT → 14.7 px/s at scroll 0.
- Validation: a note 20 ms after another on the same lane produced `lane-gap`, verdict "Valid and playable · 1 warning"; clicking the issue selected the note and sought to 10.794 s.
- Close with unsaved edits → "UNSAVED CHANGES" modal; cancel kept the editor open; confirm closed to the difficulty screen, unsuspended the game, restored the UI, wrote the recovery draft and disposed the controller.
- Console after the fixes: no errors, no warnings from the editor; all module/asset requests 200, no failed requests.

Bugs found and fixed during that verification: `[object Object]` in the generation report (complexity and removed counts are data objects), issue entries without a timestamp not seeking, `setPointerCapture` throwing on a released pointer, undo stacks dropped when a draft round-tripped through id normalisation, hold durations being flattened to taps in the working copy, and the O(n²) collision scan.

## Known limitations

- **Holds**: cannot be created in the UI. Existing hold notes are preserved (not converted) and the map is reported unplayable, unchanged from Phase 2.
- **Approved maps are locked**: save is refused on an approved map; there is no "unapprove" action in this phase (restore/regenerate with confirmation are the exits).
- **Rollback depth**: one previous revision per map and one editor draft per difficulty, matching the Phase 1 store contract.
- **Song switching** happens outside the editor (the difficulty screen is the entry point); there is no in-editor song picker.
- **Remote tracks** whose audio is blocked by CORS open with no waveform and no preview, but remain editable on the tempo grid (demo and local files decode fully).
- **No drag auto-scroll** past the viewport edge, and no pinch-zoom; use wheel/±/FIT.
- **Verification method**: canvas interactions were driven with real pointer/keyboard events inside the running page, not by a human hand, and I did not audit the layout at every breakpoint. `qa/stage35-framing-check.mjs` fails on narrow phone viewports — it is a pre-existing failure in untouched framing code (no diff in `StageFraming.js`/`config.js`), unrelated to this phase and not in the required list.
- Nothing was committed.



1111111111111111

Phase 3 hoàn thành khá tốt. Đặc biệt, đã xác nhận được các chức năng quan trọng bằng browser thật: chỉnh note, nghe preview, lưu map, reload vẫn còn, approve và rollback.

Tiếp theo nên làm Phase 4 — Song Library Integration.

Mục tiêu là biến Map Editor thành một phần tự nhiên của thư viện nhạc, để người dùng không phải tự tìm đường vào editor.

Thứ tự đề xuất:

1. Hiển thị trạng thái map trên từng bài nhạc.
2. Hiển thị số lượng map đã tạo: 0/4, 1/4... 4/4.
3. Hiển thị trạng thái Draft / Approved / Missing / Invalid.
4. Cho phép mở Map Editor trực tiếp từ bài nhạc.
5. Cho phép xem trước map trước khi chơi.
6. Cho phép regenerate từng difficulty.
7. Hiển thị BPM, duration, số note và độ khó.
8. Giữ nguyên map đã chỉnh sửa khi refresh hoặc khởi động lại game.

Phase 8.4 is implemented, verified in Node and in the running app. Everything below is what I actually ran.

## What was built

**Status layer** (`src/game/map/MapStatusIndex.js`) — one place that answers "what maps does this song have and are they playable", from the real `SongMapStore` + `SongMapValidator`, never from file existence:

- Per difficulty: `MISSING`, `GENERATED`, `DRAFT`, `APPROVED`, `INVALID`, `UNPLAYABLE`, `MISMATCH`, plus notes, NPS, obstacles, revision, validity, playability, issues.
- Precedence is deliberate: mismatch → invalid → unplayable → approved → generated/draft → missing. An approved map whose audio fingerprint changed reads **MISMATCH**, not approved.
- Per song: counts for all four tiers, `completion` (0/4–4/4), `ready` (approved + playable), `allApproved`, per-difficulty editor drafts.
- Lookups are cached and validated on demand only; a "fast" (`deep:false`) path reads the store index without validating. Cache is bounded (1200) and invalidated per song on writes.

**Library rows** (`UIManager.trackRow`) now carry title/artist/duration/BPM plus a summary line and a `MAPS` chip:

```
128 BPM  1:07  EDM  BUILT-IN      MAPS 2/4   EASY ✧  NORMAL ✓  HARD ·  EXPERT ·
```

**Difficulty screen** (`renderDifficulty`) — every card shows the map state badge with `notes · NPS · obstacles · REV n`, or "MISSING — No map yet", colour-coded, plus a footer line `Maps: 2/4 present · 1 approved · 1 draft`. The footer gained **SONG MAPS** and **EDIT THIS DIFFICULTY** (the editor now honours `data-difficulty`).

**Song maps screen** (`SongMapsScreen`, `src/styles/songMaps.css`) — per-song overlay with difficulty tabs, MAPPED/APPROVED/DRAFT/MISSING/PROBLEMS counters, four map slots with VIEW/CREATE/EDIT, a details panel (notes, NPS, obstacles, revision, timestamp, generator version, seed, per-lane split), 0.5×/1× preview playback, and actions: OPEN MAP EDITOR, REGENERATE (with confirmation), MAP DETAILS. Preview reuses `MapEditorRenderer` + `MapPreviewController`; playback never touches the game loop or `activeScreen`.

**Shared audio** (`SongAudioCache`) — one decode + analysis per song (LRU 3), used by both the maps screen and the editor, so opening the editor after previewing a song does not decode twice.

**Local identity** — `LocalProvider` fingerprints file bytes at import and discards them; `local:<fingerprint>` is content-derived, so renaming the file keeps the maps and different audio can never share them. Mismatch is surfaced per difficulty instead of being silently accepted.

**Editor round trip** — `MapEditor.open({ track, difficulty, returnTo })`; closing from the maps screen reopens the maps screen on the last difficulty instead of dropping to the difficulty screen.

## Bug found and fixed while testing

The first perf pass over 800 maps took 671 ms because every store lookup re-parsed the whole index JSON, and cached records were mutated in place (a "hit" leaked into later reads). The store now caches the parsed index behind a raw-string comparison, so external index loss is still detected. Measured on 200 songs × 4 maps (800 maps, 24 notes each):

| Operation | Time |
|---|---|
| First deep pass (validate 800 maps) | 57.9 ms |
| Cached deep pass | 4.6 ms |
| Index-only fast pass | 19.2 ms |
| Resolve 200 library rows | 4.1 ms |

`qa/stage84-library-map-integration-check.mjs` — **139/139 passed**, 13 sections: identity, status, aggregation, drafts, refresh, cache, duplicates, regeneration, generation, reload, markup, audio, performance. It covers fingerprint determinism and renaming, all seven states, 4-tier aggregation, invalidation on write/approve/rollback/remove, cache hits skipping validation, three editor opens writing one map at one revision, approved/hand-edited regeneration refusals, reload from raw storage, and the large-library case.

Regression (all exit 0): stage8-song-map 136/136, stage82-generator 128/128, stage83-map-editor 168/168, stage84 139/139, stage71-song-lifecycle 92/92, stage7-rig 90/90, stage63 101/101, stage62 68/68, stage61 60/60, stage45 138/138, stage46 133/133, stage5 77/77, stage6-voice 89/89, stage6-judgement 89/89, voice-asset-audit 1619/1619.

## Browser verification (dev server, 1440×900)

- Library: `MAPS 1/4 EASY · NORMAL ✧ HARD · EXPERT ·` with per-chip states and tooltips (`NORMAL: GENERATED · 99 notes · revision 4`).
- MAPS chip → song maps screen: header `1/4 MAPPED`, four slots, NORMAL preview rendering 4 lanes/grid/sections, details panel.
- EASY → CREATE → editor opened **on EASY with 31 generated notes** (confirmed identity `demo:demo:neon-pulse`); SAVE DRAFT wrote rev 1; closing returned to the maps screen which refreshed itself to `2/4 MAPPED` — no reload.
- Difficulty screen showed `GENERATED 31 notes · 0.45 NPS · 2 obstacles · REV 1`, `GENERATED 99 notes · 1.44 NPS · 3 obstacles · REV 4`, and both missing tiers, footer `Maps: 2/4 present · 0 approved · 2 draft`.
- NORMAL approved from the editor: maps screen went to `1/4 APPROVED`, library to `MAPS 2/4 EASY ✧ NORMAL ✓ HARD · EXPERT ·`.
- Preview ▶ advanced the clock to 00:00.663 with `activeScreen` still `library` — no gameplay started.
- REGENERATE on the approved map demanded `REPLACE APPROVED NORMAL MAP? … keeps the previous revision for rollback`; cancelling left the approved map untouched.
- Full page reload: same statuses, index has exactly 2 entries (`medium` rev 4 approved, `easy` rev 1 draft), 4 storage keys total — no duplicates after several opens of both difficulties.
- Console: no errors; only Vite connection debug lines.

## Known limitations

- Local-upload identity is proven by unit tests (bytes → fingerprint, rename, distinct audio), not by uploading a real file in the browser — the browser tools here can't set a file input. Audio bytes are never stored; only the hash-derived id reaches the map keys.
- Map availability in the library never triggers a decode; BPM/duration come from track metadata, and analysis only happens when a preview/editor needs audio.
- First open of a song still costs one decode+analysis (~0.5–1 s for 68.7 s audio); subsequent opens are cache hits (limit 3 songs).
- Gameplay still uses generated charts — Phase 5 was not started, and nothing in spawning, scoring, judgement, chart timing, audio assets or choreography changed.
- The working tree is uncommitted; nothing was committed or pushed.

Bước tiếp theo nên là Phase 5: kết nối map đã lưu vào gameplay. Đây là bước quan trọng nhất để đạt đúng mục tiêu ban đầu: chọn bài nào thì game chạy đúng map của bài đó, không tự sinh map mới mỗi lần chơi.

# Phase 5 — Gameplay Integration: Play the Saved Song Map

Implement Phase 5 by connecting the existing Song Map architecture to the actual gameplay pipeline.

## Primary objective

When a player selects a song and difficulty, gameplay MUST load and play that song's saved map instead of silently generating a new chart.

Each song + difficulty combination must use its own persistent map.

## 1\. Inspect before modifying

Inspect:

- Game.js and the complete song preparation/start flow.
- SongMapLoader.js and SongMapStore.js.
- MapStatusIndex.js.
- BeatmapGenerator.js.
- Gameplay.js and NoteManager.js.
- ScoreStore and chartVersion/mapRevision handling.
- SongMapsScreen and difficulty selection.

Identify exact integration points before changing code.

## 2\. Replace automatic generation with map resolution

In Game.\_prepare():

1. Resolve the stable song identity.
2. Resolve the selected difficulty's saved map.
3. Validate the map against the actual audio fingerprint.
4. Load the saved notes through SongMapLoader.
5. Pass the resulting chart to the existing Gameplay.start().

Never regenerate an existing map automatically.

## 3\. Missing map behaviour

If no map exists:

- Do not silently generate one.
- Show a clear message: "This song has no map for this difficulty."
- Provide an action to open Song Maps or Map Editor.
- Allow the user to explicitly generate a map.
- Return to gameplay only after a playable map is available.

## 4\. Map status handling

Handle every status explicitly:

- APPROVED: playable.
- GENERATED: playable with an appropriate indication.
- DRAFT: require an explicit policy; never silently treat an invalid draft as playable.
- MISSING: offer map creation.
- INVALID: block gameplay and explain validation errors.
- UNPLAYABLE: explain unsupported note types such as holds.
- MISMATCH: block gameplay because the audio fingerprint changed.

Never silently repair or discard notes.

## 5\. Scoring and map revisions

Update score identity to include:

- stable song ID
- difficulty
- map revision or immutable map identity

Editing or replacing a map must not mix scores from different map versions.

Preserve existing scoring and judgement behaviour.

## 6\. Gameplay invariants

Do not change:

- Note timing.
- Judgement windows.
- Scoring formulas.
- Combo and health logic.
- Audio synchronization.
- SongTimeline end handling.
- Auto Dance timing.
- Voice announcements.
- Dance choreography.
- Rig architecture.

The existing NoteManager should receive the saved chart through its current contract.

## 7\. Results screen

Include:

- Song name.
- Difficulty.
- Map revision.
- Map status.
- Score and accuracy.
- Existing judgement statistics.

Ensure results correspond to the exact map revision used during the attempt.

- No automatic regeneration occurs.

- Auto Dance uses the saved map.
- Results contain the correct map revision.
