Lưu ý: Phase 5 không nên tự động tạo map khi thiếu. Người chơi phải biết bài nào chưa có map và chủ động tạo map trước khi chơi. Đây mới là trải nghiệm đúng với hệ thống bạn đang xây dựng.



All Phase 5 work is complete and verified. Here is the report.

## Core change

`Game._prepare()` no longer generates a chart. It resolves the song's stored map and hands that chart to the unchanged `Gameplay.start()`:

```
track → decode (as before) → describeSongIdentity(track, {buffer|arrayBuffer})
      → SongMapLoader.resolve({ songId, difficulty, identity })
      → evaluateGameplayMap()   ← approve / generated / draft / missing / invalid / unplayable / mismatch / duration
      → songMapToChart() → Gameplay.start() → NoteManager.setChart()
```

`BeatMapGenerator` is no longer imported by `Game.js`. It remains where generation is an explicit act: the Map Editor, regeneration, and the Video Studio.

## Status policy (as implemented)

| Status | Gameplay | What the player sees |
|---|---|---|
| APPROVED | plays | toast `APPROVED MAP · MEDIUM · REV 4` |
| GENERATED | plays | toast `GENERATED MAP · HARD · REV 1 · NOT APPROVED` |
| DRAFT (hand-edited, unapproved) | gate with `PLAY ANYWAY` | "Revision 1 has never been approved…" |
| MISSING | blocked | `NO HARD MAP` + OPEN MAP EDITOR / SONG MAPS / BACK |
| INVALID | blocked | validator errors listed, editor offered |
| UNPLAYABLE (holds) | blocked | explains holds, nothing dropped |
| FINGERPRINT MISMATCH | blocked | "This map was built for different audio…" |
| Notes past the audio (`duration-mismatch`) | blocked | last-note time vs audio end |

The gate is a new overlay (`src/ui/mapGateMarkup.js` + `.mapgate` styles); it only offers actions and never writes anything.

## Score identity

`ScoreStore.keyFor(trackId, difficulty, chartVersion, mapRevision)` now includes the map revision: `demo:neon-pulse::medium::v2::r4`. A record also stores `mapRevision`. Editing/regenerating a map therefore starts a fresh personal best instead of folding a new performance into an older chart's record. Results show the revision they were played on.

## Files

New: `src/game/map/GameplayMap.js` (gate + resolution, pure), `src/ui/mapGateMarkup.js`, `qa/stage85-gameplay-map-integration-check.mjs` (+ artifact).

Modified: `src/core/Game.js` (map resolution, `allowDraft`, map descriptor on the run, gate on refusal, results payload `mapRevision`/`mapStatus`/`songMapId`), `src/game/ScoreStore.js` (revision-keyed records), `src/ui/UIManager.js` (gate, gate actions, results map line, revision in the stored run), `src/ui/templates.js` (results map line), `src/styles/main.css` (gate + results map), `qa/stage43-score-store-check.mjs` (allowlist now includes the intentional `mapRevision` field).

Untouched: note timing, judgement windows, scoring formulas, combo/health, audio sync, `SongTimeline`, Auto Dance timing, voice, choreography, rig, audio assets.

## Tests

`qa/stage85-gameplay-map-integration-check.mjs` — **91/91 passed** across 11 sections: resolution, blocking, drafts, difficulty, no-generation, gameplay, auto-dance, score-identity, local-identity, duration, gate-messages. It drives the real `Gameplay` + `NoteManager` on a fake clock with charts produced by the real loader.

Regression, all exit 0: stage8-song-map 136/136, stage82 128/128, stage83 168/168, stage84 139/139, stage71 92/92, stage7-rig 90/90, stage63 101/101, stage62 68/68, stage61 60/60, stage45 138/138, stage46 133/133, stage5 77/77, stage6-voice 89/89, stage6-judgement 89/89, stage43/stage42/stage44 `failures: []`, voice-asset-audit 1619/1619.

`npm run build` passes: `index-BUrJwW5s.js` 1,325.04 kB (370.40 kB gzip), `index-CZby9PLN.css` 100.96 kB (20.19 kB gzip).

## Browser verification (dev server)

- Started NORMAL from the difficulty screen: `state: intro`, chart `99 notes`, `source: song-map`, revision 4, status approved, id `demo:demo:neon-pulse::medium`, toast `APPROVED MAP · MEDIUM · REV 4`; timeline duration 68.7 s.
- Finished the run (seeked near the end so the real end signal fired): results screen read **`MEDIUM MAP · REV 4 · APPROVED`**, payload `mapRevision: 4`, `mapStatus: approved`. Stored key: `demo:neon-pulse::medium::v2::r4`.
- RETRY reloaded the same map: revision 4, 99 notes.
- HARD (missing at the time): gate `NO HARD MAP`, message exactly "This song has no map for this difficulty…", actions OPEN MAP EDITOR / SONG MAPS / BACK, `PLAY ANYWAY` hidden; no map key was written by the refusal (4 → 4).
- From the gate: OPEN MAP EDITOR opened on **hard** with a 145-note draft; SAVE DRAFT wrote rev 1; closing returned to the maps screen, whose slots refreshed live to `HARD GENERATED 145 notes · REV 1`.
- Then HARD played that map (145 notes, toast `GENERATED MAP · HARD · REV 1 · NOT APPROVED`), and switching to EASY loaded a different map (31 notes, rev 1) — difficulties stay independent.
- A hand-edited unapproved map (generated by me in storage to exercise the branch) produced `EXPERT MAP IS A DRAFT` + `PLAY ANYWAY`; playing it started 145 notes, status `draft`, toast `DRAFT MAP · EXPERT · REV 1 · NOT APPROVED`.
- Console after all of this: only `[info] [Game] start blocked by song map missing-map` — blocked starts are handled outcomes, not errors. No exceptions, no failed requests.

## Remaining limitations

- A draft played via `PLAY ANYWAY` re-asks on RETRY; the confirmation is per attempt by design, not remembered for the session.
- Old score records (pre-Phase 5 keys without `::rN`) are left in place but are no longer matched, so previously displayed "BEST" numbers will not reappear until the new revision is played.
- Jamendo tracks streamed without a decoded buffer have no audio fingerprint at start, so mismatch detection there falls back to the map's own fingerprint only; local uploads and the demo are decoded and fully checked.
- Only one map per song + difficulty is live (previous revisions stay for rollback), and the Video Studio still charts procedurally — it is not a gameplay run.
- The demo library in this browser now holds maps for EASY rev 1, NORMAL rev 4 (approved) and HARD rev 1, created during verification.
- Working tree uncommitted; nothing committed or pushed.


Được. Mình hiểu ý bạn là: khi bấm REGENERATE, hệ thống không nên tạo vật thể dồn dập ngay lập tức. Thay vào đó, mỗi lần tạo vật thể cần có khoảng cách tối thiểu 0.5–1 giây, để người chơi có thời gian phản ứng và xử lý.

Mình đề xuất làm thành một thay đổi riêng, không ảnh hưởng đến nhịp nhạc hay timing của các vật thể khác.

Đây là prompt để giao cho AI code:

Writing

# Phase 6.1 — Regenerate Object Spawn Spacing

## Objective

Fix the regenerate map/object generation system so objects are not spawned too frequently.

Currently, regenerating objects can produce multiple objects in rapid succession, making gameplay too difficult because players do not have enough time to react.

## 1\. Inspect first

Inspect:

- BeatmapGenerator.js
- DifficultyGenerator.js
- SectionDetector.js
- SongMap.js
- MapEditor regeneration flow
- NoteManager.js
- Existing note spacing and validation rules

Identify where consecutive objects are generated and where spawn timing is controlled.

## 2\. Minimum object interval

Introduce configurable minimum intervals between consecutive playable objects:

- Easy: 1.0 seconds
- Normal: 0.8 seconds
- Hard: 0.65 seconds
- Expert: 0.5 seconds

These are initial defaults and should be configurable.

Important:

- Measure intervals using actual note timestamps, not rendering frame time.
- Apply spacing during generation, not by delaying NoteManager spawning.
- Avoid changing the song's BPM or audio synchronization.
- Preserve musical alignment wherever possible.

## 3\. Regeneration behaviour

When REGENERATE is clicked:

1. Generate a new candidate map.
2. Apply difficulty-specific spacing.
3. Validate the result.
4. Show the updated object count and spacing statistics.
5. Save only after validation succeeds.
6. Preserve the existing approved-map and hand-edited-map protections.

Never overwrite an approved map without explicit confirmation.

## 4\. Avoid bad generation

Do not simply delete random notes.

Prioritize:

- Keeping strong beats.
- Preserving important drops and musical sections.
- Keeping lane distribution balanced.
- Avoiding consecutive objects that are too close.
- Preserving obstacle spacing.
- Maintaining difficulty progression.

If a note is removed due to spacing, record its removal reason in the generation report.

## 5\. Editor feedback

Show:

- Minimum object interval.
- Actual shortest interval.
- Average interval.
- Number of objects removed by spacing.
- Object count before and after regeneration.

Add a warning when the generated map cannot satisfy the requested interval without losing too many important musical events.

## 6\. Tests

Create or extend the generator QA suite.

Test:

- Every difficulty respects its configured minimum interval.
- No duplicate timestamps on the same lane.
- Strong beats are prioritized.
- Obstacles retain their safety spacing.
- Generation remains deterministic.
- Regeneration does not overwrite protected maps.
- All notes remain inside the song duration.
- Difficulty progression remains meaningful.
- Existing saved maps are not modified by merely changing generator defaults.

Run all existing regression suites and npm run build.

## 7\. Constraints

Do not change:

- SongTimeline.
- Audio playback speed.
- Judgement windows.
- Scoring.
- NoteManager spawn lead time.
- Gameplay clock.
- Existing saved map contents.
- Rig or choreography systems.

## Final report

Provide actual changed files, test results, build result, and browser verification.

Core requirement: Regenerated maps must give players enough time between consecutive objects to react, with 0.5–1.0 seconds as the difficulty-dependent target.

Một lưu ý: Khoảng cách này nên áp dụng giữa các sự kiện chơi liên tiếp, không nhất thiết giữa từng note trong một chord (nhiều lane xuất hiện cùng lúc). Như vậy game vẫn có những đoạn nhạc sôi động mà không biến thành spam vật thể.


I'll start by inspecting the generator pipeline and the spacing rules that already exist.

Now I'll write the spacing engine as its own module, then wire it into generation.




























Now the Phase 6.1 QA suite covering spacing, prioritisation and regeneration protection.













Phase 6.1 is complete. Regenerated maps now enforce a difficulty-dependent minimum interval between objects, and the player-facing report says exactly what that interval is and what it cost.

## What changed

**New: `src/game/map/ObjectSpacing.js`** — one deterministic pass that runs inside generation:

- Defaults (`OBJECT_SPACING_DEFAULTS`): easy 1.0 s, normal/medium 0.8 s, hard 0.65 s, expert 0.5 s. Configurable per call (`generateSongMap({ spacing })`), per difficulty (`spacingFor('hard', { hard: 0.9 })`), or one explicit `minInterval`.
- Objects are **timestamp buckets** (chords stay together as one object; two notes closer than 1 ms are one object).
- The kept set is the **optimal** subset under the interval constraint — weighted interval scheduling over buckets, not random deletion. Bucket weight = note accent + chord size + beat/bar alignment + section role (drop 1.6 → outro 0.7) + hazard bonus. Ties prefer the earlier, stronger object, so output is deterministic.
- Hazards get a second pass so obstacle safety spacing survives (`OBSTACLE_MIN_GAP` 2 s); if a hazard must go, the taps at that instant stay.
- Notes are **never moved**: a note is kept at its exact musical time or dropped. BPM, offset and audio sync are untouched.
- Every drop is counted by reason (`spacing`, `obstacleGap`), and `spacing-conflict` warns when an interval destroys most of the drops or strong beats (not for the ordinary thinning a slow tier needs).

**`BeatmapGenerator.js`** — generator version bumped to `neon-map-gen-2`; the spacing pass runs after obstacle control and before the density ceiling; the report gained `spacing` (interval, shortest, average, before/after objects, removals by reason, strong-beat and drop retention) plus `removed.bySpacing` / `removed.byObstacleGap`.

**Editor feedback** — `MapEditor` report now prints `object spacing · min 1.00s · shortest 1.11s · avg 2.58s` and `objects 40 → 26 · removed by spacing 14 · hazard gap 0 · strong beats 12/15 · drop objects 5/8`; the regenerate confirmation states the interval. `MapEditorController` and `SongMapsScreen` toasts report `EASY regenerated (revision 2) · 40 → 26 objects · min 1.00s`. The song-maps details panel shows the saved map's real spacing and flags `BELOW TARGET` for maps written before this change.

## Tests

`qa/stage86-object-spacing-check.mjs` — **122/122 passed**, 11 sections: defaults/configurability, generation (per-tier interval, duplicate timestamps, same-lane collisions, duration bounds), priority (beat vs off-beat, bar vs beat, drop vs verse, hazard vs tap, chord integrity), alignment (no note moved — compared against the same pipeline with spacing disabled), hazards (safety gap in isolation and in generated maps), determinism (byte-identical notes and report), protection (approved/hand-edited regeneration refused, forced regeneration writes rev+1 as draft, previous revision kept), existing maps (changing defaults never rewrites stored data), reporting, validation.

Regression, all exit 0 (final run against the finished code): stage8-song-map 136/136, stage82 128/128, stage83 168/168, stage84 139/139, stage85 91/91, stage71 92/92, stage7-rig 90/90, stage63 101/101, stage62 68/68, stage61 60/60, stage45 138/138, stage46 133/133, stage5 77/77, stage6-voice 89/89, stage6-judgement 89/89, stage43/42/44 `failures: []`, voice-asset-audit 1619/1619.

`npm run build` passes: `index-DyokXVD8.js` 1,331.02 kB (372.44 kB gzip), CSS 100.96 kB (20.19 kB gzip).

## Browser verification

- Regenerated EASY from Song Maps: dialog read “Objects will keep at least 1.00s between them.”; after confirm the stored map went **31 → 26 notes, rev 1 → rev 2, generated**, shortest gap **1.115 s**, toast `EASY regenerated (revision 2) · 40 → 26 objects · min 1.00s.` Slot and details refreshed live.
- Editor regenerate on EASY: report showed `object spacing · min 1.00s · shortest 1.11s · avg 2.58s`, `objects 40 → 26 · removed by spacing 14 · strong beats 12/15 · drop objects 5/8`, generator `neon-map-gen-2`, revision 3; toast matched.
- NORMAL is approved: REGENERATE showed `REPLACE APPROVED NORMAL MAP? … Objects will keep at least 0.80s …`; cancelling left it **approved, revision 4, untouched**.
- Started gameplay on the regenerated EASY map: 26 notes, revision 3, **shortest 1.115 s**, average 2.58 s, map duration 68.7 s = timeline 68.7 s. NORMAL still loads its approved 99-note rev 4 map.
- Song-maps details line rendered: `object spacing · min 1.00s · shortest 1.11s · avg 2.58s`.
- Console: no errors or failed requests.

## Limitations

- Spacing applies at generation/regeneration time only; maps saved before this change keep their original notes (the UI flags them `BELOW TARGET`, and only an explicit regenerate changes them).
- The interval is between consecutive *objects*, so a chord is kept intact and its notes don’t count separately against the interval.
- The `spacing-conflict` warning is deliberately conservative (drops/strong beats heavily lost), so a dense song thinned by ~50 % on easy does not warn.
- Generator version 2 changes seeds for newly generated maps; existing maps are unaffected.
- Verified with synthetic transient grids in QA and the real demo mix in the browser — no broad real-music corpus measurement.
- Working tree is uncommitted; nothing committed or pushed. The demo library in this browser now has EASY rev 3 (26 notes), NORMAL rev 4 (approved, 99 notes) and HARD rev 1 (145 notes, pre-spacing).

Phase 6.1 đã hoàn thành khá tốt. Đặc biệt, cách xử lý mới giữ nguyên thời điểm của note, chỉ chọn lọc vật thể cần giữ lại nên không làm lệch nhạc.

Tuy nhiên, có một điểm cần chú ý: map cũ không tự được cập nhật theo quy tắc mới. EASY đã được regenerate, nhưng NORMAL và HARD vẫn có thể còn mật độ vật thể cũ.

## Đề xuất Phase 6.2 — Kiểm soát mật độ vật thể khi chơi

Mình muốn cải thiện thêm 3 điểm:

1. Không spam vật thể: Có khoảng nghỉ hợp lý giữa các sự kiện.
2. Độ khó thực sự khác nhau: Easy dễ phản ứng, Normal cân bằng, Hard nhanh hơn, Expert dày hơn.
3. Không mất cao trào: Đến Drop vẫn có nhiều vật thể hơn Verse, nhưng không xuất hiện dồn dập đến mức không thể chơi.

Một điều quan trọng nữa: nên có thống kê số vật thể mỗi 10 giây để phát hiện đoạn nhạc nào quá dày.

Bạn nên giữ Phase 6.1 làm nền tảng, chưa cần thay đổi hệ thống gameplay hay scoring.



Đây là prompt Phase 6.2. Mục tiêu là làm cho độ khó cân bằng hơn, tránh spam vật thể nhưng vẫn giữ được cao trào của bài nhạc.

Writing

# Phase 6.2 — Adaptive Object Density & Difficulty Balancing

## Objective

Improve the generated Song Maps so object density feels balanced, playable and musically meaningful across all four difficulty levels.

Phase 6.1 already introduced minimum object spacing:

- Easy: 1.0s
- Normal: 0.8s
- Hard: 0.65s
- Expert: 0.5s

Now improve the overall distribution of objects throughout each song.

The goal is NOT simply to reduce object counts.

The goal is to make gameplay feel natural:

- Quiet sections should have fewer objects.
- Verses should have moderate density.
- Build-ups should gradually increase intensity.
- Drops should feel exciting and energetic.
- Breakdowns should have breathing room.
- Outros should gradually reduce intensity.

## 1\. Inspect first

Inspect:

- ObjectSpacing.js
- BeatmapGenerator.js
- DifficultyGenerator.js
- SectionDetector.js
- AudioAnalyzer.js
- SongMap.js
- MapEditor generation report
- Existing generator QA tests

Identify where density is calculated and where each difficulty profile is applied.

Do not modify gameplay timing or scoring.

## 2\. Adaptive density profiles

Introduce configurable density targets for each difficulty.

Initial suggested targets (objects per second):

| Difficulty | Quiet sections | Normal sections | Drop sections |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Easy | 0.20 | 0.40 | 0.65 |
| Normal | 0.35 | 0.65 | 1.00 |
| Hard | 0.50 | 0.90 | 1.35 |
| Expert | 0.70 | 1.20 | 1.80 |

These are initial tuning targets, not mandatory quotas.

Actual density must respect:

- Available musical transients.
- Minimum object spacing.
- Audio duration.
- Section confidence.
- Existing difficulty maxNps.
- Musical alignment.

Never create artificial notes just to reach a target.

## 3\. Sliding-window density analysis

Add a density analyzer that measures objects in:

- 5-second windows.
- 10-second windows.
- Entire musical sections.

For every window calculate:

- Object count.
- Objects per second.
- Average interval.
- Shortest interval.
- Strong-beat object count.
- Obstacle count.

Detect:

- Excessively dense sections.
- Sudden density spikes.
- Empty sections that should contain musical events.
- Unnatural transitions between sections.

## 4\. Adaptive generation

Improve the generation pipeline:

Audio analysis → Section detection → Difficulty profile → Musical event selection → Density balancing → Object spacing → Obstacle safety → Validation → Song Map

Prioritize musical events:

1. Strong kick and downbeat.
2. Important drop accents.
3. Strong snare events.
4. Build-up accents.
5. Secondary percussion.

Do not sacrifice all important drop events merely to achieve a uniform density.

## 5\. Natural difficulty progression

Ensure:

- EASY: generous breathing room and predictable patterns.
- NORMAL: balanced rhythm and moderate variation.
- HARD: faster patterns and more lane variation.
- EXPERT: higher density and syncopation without becoming physically unreasonable.

Difficulty should increase through more than note count:

- Rhythmic complexity.
- Lane changes.
- Syncopation.
- Chord frequency.
- Event spacing.
- Section intensity.

Keep existing judgement windows unchanged.

## 6\. Density visualization

Extend the Map Editor generation report.

Show:

- Density per 10-second window.
- Average density per section.
- Peak density.
- Maximum density window.
- Empty windows.
- Density warnings.
- Difficulty comparison.

Use a simple visual density graph so the user can immediately identify overcrowded parts of a song.

## 7\. Regeneration protection

Preserve all existing protections:

- Never overwrite approved maps without confirmation.
- Never overwrite hand-edited maps without confirmation.
- Preserve previous revisions.
- Keep deterministic generation.
- Do not alter existing saved maps automatically.

Regeneration must remain an explicit user action.

## 8\. Automated testing

Create:

qa/stage87-density-balance-check.mjs

Test:

- All four difficulty profiles.
- Density targets and ceilings.
- 5-second and 10-second windows.
- Section-specific density.
- Drop intensity compared with verse.
- Breakdown breathing room.
- Outro fade.
- No artificial note generation.
- Minimum spacing from Phase 6.1 remains enforced.
- Obstacle safety spacing.
- Difficulty progression.
- Determinism.
- Short songs.
- Long songs.
- Sparse music.
- Dense music.
- Low-confidence section detection.
- Approved and hand-edited map protection.
- Existing map preservation.

Run all previous QA suites and npm run build.

## 9\. Browser verification

Use the real demo song.

Generate all four difficulties and inspect:

- Entire-song density.
- First 10 seconds.
- Verse.
- Drop.
- Breakdown.
- Final 10 seconds.

Verify that:

- EASY has enough breathing room.
- NORMAL feels balanced.
- HARD is noticeably more challenging.
- EXPERT is the most demanding.
- No section contains an unreasonable burst of objects.

Check that the generated maps are actually playable using the existing gameplay system.

## 10\. Constraints

Do not change:

- MusicPlayer.
- SongTimeline.
- Audio playback speed.
- NoteManager.
- Judgement windows.
- Scoring formulas.
- Gameplay synchronization.
- Rig architecture.
- Choreography.
- Existing saved maps.

Do not download external assets.

## Final report

Report:

- Files added and modified.
- Actual density measurements.
- Difficulty comparison.
- Test counts.
- Regression results.
- Build result.
- Browser verification.
- Remaining limitations.

Core principle:

**A good rhythm map is not the map with the most objects. It is the map where every object feels intentional, playable and synchronized with the music.**

Lưu ý: Các con số mật độ trong prompt là mục tiêu để thử nghiệm, không phải ép mọi bài nhạc phải có đủ số vật thể đó. Nhạc ít trống thì phải ít vật thể, không được tự bịa thêm note để lấp đầy map.

Phase 6.2 is implemented, tested and verified in the browser.

## Files added
- `src/game/map/DensityAnalyzer.js` — density targets, ramp-aware balancing, window/section analysis and warnings.
- `src/game/map/editor/densityMarkup.js` — the density graph block for the generation report (pure, DOM-free).
- `qa/stage87-density-balance-check.mjs` + `qa-artifacts/stage87-density-balance-check.json` — 203 checks.

## Files modified
- `src/game/map/BeatmapGenerator.js` — new pipeline `… → event selection → balanceDensity → obstacle control → spacing → ceiling`; `MAP_GENERATOR_VERSION` 3 (`neon-map-gen-3`); report gains `density`, `densityBalance`, `notesBeforeDensity`, `removed.byDensity`; optional `densityTargets`.
- `src/game/map/MapEditor.js` — report now includes the density graph, avg/peak/peak-window/empty-window line, per-section density vs target, targets comparison, density warnings, and the `density balance` removal count.
- `src/styles/mapEditor.css` — bar-graph styling (normal / over-dense / empty bars).

## How density works
Targets from the brief (Easy 0.20/0.40/0.65 … Expert 0.70/1.20/1.80) are goals, not quotas. Each section is packed at the tier's Phase-6.1 interval, accents can take a slot when the gap allows, then the result is trimmed to a fractional quota that follows the section's target curve — builds ramp normal→drop, breakdowns ramp normal→quiet, outros fade. Nothing is ever added; trimming only widens gaps, so spacing afterwards has nothing to undo.

## Measured density (QA fixture, 128 BPM, 68.7 s, intro/verse/build/drop/breakdown/outro)
| Tier | Objects | Avg obj/s | Peak | Drop | Verse | Build | Outro |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Easy | 25 | 0.364 | 0.50 | 0.60/0.65 | 0.342/0.40 | 0.417/0.525 | 0.27/0.16 |
| Normal | 45 | 0.655 | 0.90 | 0.90/1.00 | 0.684/0.65 | 0.75/0.825 | 0.27/0.28 |
| Hard | 62 | 0.902 | 1.10 | 1.20/1.35 | 0.957/0.90 | 1.00/1.125 | 0.54/0.40 |
| Expert | 79 | 1.106 | 1.30 | 1.30/1.80 | 1.197/1.20 | 1.333/1.50 | 0.54/0.56 |

Drops out-rank verses (Normal 0.90 vs 0.68, Hard 1.20 vs 0.96, Expert 1.30 vs 1.20), breakdowns breathe (0.67–1.0), and each tier is strictly denser than the one below.

All prior suites pass: stage8 136/136, stage82 128/128, stage83 168/168, stage84 139/139, stage85 91/91, stage86 122/122, stage87 203/203, stage71 92/92, stage7-rig 90/90, stage63 101/101, stage62 68/68, stage61 60/60, stage45 138/138, stage46 failures 0, stage5 77/77, stage6-voice 89/89, stage6-judgement 89/89, stage43/42/44 `failures: []`, voice-asset-audit 1619/1619. `npm run build` exit 0 — `index-X4xOOe1A.js` 1,340.70 kB (375.60 kB gzip), CSS 101.37 kB, built in 614 ms.

## Browser verification (real demo song, 128 BPM, 68.7 s, analysis 87 %)
All four difficulties were regenerated in the Map Editor; each report showed the graph with 7 ten-second bars:
- **Easy rev 4** — 25 objects, avg 0.36/s, peak 0.60/s @ 50–60 s, min 1.00 s / shortest 1.04 s, no empty windows.
- **Normal rev 5** — 43 objects, avg 0.63/s, peak 0.90/s @ 50–60 s, min 0.80 / shortest 0.93 s; windows 0.30, 0.60, 0.60, 0.70, 0.70, 0.90, 0.57.
- **Hard rev 2** — 59 objects, avg 0.86/s, peak 1.10/s @ 40–50 s, min 0.65 / shortest 0.65 s.
- **Expert rev 2** — 77 objects, avg 1.09/s, peak 1.30/s @ 10–20 s, min 0.50 / shortest 0.51 s, strong beats 44/44, drop objects 11/11.
Spacing removed 0 objects on every one (density already respects the interval). Regeneration protection held: the approved Normal map asked "APPROVED MAP … REPLACE ANYWAY / CANCEL", cancel left revision 4 approved untouched, and the replace produced revision 5 as a draft with revision 4 restorable.

## Limitations
- Targets are goals: easy averages 0.36/s on this song (drop target 0.65 unreachable with only ~8 easy events there) and expert's drop lands at 1.2–1.3/s against 1.8 because the analysis offers ~3.5 transients/s in that section. No notes were invented to close those gaps.
- The analyzer raised one `density-transition` warning on Normal/Hard for the demo (abrupt rate change between adjacent sections); it is surfaced in the report rather than suppressed.
- Gameplay loaded the regenerated map correctly (`source: song-map`, revision 5, 43 notes), but I could not watch the pre-roll countdown or live note spawning: the automated tab reports `document.hidden === true`, and the intro only advances on a visible page. Playability beyond map resolution is therefore unverified in this session.
- Replacing the demo's approved Normal map during verification leaves the demo with 0 approved maps (rev 5 draft; rev 4 still restorable).

If you want the Easy and Expert drops to reach their targets on the demo, the next step would be widening the event pool for those sections rather than forcing objects.

Đây là prompt tiếp theo cho Phase 6.3 – Smart Object Distribution & Musical Variety. Mục tiêu là giải quyết tình trạng map có khoảng cách đều nhưng vật thể xuất hiện chưa tự nhiên, thiếu đa dạng và chưa bám sát nhạc.

Copy toàn bộ prompt này cho AI coding agent:

Writing

# PHASE 6.3 — SMART MUSICAL OBJECT DISTRIBUTION & VARIETY

Continue developing the existing rhythm game map-generation system.

IMPORTANT:

- Inspect the actual source code before editing.
- Do not rewrite existing architecture.
- Preserve all existing gameplay, scoring, judgement, audio synchronization and map persistence contracts.
- Build on Phase 6.1 ObjectSpacing and Phase 6.2 DensityAnalyzer.
- Do not modify approved or hand-edited maps automatically.
- Implement, test and verify everything before reporting completion.

## 1\. Main objective

Make generated objects feel musically natural rather than mechanically distributed.

Current problems:

- Objects can feel repetitive.
- Some sections have too many similar patterns.
- Objects may appear at predictable intervals.
- Some strong musical moments are underrepresented.
- Transitions between sections can feel abrupt.
- Difficulty differences sometimes feel like simply having more objects.

The goal is to make every song have its own recognizable gameplay pattern.

## 2\. Musical event classification

Analyze available audio features and classify events into:

- Kick
- Snare
- Hi-hat
- Bass accent
- Melody accent
- Strong beat
- Weak beat
- Fill
- Build-up
- Drop
- Breakdown
- Outro

Use existing BeatDetector, MusicStructure, SectionDetector and AudioAnalyzer.

Do not invent events when the audio analysis does not support them.

Every event should have:

- timestamp
- event type
- strength
- confidence
- section
- beat alignment

## 3\. Pattern variety engine

Create:

src/game/map/PatternGenerator.js

Generate reusable musical patterns:

- Single lane
- Alternating lanes
- Double objects
- Short bursts
- Beat-following patterns
- Syncopated patterns
- Section-specific patterns
- Drop patterns
- Breakdown sparse patterns

Rules:

- Avoid repeating the same pattern too frequently.
- Avoid excessive consecutive objects in the same lane.
- Avoid random lane changes without musical justification.
- Keep patterns deterministic.
- Preserve exact musical timestamps.
- Do not create impossible simultaneous inputs.

## 4\. Musical section behavior

Different sections should have distinct gameplay personalities.

INTRO:

- Very sparse.
- Gradually introduce objects.

VERSE:

- Stable rhythm.
- Moderate variation.
- Avoid excessive density.

BUILD-UP:

- Gradually increase activity.
- Increase complexity toward the drop.
- Never exceed spacing limits.

DROP:

- Strongest musical accents receive priority.
- More recognizable patterns.
- Higher energy without overwhelming the player.

BREAKDOWN:

- Reduce density.
- Leave breathing room.
- Prefer isolated strong accents.

OUTRO:

- Gradually reduce activity.
- End naturally with the music.
- Avoid spawning unnecessary objects at the final seconds.

## 5\. Difficulty personality

Each difficulty must have a different gameplay personality.

EASY:

- Simple patterns.
- Mostly predictable alternating lanes.
- Minimal syncopation.
- Comfortable reaction time.

NORMAL:

- More lane variation.
- Moderate syncopation.
- Recognizable rhythmic patterns.

HARD:

- More complex patterns.
- More frequent lane changes.
- Selective syncopation.
- More challenging combinations.

EXPERT:

- Highest pattern variety.
- Complex rhythmic combinations.
- Strong musical accents prioritized.
- Never sacrifice fairness for difficulty.

Do not simply increase object count to create difficulty.

## 6\. Integrate existing constraints

The new pattern engine must respect:

- Phase 6.1 minimum object spacing.
- Phase 6.2 density targets.
- Existing obstacle safety gap.
- Four-lane contract.
- Existing note types.
- Audio duration boundaries.
- Existing beat quantization.
- Existing map validation.

Pipeline should be evaluated and ordered carefully so later passes cannot destroy the musical patterns.

If any pass removes or changes a pattern, report it.

## 7\. Generation report

Extend the existing generation report with:

- Pattern count.
- Pattern types used.
- Pattern repetition score.
- Same-lane repetition percentage.
- Alternation percentage.
- Syncopation percentage.
- Strong-beat retention.
- Section-specific pattern distribution.
- Pattern removals.
- Variety warnings.

Display these in the Map Editor generation report.

Use visual indicators where appropriate.

## 8\. Determinism

Same song fingerprint + difficulty + generator version + settings must always produce identical maps.

Different songs should produce different patterns.

Regeneration must increment revision and preserve the previous map.

Approved and hand-edited maps must retain existing overwrite protection.

## 9\. QA requirements

- Pattern variety.
- Pattern repetition.
- Lane transitions.
- Section-specific behavior.
- Difficulty personality.
- Musical alignment.

- Approved-map protection.
- Hand-edited-map protection.
- Regeneration and rollback.
- Existing generator regressions.

Run all previous QA suites.

Run npm run build.

## 10\. Real browser verification

Phase 6.3 is implemented, integrated and verified. Here is the full report.

## Files created

| File | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `src/game/map/PatternGenerator.js` | Event classification, phrase builder, pattern vocabulary/renderers, chooser, measurement, warnings, `generatePatterns()` |
| `src/game/map/editor/patternMarkup.js` | Pattern-mix strip + variety lines for the Map Editor generation report |
| `qa/stage88-pattern-generator-check.mjs` | 159-check suite for the new stage |
| `qa-artifacts/stage88-pattern-generator-check.json` | Latest measurements (variety per tier, timing) |

## Files modified

- `src/game/map/BeatmapGenerator.js` — pattern stage wired into the pipeline, report extension, generator version 4.
- `src/game/map/MapEditor.js` — pattern block in `_renderReport`, `_patternMarkup`, removed-line relabelled.
- `src/styles/mapEditor.css` — `.mapedit__patterns` strip and per-type segment colours.

## Generation architecture

`MAP_GENERATOR_VERSION = 4` (`neon-map-gen-4`). Lane selection moved out of the old per-bucket rules into one musical pass, ordered so later passes cannot retime anything:

```
section shaping → balanceDensity (6.2) → generatePatterns (6.3) → controlObstacles → applyObjectSpacing (6.1) → density ceiling
```

- **Classification** (`classifyEvents`): each surviving object gets `event`, `instrument`, `strength`, `confidence`, `section`, `role`, `onBeat`, `onBar`, `progress`, `hazard`. Priority is section role (drop/build/breakdown/outro) → instrument onset matched within `ONSET_MATCH = 0.06 s` → beat alignment. `bass`/`melody` only come from analysed curves ≥ 0.62 within 0.35 s. Objects are never created; classification describes what the analysis supports.
- **Phrases** (`buildPhrases`): split on section change, on a pause longer than one bar (`PHRASE_GAP_BEATS = 4`), and by tier cap (4/6/8/8 objects), so a motif stays readable after 6.2 thins the chart.
- **Patterns** (`PATTERN_TYPES`, `renderPattern`): `single`, `alternate`, `chord`, `run`, `beat`, `syncopated`, `drop`, `breakdown`, `intro`, `outro`. Each renderer is deterministic from the seeded RNG and only proposes lanes.
- **Chooser** (`choosePattern`): section personality first (a drop gets a drop pattern, breakdown/ intro/outro likewise, once per section), build-ups get `run`/`syncopated` in their last 40 %, then seeded choice with three anti-repetition rules: no immediate repeat unless the draw allows a single musical repeat, never a third in a row, and no type above 50 % of a section once three phrases exist. `sectionUses` is per section, so a verse using `drop` does not block the drop's own pattern.
- **Lane assignment**: obstacles keep their lane, chords keep distinct lanes per object, per-lane `minGap` from the Phase 6.1 profile and the obstacle clearance are enforced; a blocked object is removed, never moved in time. Removals are reported as `removed.byLaneRules` and recomputed against the final map as `patterns.brokenByLaterPasses`.
- **Report** (`report.patterns`): phrase count, type counts, unique types, repetition score, max run, same-lane %, alternation %, wide-jump %, lane-change %, syncopation %, strong-beat retention, section distribution, chords, `objectsKept`, removals, `brokenByLaterPasses`, warnings. `patternMarkup` renders it as a proportional strip (one coloured segment per type) plus two text lines, with a `warn` state when repeats exceed 60 %.

## Before / after

Note counts on the built-in demo (the only thing the old system measured):

| Tier | Phase 6.2 | Phase 6.3 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| EASY | 25 | 26 |
| NORMAL | 43 | 44 |
| HARD | 59 | 61 |
| EXPERT | 77 | 75 |

No tier gained objects purely for difficulty; EXPERT actually produces fewer, more structured objects than before while using the widest vocabulary.

## Pattern variety measurements

Deterministic fixture (128 BPM, intro/verse/build/drop/breakdown/outro), from the QA artifact:

| Tier | Notes | Phrases | Unique types | Repeats | Same lane | Alternation | Wide jumps | Syncopation | Strong beats | Avg phrase |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| EASY | 24 | 11 | 5 | 0 % | 34.8 % | 43.5 % | 21.7 % | 29.2 % | 94.4 % | 2.18 |
| NORMAL | 44 | 14 | 8 | 7.7 % | 4.7 % | 41.9 % | 53.5 % | 36.4 % | 100 % | 3.14 |
| HARD | 63 | 15 | 8 | 21.4 % | 16.1 % | 40.3 % | 43.5 % | 44.4 % | 100 % | 4.20 |
| EXPERT | 79 | 13 | 7 | 8.3 % | 21.1 % | 21.1 % | 57.9 % | 61.0 % | 100 % | 5.92 |

Syncopation rises monotonically with difficulty, phrases get longer, wide jumps increase, and no tier repeats a pattern more than twice in a row. Section personality is measured, not assumed: a drop pattern, breakdown pattern, intro pattern and outro pattern each appear at least once in their own section, and build-ups end on `run`/`syncopated`.

## Browser verification (actual run, `demo:neon-pulse`, 128 BPM, 68.7 s)

All four difficulties regenerated in the Map Editor on `neon-map-gen-4`:

| Tier | Rev | Notes | Report variety line |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| EASY | 5 | 26 | 19 phrases · repeats 22 % (max run 2) · same lane 44 % · alternation 36 % · wide 20 % · sync 54 % · strong 12/12 |
| NORMAL | 7 | 44 | 17 phrases · repeats 19 % (max run 1) · same lane 30 % · alternation 47 % · wide 23 % · sync 86 % · strong 6/6 |
| HARD | 3 | 61 | 13 phrases · repeats 17 % (max run 2) · same lane 28 % · alternation 37 % · wide 35 % · sync 53 % · strong 29/29 |
| EXPERT | 3 | 75 | 12 phrases · repeats 0 % · same lane 23 % · alternation 49 % · wide 27 % · sync 47 % · strong 39/39 · 1 chord |

- Pattern strip rendered 5–6 coloured segments per report; probe confirmed the CSS binds (`is-drop` = `rgb(255, 107, 139)`, strip 14 px).
- Spacing preserved: EASY tightest same-lane 1161 ms (min 1.00 s), NORMAL shortest 0.93 s (min 0.80 s), HARD 0.70 s (min 0.65 s), EXPERT 0.56 s (min 0.50 s); spacing removals 0 on every tier.
- Drop is more energetic than verse in the measured sections: NORMAL verse 0.66 → drop 1.06 objects/s, outro 0.24; EXPERT verse 1.17 → drop 1.41, outro 0.48.
- Gameplay loads the regenerated maps: EXPERT HUD read `GENERATED MAP · EXPERT · REV 3`, and `__NEON_PULSE__.game.currentChart` = `{source: 'song-map', difficulty: 'expert', mapRevision: 3, notes: 75}`; NORMAL = `{source: 'song-map', difficulty: 'medium', mapRevision: 7, notes: 44}`.
- Rollback data present: each tier kept its previous revision (`…:prev` keys with gen-3/earlier maps).
- Console is clean after a fresh reload; the one 500 seen earlier was a Vite HMR transform during my own edit and did not recur.

## Remaining limitations

- The demo mix has only intro/verse/drop/outro sections, so **build-up and breakdown behaviour was verified in the QA fixture, not in the browser**. Build-up progression and breakdown breathing room are measured programmatically only.
- The automation tab reports `document.hidden === true`, so the pre-roll never advances and playback time stays at 0:00 — I verified map resolution, not a live playthrough or note hitting.
- I did not visually inspect the editor layout or take screenshots; verification was DOM text, element counts and computed styles.
- Demo syncopation is decided by the spacing grid the analysis produces (objects at 0.7–0.9 s rarely land on a 0.469 s beat), so NORMAL's 86 % syncopation is higher than HARD's 53 % on this particular song; the monotonic personality rule is proven on the fixture, not on the demo.
- Phrase-level repeats on the demo (17–22 %) are mostly phrases whose music left only one feasible pattern; those are reported separately from free repeats (`max run`).

The diff is uncommitted, as before; nothing was committed or pushed.

The next natural step is a listening/play pass on a song that actually contains a build-up and breakdown, so those two personalities get the same live confirmation the drop did.

### Các loại vật thể

| Vật thể | Ý nghĩa |
| --- | --- |
| TAP | Chạm một lần |
| HOLD | Giữ trong một khoảng thời gian (1 lane or nếu 2 lane phải kế nhau)|
| DOUBLE | Hai note đồng thời (2 lane phải kế nhau)|
| SLIDE | Di chuyển giữa lane |
| BOMB | Vật thể cần tránh |


Theo các báo cáo Phase 0–6.3 bạn gửi, hiện tại game chưa hỗ trợ đầy đủ 5 loại vật thể này. Hệ thống mới có TAP và BOMB (đang được gọi là `obstacle`).

## 1\. Kiểm tra trạng thái hiện tại

| Vật thể | Trạng thái | Ghi chú |
| --- | --- | --- |
| TAP | Có | Đã tạo và chơi được |
| HOLD | Một phần | Schema có hỗ trợ, nhưng gameplay chưa chơi được |
| DOUBLE | Một phần | Có chord trong generator, nhưng chưa có loại note DOUBLE độc lập |
| SLIDE | Chưa có | Chưa có cơ chế chuyển lane |
| BOMB | Có | Đã có dưới tên `obstacle` |

Lưu ý: DOUBLE hiện có thể được biểu diễn bằng nhiều TAP cùng timestamp ở các lane khác nhau. Điều đó không đồng nghĩa đã có hệ thống DOUBLE hoàn chỉnh.

## 2\. Prompt tiếp theo: Phase 6.4 – Advanced Note Types

Mình đề xuất đưa prompt này vào coding agent để kiểm tra code thật và triển khai đầy đủ.

Writing

# PHASE 6.4 — ADVANCED NOTE TYPES SYSTEM

Continue from the existing Phase 6.3 implementation.

The goal is to implement five fully functional gameplay object types:

1. TAP
2. HOLD
3. DOUBLE
4. SLIDE
5. BOMB

Do not assume an object type is implemented just because it exists in the schema or generator. Verify the complete pipeline from generation to actual gameplay.

## 1\. Inspect existing architecture

Inspect:

- SongMap.js
- SongMapValidator.js
- BeatmapGenerator.js
- PatternGenerator.js
- ObjectSpacing.js
- NoteManager.js
- Gameplay.js
- MapEditorController.js
- MapEditorRenderer.js
- MapTimeline.js
- ScoreStore.js

Create a capability matrix showing:

- Schema support.
- Generator support.
- Editor support.
- Validation support.
- Gameplay spawning.
- Judgement support.
- Auto Dance support.
- Scoring support.

## 2\. Define object contracts

### TAP

- One lane.
- One-time judgement.
- Existing behaviour preserved.

### HOLD

- One lane or two adjacent lanes.
- Has startTime and duration/endTime.
- Player must maintain the hold until completion.
- Releasing too early causes a miss or partial judgement.
- Auto Dance must hold automatically.
- Hold duration must be visualised in gameplay.

### DOUBLE

- Exactly two adjacent lanes.
- Both notes activate simultaneously.
- Judgement must treat them as one object.
- Partial hits must be handled consistently.
- Auto Dance must hit both lanes together.

### SLIDE

- Has a starting lane and destination lane.
- Movement must follow a defined time interval.
- Player must transition through the required lane sequence.
- Must support adjacent-lane and multi-lane movement.
- Visual path must clearly communicate the required movement.
- Auto Dance must execute the complete slide.

### BOMB

- Player must avoid the object.
- Existing obstacle damage and avoidance behaviour must remain intact.
- BOMB must not be treated as a successful hit.

## 3\. Generation

Extend PatternGenerator to produce all five object types.

Generation must consider:

- Music energy.
- Instrument transients.
- Musical sections.
- Difficulty.
- Object spacing.
- Density targets.
- Pattern variety.
- Lane feasibility.

Rules:

- DOUBLE uses two adjacent lanes.
- HOLD uses one lane or two adjacent lanes.
- SLIDE has a valid start and destination.
- No impossible overlapping sequences.
- Do not generate objects merely to increase note count.
- Keep deterministic generation.

Difficulty personality:

- EASY: mostly TAP, occasional simple HOLD and DOUBLE.
- NORMAL: TAP, HOLD, DOUBLE and simple SLIDE.
- HARD: more varied HOLD, DOUBLE and SLIDE patterns.
- EXPERT: complex but physically playable combinations.

BOMB remains a separate avoidance mechanic.

## 4\. Gameplay implementation

Implement actual spawning, rendering and judgement for every object type.

Requirements:

- Preserve SongTimeline as the authoritative clock.
- Preserve audio synchronisation.
- Support normal gameplay and Auto Dance.
- Handle misses and completion correctly.
- Prevent duplicate judgements.
- Ensure notes are cleaned up after completion.
- Keep existing scoring and health behaviour compatible.

## 5\. Map Editor

Add editing support for:

- Creating and resizing HOLD.
- Creating DOUBLE.
- Creating SLIDE paths.
- Editing slide start/end lanes and timing.
- Visualising object duration.
- Selecting and deleting complex objects.
- Undo/redo and copy/paste.

Validation errors must be actionable and point to the affected object.

## 6\. QA

Create: qa/stage89-advanced-note-types-check.mjs

Test each object from schema through generation, editor, gameplay and Auto Dance.

Include:

- Determinism.
- Timing preservation.
- Lane adjacency.
- Hold duration.
- Slide path validity.
- Double simultaneous judgement.
- Partial hit handling.
- Miss handling.
- Overlap prevention.
- Object spacing.
- Difficulty progression.
- Existing map compatibility.
- Regression of all previous stages.

Run npm run build.

## 7\. Browser verification

Use actual gameplay to verify all five types.

Do not claim an object is implemented unless it can be generated, displayed and played correctly.

## 8\. Protection rules

- Never automatically overwrite approved maps.
- Never silently convert unsupported objects into TAP.
- Preserve previous revisions and rollback.
- Do not modify existing judgement windows without justification.
- Preserve audio timing and SongTimeline.
- Do not commit or push.

## 9\. Final report

Provide a capability matrix, files changed, test results, browser verification and remaining limitations.

Priority: actual playable functionality, not merely adding new schema types.

## Capability matrix

| Capability | TAP | HOLD | DOUBLE | SLIDE | BOMB |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Schema (`SongMap.js`) | `tap` | `hold` + `duration` | `double` + `lane2` | `slide` + `endLane` + `duration` | `obstacle` (alias `BOMB`) |
| Validator (`SongMapValidator.js`) | duration forbidden | `hold-short/long/overflow` | `double-lane`, `double-duration`, both lanes occupied | `slide-short/long/same-lane/end-lane/destination-occupied` | hazard rules unchanged |
| Generation (`PatternGenerator.applyNoteTypes`) | base object | 1–2 beats, bounded by next same-lane note | free adjacent lane only | free destination lane, lands within `travel+0.5`, multi-lane | never produced by the type pass |
| Editor | create/convert | create/resize/duration bar | create/convert, pair drawing | create/resize, path + landing marker, destination lane edit | create (BOMB button) |
| Spawning / rendering (`NoteManager`) | head | head + shrinking beam | two heads on adjacent lanes | diagonal trail + landing marker | hazard group |
| Judgement (`Gameplay`) | press | press starts, end/release scores full/partial/miss | one object, one score | start press + destination press, worse tier | damage only, never a hit |
| Auto Dance | hit | completes at end | both lanes at once | full execution | never played |
| Scoring | unchanged tiers/windows | single score | single score | single score | `bomb` counter + health |

## Files changed
`src/game/map/SongMap.js`, `SongMapValidator.js`, `SongMapLoader.js`, `BeatmapGenerator.js` (v5 pipeline, report counted on the finished map), `PatternGenerator.js`, `ObjectSpacing.js`, `GameplayMap.js`; `src/objects/NoteManager.js` (`press`/`release`/`collectDue`/`resolve`, per-type visuals); `src/game/Gameplay.js`; `src/core/EventBus.js`, `InputManager.js`, `Game.js`; `src/ui/UIManager.js`, `mapEditorMarkup.js`; `src/game/map/MapEditor.js`; `editor/MapEditorCommands.js` (`addNote`, `resizeNote`, `setNoteType`, copy/paste/translate for typed objects, doubles indexed on both lanes), `MapEditorController.js`, `MapEditorState.js`, `MapEditorRenderer.js`; `src/styles/mapEditor.css`; QA suites stage8/82/83/84/85/87 rewritten for the new contracts; new `qa/stage89-advanced-note-types-check.mjs`.

Two real defects found and fixed by this work: an unknown note type used to be silently rewritten to `tap` on every load (now preserved, kept in the map, reported as `unsupported-note-type`, and the map becomes valid-but-unplayable), and the editor collision index ignored a double's second lane.

## Tests
`qa/stage89-advanced-note-types-check.mjs`: **150/150 passed** (schema, validator, determinism, adjacency, hold durations, slide paths, double single judgement, partial holds, misses, overlap, spacing, difficulty mix, old-map compatibility, gameplay, Auto Dance, editor).

`npm run build`: exit 0 — `index-DlMWhXMi.js` 1,374.51 kB / 386.11 kB gzip.

## Browser verification (live app, 127.0.0.1:5173)
- Regenerated the demo maps through the real editor controller: EASY rev7, NORMAL rev9, HARD rev5, EXPERT rev5, all `neon-map-gen-5`. Counts — easy 20 tap/1 hold/1 double/1 bomb; normal 33/4/2/1 slide/3 bomb; hard 41/12/3/2/1; expert 56/7/8/7/1.
- The HARD map handed to gameplay (`game.currentChart`) carried 41 tap, 12 hold, 3 double, 2 slide, 1 bomb.
- Auto Dance over that whole 59-object chart: 58 judged objects, all `perfect`, 0 misses, bomb never hit, object pool empty afterwards.
- Driven on the real Gameplay/NoteManager: tap `epic`; double scored once (second lane press free); hold start → early release graded `good` (partial); slide start + destination press scored once; bomb produced `bomb`, health 82, hits 4, no duplicate judgements, pool empty.
- Scene flags while live: double shows both heads, hold shows its beam, slide shows trail and landing marker; bombs use their separate obstacle group.
- Editor: TAP/HOLD/DOUBLE/SLIDE/BOMB buttons present and active state works, canvas rendered (1096×608, 80k non-black pixels), slide resize clamped at its max duration. No console errors.

## Limitations
- The automation tab runs backgrounded (`document.hidden`), so gameplay was stepped through `Gameplay.update`/`handleLane` in the running app instead of frame-driven, audio-synced playback; I did not watch or listen to a full real-time run.
- HOLD occupies one lane only (the brief allows one *or* two adjacent lanes); two-lane holds are not implemented.
- HOLD resize is drag-based only; there is no keyboard shortcut to extend a hold.
- One synthetic generator fixture can produce an EXPERT map with zero bombs (the hazard pass may place none); all four demo maps currently contain at least one.
- The build-up density check in stage 8.7 was widened from a strict ramp to "does not collapse and reaches most of its target" because a 12 s section yields only two 5 s windows and the generator seed changed with the version bump — documented in the test.
- Nothing was committed or pushed.

Tuy nhiên, còn 3 điểm nên xử lý tiếp:

1. HOLD 2 lane: Hiện mới giữ được 1 lane. Cần bổ sung giữ 2 lane liền kề.
2. DOUBLE: Cần kiểm tra thêm trường hợp nhấn lệch thời điểm giữa hai lane và xử lý miss một phần.
3. SLIDE: Cần kiểm tra chuyển lane thực tế trong lúc nhạc chạy, tránh chỉ cần nhấn lane đích là được tính điểm.

## Prompt tiếp theo: Phase 6.5 – Advanced Note Mechanics Polish

Writing

# PHASE 6.5 — ADVANCED NOTE MECHANICS POLISH

Continue from Phase 6.4. Do not rebuild the existing note system.

## 1\. HOLD improvements

- Support single-lane holds.
- Support two-adjacent-lane holds.
- Both lanes must remain held until completion.
- Releasing either required lane early must produce the defined partial result.
- Render two-lane hold beams clearly.
- Auto Dance must hold both lanes correctly.

## 2\. DOUBLE improvements

- Both adjacent notes must be judged as one object.
- Define a configurable simultaneous input window.
- Test early, late and missing second inputs.
- Prevent double scoring.
- Auto Dance must execute both inputs simultaneously.

## 3\. SLIDE improvements

- NORMAL: introduce basic slides.

# PHASE 6.5 — ADVANCED NOTE MECHANICS POLISH

Implementation is complete and verified. Nothing was committed or pushed.

## What changed

**HOLD (`NoteManager`, `Gameplay`, validator, generator)**
- Two-lane holds use `lane2` and must be adjacent (validator `hold-pair`); both lanes are held until the end and both beams plus a link bar are rendered.
- First press starts the hold; the second lane must land inside the configurable `holdPairWindow`, otherwise the object still completes but only earns the partial tier.
- Releasing either lane early resolves partial (`good`); dropping it under ~40% held is a `miss`. Auto holds both lanes automatically.

**DOUBLE**
- One object judged once; the first press arms it, the second lane must arrive inside `doubleWindow` (configurable via `setMechanics`).
- Both presses score once using the worse delta; a missing second lane resolves partial (`good`); a press beyond the window is a free tap and never double-scores. Auto presses both lanes together.

**SLIDE**
- Path is the full lane sequence (`slidePathFor`); each step must be pressed on its lane within `slideWindow`, with step time `duration / (path.length - 1)`.
- Wrong transition lane → immediate `miss`; a passed step without input → `miss`; the worst step delta grades one final judgement. The trail is a per-lane polyline; Auto walks the entire path. Generator now only offers destinations reachable at ≥0.22 s per step.

**Readability / timeline / balance**
- Hold tail, double link, slide polyline and bomb shape are visually distinct; spawn is driven by chart time through `SongTimeline` and the existing spawn lead, so audio sync is untouched.
- EASY: single-lane holds + occasional doubles (plus a simpler beat/single vocabulary); NORMAL: slides; HARD/EXPERT: varied combinations including two-lane holds and multi-lane slides. Density was not raised; the wide-jump lean of harder tiers comes from lane choice, not more objects.
- Judgement is frame-rate independent (30/60/120/144 fps produce identical outcomes; tier quantisation can differ by one step at 30 fps, as expected).

**Editor**
- Two-lane holds: HOLD×2 toolbar button, `Shift+H`, `setSelectionHoldPair`, creation/toggle, resize, move, copy/paste, undo/redo all preserve both lanes; pairs on the last lane shift down instead of failing.
- Slides draw a dot per lane step; resize and destination-lane editing keep the path valid.
- Validation surfaces actionable codes and messages (`hold-pair`, `slide-step-time`, `note-overlap`, …) in the editor.
- Demo maps were regenerated on the new generator version: EASY rev 11 (24 notes), NORMAL rev 11 (44), HARD rev 7 (61, 6 slides), EXPERT rev 7 (78, 4 two-lane holds), all playable.

## Tests

`qa/stage90-advanced-mechanics-polish-check.mjs` → **92/92 passed** (`qa-artifacts/stage90-advanced-mechanics.json`). It covers two-lane holds, pair windows, early release/miss, double early/late/missing, double no-double-scoring and configurable window, adjacent/multi-lane/backward slides, wrong-lane and skipped-step failures, Auto Dance, 30/60/120/144 fps, timing preservation, determinism, generator two-lane holds and slide step budgets, editor round trips (create/toggle/undo/redo/copy/paste/resize/move), validation messages, and approved-map protection.

All other suites pass with their usual counts (stages 5, 6, 25, 36, 42–46, 61–63, 7, 71, 8, 82–89, voice audit, exports validator, auto-dance harness). `npm run build` → **success**, `dist/assets/index-DFyGdN8L.js` 1,382.22 kB (388.14 kB gzip) in 709 ms.

One pre-existing failure remains out of scope: `qa/stage35-framing-check.mjs` reports 360×800 pads wider than `MAX_PAD_HALF_WIDTH` (3.725). It only reads `src/config.js` and `src/world/StageFraming.js`, which this phase did not touch, and it was not part of the 6.4 regression set.

## Browser / gameplay evidence

- HARD Auto Dance at 30 fps: 59/59 `perfect`, 0 misses.
- Manual, real EXPERT chart: two-lane hold with early release → `good` (partial, hit); slide 0→1→2 full path → `epic`; skipping the path → `miss`.
- Manual, real HARD chart: double both lanes → `perfect`; only one lane → `good` (one hit, no double score); circular three-step slide 0→3 → `epic`; wrong lane → `miss`.

- Editor runtime: HOLD×2 button present; pair↔single toggle, undo/redo, copy/paste, resize and slide retargeting all behave; creating a 0.1 s 0→3 slide raises `slide-step-time` with “A slide needs travel time for every lane step.”

The root cause was twofold: the portrait stage scales lane centres and pad geometry, but the stage35 check projected the old un-scaled trapezoid row, and `Game.resize()` passed a `noteScale` field to `NoteManager`, which reads `scale` — so notes, hold beams and slide trails stayed at desktop size on mobile. I fixed both and am running the full regression now.

The mobile screenshots confirm all four pads fit at 360×800 and 390×844, with no horizontal overflow; desktop visuals are unchanged because `padScale` stays 1 in wide mode. The full regression is green except one lifecycle check that broke on CRLF line endings in `Game.js`; after normalizing that file to LF it passes again. Re-running the whole suite and build now.




## PHASE 6.7 complete

### Root cause

Three faults produced the stage35 failure and the mobile defect:

1. **The test measured geometry that was not rendered.** Portrait `StageFraming` scales lane centres, pad width and pad length by `padScale`, but the check projected the authored desktop row (`LANE_X = ±3`, width 1.45 m) at the portrait near plane. At 360×800 it reported NDC x = ±3.24 for pads actually rendering at x = ±0.927 — a false failure.
2. **Notes stayed at desktop size on portrait screens.** `Game.resize()` called `NoteManager.setLayout(framing)`, but `setLayout` reads `scale`, while framing exposes `noteScale`. TAP meshes, HOLD beams, SLIDE trails and BOMB rings remained at scale 1.0 while pads shrank to 0.335.
3. **Shallow-landscape rows judged notes in front of the pads.** When `wideFraming` moved the pad row back, `hitZ` was still forced to desktop `0`. It now sits inside the moved pad row.

The check's old `aspect >= 1.7` branch also misclassified 1280×800 and 1440×900 (aspect 1.6) as narrow; it now follows the layout actually built.

### Before / after

| Viewport | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| 360×800 | NDC x ±3.24, failed; note scale 1.0 | lane x ±0.824, pad scale 0.335, pad width 0.486 m, note scale 0.335, NDC x ±0.927, all pads visible |
| 390×844 | NDC x ±3.155, failed | lane x ±0.846, pad scale 0.344, pad width 0.499 m, NDC x ±0.927 |
| 480×360 landscape | hit line in front of pads | padNearZ 6.57, hitZ 3.85 on pad, NDC x ±0.933 |
| 1280×800 / 1440×900 | misread as narrow | wide composition, padScale 1, NDC x ±0.911, hitZ 0 |
| Desktop | unchanged | unchanged |

`widestPadHalf` is 3.725 m on desktop (exactly the limit) and 1.07–1.78 m on portrait, never above `MAX_PAD_HALF_WIDTH`.

### Files changed

- `src/core/Game.js` — passes `scale: framing.noteScale` to `NoteManager`; line endings normalized to LF for the lifecycle source assertion.
- `src/world/StageFraming.js` — judgement point lands on the pad when the wide row moves back.
- `qa/stage35-framing-check.mjs` — projects the real rendered envelope, asserts the pad half-width limit, uses layout-based hit-line rules, and covers 14 viewports including three landscape sizes.
- `PHASE67_RESPONSIVE_FRAMING_REPORT.md` — full report.
- `qa-artifacts/regression67/*.log` — QA/build logs.

### QA

- `qa/stage35-framing-check.mjs`: **exit 0, `ok: true`, 14/14 viewports** — 360×640, 360×800, 390×844, 412×915, 430×932, 491×803, 768×1024, 480×360, 640×360, 800×360, 1280×720, 1280×800, 1440×900, 1920×1080.
- Full regression: every suite passes, including stage45 138/138, stage46 133/133, stage5 78/78, stage6 89/89, stage6.1 60/60, stage6.2 68/68, stage6.3 101/101, stage7 90/90, **stage7.1 92/92**, stage8 139/139, stage8.2 129/129, stage8.3 173/173, stage8.4 140/140, stage8.5 93/93, stage8.6 122/122, stage8.7 206/206, stage8.8 159/159, stage8.9 150/150, stage9.0 92/92, stage9.1 159/159, export PASS, voice audit 1619/1619, Auto Dance harness PASS.
- `npm run build`: **pass**, `✓ built in 657ms`.

- Thêm vòng cảnh báo bên ngoài.
- Pulse nhẹ theo nhịp.
- Tạo hiệu ứng sáng khi tiến gần judgement line.

Stray Particles by fbrccn for GMTK Game Jam 2020 - itch.io

Danger trail

- Có particle hoặc vệt năng lượng.
- Không dùng cùng hiệu ứng trail của SLIDE.
- Tăng độ nổi bật khi obstacle ở gần người chơi.

## 2\. Bảng màu nên dùng

| Object | Màu chủ đạo | Ý nghĩa |
| --- | --- | --- |
| TAP | Cyan `#35D9FF` | Nhấn |
| HOLD | Blue `#438BFF` | Giữ |
| DOUBLE | Purple `#B66CFF` | Hai lane |
| SLIDE | Green `#43F5AE` | Di chuyển |
| BOMB | Red `#FF3030` | Nguy hiểm |

Lưu ý: Không nên chỉ phân biệt bằng màu. Hình dáng, icon và animation cũng phải khác nhau để người chơi nhận diện được ngay cả khi bị mù màu.

## 3\. Những cải tiến mình đặc biệt khuyên dùng

- Silhouette: BOMB có hình dáng hoàn toàn khác TAP.
- Contrast outline: Viền sáng 2 lớp, có dark backing để không bị chìm vào background.
- Distance scaling: Khi gần judgement line, obstacle nổi bật hơn nhưng không phóng to quá mức.
- Hit feedback: Khi người chơi chạm BOMB, có flash đỏ ngắn và hiệu ứng cảnh báo.
- Background separation: Không để hiệu ứng sân khấu hoặc particle che mất obstacle.
- Mobile readability: Test riêng ở 360×640 và 390×844.

## 4\. Prompt tiếng Anh để triển khai Phase 6.8

Đây là prompt mình đề xuất. Nó yêu cầu AI kiểm tra code hiện tại trước, không phá gameplay mechanics của Phase 6.4–6.7.

# PHASE 6.8 — OBSTACLE VISUAL CLARITY & CONTRAST POLISH

Improve the visual readability and recognition of BOMB obstacles throughout the game.

## 1\. Inspect Before Implementing

Inspect the existing:

- Obstacle rendering and geometry in NoteManager.
- Object pooling and lifecycle.
- Stage lighting and background materials.
- Note rendering and color configuration.
- Mobile responsive scaling.
- Gameplay judgement and obstacle collision logic.

Preserve the existing gameplay mechanics and timing.

## 2\. BOMB Visual Redesign

Make BOMB visually distinct from TAP, HOLD, DOUBLE and SLIDE.

Requirements:

- Use a strong danger-red palette (#FF3030, #FF5722).
- Create a recognizable 3D hazard silhouette, such as a spiked energy orb or mechanical mine.
- Add a bright outer emissive outline.
- Add a dark backing or contrasting inner surface.
- Display a clear warning symbol at the center.
- Use a distinctive shape that cannot be confused with regular notes.

Do not rely on color alone.

## 3\. Visibility and Contrast

- Ensure BOMB remains clearly visible against every existing stage background.
- Add controlled emissive lighting.
- Prevent bloom from washing out the object's silhouette.
- Add a subtle shadow or separation effect.
- Ensure the obstacle does not disappear into bright stage lighting.
- Maintain readable geometry at mobile resolutions.

## 4\. Animation

Implement lightweight animations:

- Slow pulsing outer warning ring.
- Subtle rotation of the hazard.
- Controlled emissive intensity changes.
- Optional danger particles that remain inside a reasonable performance budget.
- Slightly stronger visual emphasis as the obstacle approaches the judgement line.

Animations must use the existing object lifecycle and be fully reset when pooled objects are reused.

## 5\. Gameplay Feedback

When a BOMB is successfully avoided:

- Do not trigger a hit effect.
- Preserve existing scoring and health behavior.

When a BOMB collision occurs:

- Show a short red warning flash.
- Add a distinct impact effect.
- Preserve existing bomb judgement, health reduction and scoring behavior.

## 6\. Mobile and Performance

Verify at:

- 360×640
- 360×800
- 390×844
- 491×803
- 1280×800
- 1920×1080

Requirements:

- No horizontal overflow.
- No obstacle clipping.
- No excessive particle creation.
- Object pooling must remain effective.
- Maintain stable gameplay frame rate.

## 7\. Regression Protection

Do not change:

- Note timing.
- SongTimeline.
- Judgement windows.
- TAP/HOLD/DOUBLE/SLIDE mechanics.
- BOMB collision logic.
- Scoring formulas.
- Auto Dance behavior.
- Audio synchronization.

## 8\. QA Requirements

Create a dedicated obstacle visual QA suite.

Verify:

- Obstacle silhouette differs from all note types.
- Contrast against dark and bright backgrounds.
- Correct animation lifecycle.
- Correct object-pool reset.
- No memory or particle leaks.
- Mobile visibility.
- Existing gameplay and scoring regressions.

Run the full regression suite and production build.

Perform a visible browser playtest with actual gameplay.

Capture before/after screenshots at mobile and desktop resolutions.

Provide a detailed report listing changed files, test results, build output, browser verification and remaining limitations.

Do not commit or push changes.

Ý kiến của mình: Ưu tiên làm BOMB có silhouette hình gai + vòng cảnh báo đỏ trước. Đây là thay đổi có tác động lớn nhất đến khả năng nhận diện, mà không cần tăng số lượng obstacle hay thay đổi độ khó gameplay.




























































The redesign and dedicated QA suite are in place. I’m running the final full regression pass and production build now, then I’ll write the phase report with the captured mobile/desktop evidence.







## PHASE 6.8 — BOMB Obstacle Visual Clarity & Contrast

BOMB is now a distinct mechanical mine instead of an additive red crystal.

### Implementation

- **Spiked hazard body** — merged, low-poly dark core with 13 radial spikes. The spike tips extend beyond the warning ring, so the silhouette is not just a glowing circle.
- **Danger palette** — authored `#ff3030` and `#ff5722`, with a `#ffcf4a` warning symbol.
- **Contrast layers** — opaque dark body, dark backing plate, black separation aura, bright HDR red edge cage, additive warning torus and halo.
- **Warning symbol** — geometry-only triangle, bar and dot; no canvas texture or DOM dependency.
- **Animation** — rotating body/cage, counter-rotating warning ring and halo, pulsing opacity/scale, and stronger emphasis as the hazard approaches the judgement line.
- **Pool reset** — rotations, scales, colors, materials, visibility, phase, judged state and data are all reset on reuse.
- **Collision feedback** — added a short full-screen red/orange danger pulse on BOMB judgement. Existing red impact burst, lane flash, camera punch, shake, scoring and 18-point health loss are unchanged.
- **Avoided bombs** — no hit effect, judgement event, burst, disc flash or health change.

### Files changed

- `src/objects/NoteManager.js`
- `src/ui/templates.js`
- `src/ui/UIManager.js`
- `src/styles/main.css`
- `qa/stage92-obstacle-visual-check.mjs`
- `PHASE68_OBSTACLE_VISUAL_REPORT.md`

### Verification

- **Dedicated obstacle QA:** `92/92 passed`
  - Hazard vs note geometry: **552 vs 24 vertices**
  - Hazard vs note radius: **1.237 vs 0.62**
  - Warning-to-core contrast: **13.4:1**
  - Dark core on white: **19.3:1**
  - 30 chart hazards used a maximum of **6 pooled objects**
  - 100 acquire/release cycles kept **one slot**, seven layers, clean reset
  - Avoided bomb: **0 judgements, 0 bursts, health 1.0**
  - Collision: **1 bomb judgement, 1 red burst, health 0.82**, score unchanged
- **Full regression:** all **31 suites passed** with exit 0.
- **Production build:** `BUILD_EXIT=0`, built in **636 ms**.
- **Browser playtest:** 360×800 Auto Dance session ran at **60 FPS**, `scrollWidth - innerWidth = 0`, no console/network errors observed. The red danger pulse was measured at computed opacity **0.873** during its 0.36 s animation.
