## Phase tiếp theo mình đề xuất: Phase 6.9

Thay vì tiếp tục chỉ cải thiện BOMB, nên nâng cấp visual readability cho toàn bộ 5 loại vật thể.

Mục tiêu là người chơi nhìn trong khoảng 0.1–0.2 giây đã hiểu phải làm gì:

- TAP: chạm.
- HOLD: giữ.
- DOUBLE: chạm hai lane.
- SLIDE: đi theo đường.
- BOMB: né.

Một điểm quan trọng nữa: cần kiểm tra độ dễ nhận diện khi nhiều loại vật thể xuất hiện cùng lúc, không chỉ kiểm tra từng vật thể riêng lẻ.

Mình đề xuất Phase 6.9 tập trung vào ba việc:

1. Visual hierarchy: hình dáng, màu sắc, hiệu ứng của 5 loại không bị nhầm lẫn.
2. Approach readability: vật thể càng gần judgement line càng dễ nhận diện.
3. Mobile readability: không để hiệu ứng, HUD hoặc vật thể chồng lấp làm mất thông tin.

Mình khuyên chưa nên tăng độ khó hoặc mật độ note. Hãy làm cho game dễ đọc trước, rồi mới cân bằng gameplay tiếp.



Đây là prompt tiếng Anh cho PHASE 6.9 — Universal Note Visual Readability & Gameplay Clarity. Prompt yêu cầu AI kiểm tra toàn bộ 5 loại vật thể, cải thiện khả năng nhận diện và giữ nguyên gameplay mechanics hiện tại.

Writing

# PHASE 6.9 — UNIVERSAL NOTE VISUAL READABILITY & GAMEPLAY CLARITY

## Objective

Following the successful completion of Phase 6.8, improve the visual readability, recognition speed, and visual consistency of all five gameplay object types:

1. TAP
2. HOLD
3. DOUBLE
4. SLIDE
5. BOMB

The goal is to make every object immediately recognizable, even during fast gameplay, overlapping visual effects, bright backgrounds, and small mobile screens.

This phase focuses on visual communication, not increasing gameplay difficulty.

## 1\. Inspect the Existing Rendering Architecture

Before implementing changes, inspect:

- `src/objects/NoteManager.js`
- Note geometry and material definitions.
- Object pooling and lifecycle management.
- HOLD beam and tail rendering.
- DOUBLE lane connections.
- SLIDE path and landing markers.
- BOMB rendering from Phase 6.8.
- Stage lighting, background effects and post-processing.
- Mobile scaling and `StageFraming`.
- Existing judgement and impact feedback.

Reuse existing rendering infrastructure wherever possible.

Do not create unnecessary duplicate rendering systems.

## 2\. Establish a Clear Visual Identity for Every Object

Each object must have a unique combination of:

- Silhouette.
- Primary color.
- Secondary accent.
- Surface details.
- Animation behavior.
- Visual interaction indicators.

Suggested visual direction:

### TAP — Cyan

- Compact, clean geometric shape.
- Strong cyan outer edge.
- Clear central target.
- Minimal animation.
- Immediately communicates a single press.

### HOLD — Blue

- Clearly visible starting head.
- Continuous beam connecting the head to the endpoint.
- Distinct tail or duration indicator.
- Two-lane HOLD must visibly connect both lanes.
- The remaining duration must be visually understandable.

### DOUBLE — Purple

- Two clearly separated note heads.
- Bright connecting element between both lanes.
- Both targets must be visible simultaneously.
- Make it visually obvious that both lanes must be pressed.

### SLIDE — Green

- Distinctive directional trail.
- Clearly visible lane transition path.
- Number or marker for each required step when appropriate.
- Landing point must stand out.
- Avoid visual similarity with HOLD beams.

### BOMB — Red

Preserve the Phase 6.8 mechanical mine redesign.

Do not regress:

- Spiked silhouette.
- Warning symbol.
- Contrast layers.
- Danger ring.
- Approach animation.
- Collision feedback.

Only make additional changes if testing identifies a genuine readability problem.

## 3\. Improve Visual Hierarchy

Implement a consistent visual hierarchy:

- Object silhouette must remain recognizable without relying on color.
- Main object geometry must be more visually prominent than particles.
- Important gameplay indicators must never be hidden behind bloom.
- Use controlled emissive intensity.
- Avoid excessive glow and overlapping light effects.
- Preserve sufficient contrast against both bright and dark environments.

Do not make every object equally bright.

Prioritize gameplay information over decorative effects.

## 4\. Distance-Based Readability

Improve how objects communicate their approach toward the judgement line.

As objects approach the player:

- Their silhouette should become easier to identify.
- Important lane indicators should become clearer.
- HOLD duration and SLIDE direction must remain readable.
- BOMB warning elements may become more prominent.
- Avoid sudden changes in object scale.
- Avoid animations that distract from timing.

Use existing chart-time and world-position calculations.

Do not change note travel speed or timing.

## 5\. Visual Clarity During Complex Situations

Test situations involving:

- TAP appearing alongside HOLD.
- DOUBLE overlapping visually with another note.
- SLIDE crossing multiple lanes.
- BOMB appearing near regular notes.
- Two-lane HOLD appearing with other objects.
- Multiple objects approaching the judgement line simultaneously.

Ensure that:

- Objects do not become visually indistinguishable.
- Important indicators do not overlap unnecessarily.
- Lane ownership remains obvious.
- The player can identify the required action immediately.

Do not solve readability problems by deleting valid gameplay objects.

## 6\. Judgement Feedback

Improve visual feedback consistency for:

- Perfect.
- Excellent.
- Good.
- Miss.
- Bomb collision.

Each judgement should have a recognizable visual response.

Requirements:

- Feedback must be brief.
- Avoid obscuring approaching objects.
- Avoid excessive full-screen flashes.
- Preserve existing scoring and health behavior.
- Avoid introducing distracting effects during critical gameplay moments.

## 7\. Mobile Optimization

Verify visual readability at:

- 360×640
- 360×800
- 390×844
- 412×915
- 491×803
- 768×1024

Also verify desktop at:

- 1280×800
- 1440×900
- 1920×1080

Requirements:

- No horizontal overflow.
- No clipped note geometry.
- No unreadable HOLD or SLIDE indicators.
- No overlapping HUD elements covering gameplay objects.
- All five object types must remain recognizable at small screen sizes.

Do not simply shrink all geometry proportionally if that makes important indicators unreadable.

## 8\. Performance and Object Pooling

Maintain the existing performance budget.

- Reuse pooled objects.
- Avoid unnecessary geometry creation during gameplay.
- Avoid per-frame allocations.
- Dispose of resources correctly.
- Reset all animation state when objects return to the pool.
- Prevent particle and material leaks.
- Maintain stable 60 FPS on supported mobile test devices.

## 9\. Dedicated QA

Create:

`qa/stage93-note-visual-readability-check.mjs`

Cover:

- Five-type silhouette distinction.
- Color and luminance contrast.
- HOLD single-lane and two-lane readability.
- DOUBLE lane separation.
- SLIDE path visibility.
- BOMB Phase 6.8 visual preservation.
- Bright and dark background contrast.
- Overlapping object scenarios.
- Distance-based visual transitions.
- Pool acquire/release/reset.
- Mobile scaling.
- Performance and memory behavior.
- Existing gameplay compatibility.

Use measurable assertions where possible instead of subjective visual claims.

## 10\. Real Browser Verification

Perform actual visible gameplay tests.

Requirements:

- Run real charts containing all five object types.
- Test EASY, NORMAL, HARD and EXPERT.
- Test both portrait and desktop layouts.
- Capture screenshots of each object type.
- Capture at least one complex scene containing multiple object types simultaneously.
- Inspect objects at different distances from the judgement line.
- Verify that visual feedback does not obscure gameplay.

Do not claim a live gameplay test if only synthetic engine calls were performed.

## 11\. Regression Protection

Do not modify:

- SongTimeline.
- Audio synchronization.
- Note timestamps.
- Judgement windows.
- Scoring formulas.
- Health calculations.
- Gameplay mechanics.
- Generator density targets.
- Object spacing rules.
- Pattern generation.
- Map validation.
- Map revision identity.
- Auto Dance timing.

If any change to these systems is genuinely necessary, document it and add dedicated regression tests.

Run the complete existing regression suite and production build.

## 12\. Final Report

Create:

`PHASE69_VISUAL_READABILITY_REPORT.md`

Include:

- Root problems identified.
- Before/after visual comparisons.
- Every modified file.
- Design decisions for each object type.
- QA results.
- Full regression results.
- Production build output.
- Browser screenshots.
- Performance measurements.
- Known limitations.

Do not commit or push changes.

## Final Acceptance Criteria

Phase 6.9 is complete only when:

1. All five objects are immediately distinguishable.
2. Gameplay actions are visually understandable without relying on color alone.
3. HOLD, DOUBLE and SLIDE remain readable during complex scenes.
4. BOMB retains its Phase 6.8 visual identity.
5. Mobile and desktop layouts pass.
6. Object pooling remains stable.
7. Existing gameplay mechanics remain unchanged.
8. Dedicated QA, full regression and production build pass.

Prioritize gameplay readability over visual decoration.

Một yêu cầu mình muốn AI đặc biệt chú ý: Đừng làm tất cả note sáng rực như BOMB. TAP/HOLD/DOUBLE/SLIDE cần có visual hierarchy riêng; nếu mọi thứ đều phát sáng mạnh thì cuối cùng người chơi vẫn không phân biệt được gì.



I’m auditing the current object visuals and lifecycle first, then I’ll add the new identity layers, Stage 9.3 QA, full regression, and a real browser playtest with screenshots.





















































































The five object types now have separate body hues, front-face identity glyphs, and lane-colour outer rings; HOLD gains visible end caps, DOUBLE gets a bright connector and second lane ring, and SLIDE gets step pips plus a direction chevron. I’m encoding those distinctions in the Stage 9.3 suite next.



































































PHASE 6.9 is complete.

## What changed

- `src/objects/NoteManager.js` now gives every playable object a non-colour identity:
  - **TAP** — cyan compact body, four-sided target ring, centre dot.
  - **HOLD** — blue beam, front pause bars, moving end caps, two-lane beam and end connector.
  - **DOUBLE** — purple two-head object with a bright lane connector, second lane ring and separate dark contrast discs.
  - **SLIDE** — green directional chevron, green quad-strip path ribbon, step pips and bright landing marker.
  - **BOMB** — Phase 6.8 mine preserved unchanged.
- Type colour now identifies the action; the existing lane-coloured outer ring identifies lane ownership.
- Added a dark separation disc behind every normal note for contrast against bright stages.
- Added subtle distance-based emphasis without changing travel speed, timing or scale jumps.
- Kept object pooling, shared materials and teardown behavior. Normal notes now have 20 pooled layers; SLIDE trail, steps and ribbon geometries are disposed correctly.

## Verification

- **Stage 6.9 QA:** `158/158 passed`
  - Five-type silhouette signatures
  - Contrast and luminance checks
  - HOLD beam shrink and duration caps
  - DOUBLE separation
  - SLIDE path, steps, arrow and landing marker
  - BOMB Phase 6.8 preservation
  - Overlap and lane ownership scenarios
  - Nine required viewports
  - Pool acquire/release/reset
  - 600-frame hot-loop resource and memory checks
  - Auto Dance gameplay compatibility
- **Stage 6.8 BOMB regression:** `92/92 passed`
- **Full regression:** all **32 suites** exited `0`
- **Production build:** `✓ built in 650ms` with Vite 8.3.8
- **Measured hot loop:** `0.02 ms/frame`, 68 geometries and 17 materials stable, heap growth `+4.49 MB` over 600 frames.

## Browser evidence

Real Quick Demo medium run through the visible menu: `playing`, 42 notes, including all five object types. EASY, HARD and EXPERT real demo charts were also started and their generated charts inspected: EASY 25 notes, HARD 58, EXPERT 79. EASY’s generated demo chart contains no SLIDE, so the all-five-type visual proof comes from the dedicated live-engine frames and the NORMAL/HARD/EXPERT chart counts.

![Complex scene mobile](qa-artifacts/stage69-complex-all-types-360x800.png)

![Complex scene desktop](qa-artifacts/stage69-complex-all-types-1280x800.png)

## Limitations

- The browser panel became background-hidden during late FPS profiling, so I am not claiming a sustained HARD/EXPERT renderer FPS measurement.
- No manual lane-key scored run was performed; Auto Dance was used to verify judgement feedback and BOMB immunity.
- TAP and SLIDE have low direct body-versus-white contrast and rely on the dark backing, bright glyph/ribbon and lane ring in very bright scenes.
- The desktop evidence is a raw engine canvas capture without DOM HUD.

Full details, per-type screenshots and the complete QA/regression/build output are in `PHASE69_VISUAL_READABILITY_REPORT.md`.

No commit or push was made.

## 3. Mình phát hiện 4 việc vẫn cần cải thiện

Dựa trên chính báo cáo của OpenCode:

| Vấn đề | Đánh giá |
| --- | --- |
| TAP và SLIDE tương phản thấp trên nền trắng | Cần cải thiện |
| Chưa có manual scored run | Cần kiểm chứng |
| Chưa đo FPS ổn định HARD/EXPERT | Cần kiểm chứng |
| Desktop screenshot thiếu DOM HUD | Cần bổ sung |

Mình đặc biệt quan tâm vấn đề thứ nhất.

Một rhythm game tốt phải giúp người chơi nhận biết note trong tích tắc, ngay cả khi:

- Nền có hiệu ứng Bloom mạnh.
- Màn hình có nhiều particle.
- Note di chuyển nhanh.
- Có nhiều note chồng lấn.
- Người chơi sử dụng màn hình điện thoại nhỏ.

### 1. Đường chơi gồm 4 lane

- Lane 1: ngoài cùng bên trái.
- Lane 2: giữa bên trái.
- Lane 3: giữa bên phải.
- Lane 4: ngoài cùng bên phải.

Mỗi lane có đường kẻ dọc phát sáng để phân biệt. Đường kẻ chạy xuyên suốt từ xa đến gần theo phối cảnh 3D.

### 2. Vật thể phải bay đúng lane

Rhythm Journey – Review – Higher Plain Games

[higherplaingames.com](<https://higherplaingames.com/pc/rhythm-journey-review/?utm_source=chatgpt.com>)

Nguyên tắc quan trọng:

- Note lane 1 chỉ bay trên lane 1.
- Note lane 2 chỉ bay trên lane 2.
- Note lane 3 chỉ bay trên lane 3.
- Note lane 4 chỉ bay trên lane 4.
- Không được tự ý lệch sang lane bên cạnh.
- Khi note đến vạch phán định, nó phải nằm chính giữa lane tương ứng.

### 3. Quy tắc hiển thị

| Thành phần | Thiết kế |
| --- | --- |
| Lane divider | Đường neon dọc |
| Lane boundary | Giới hạn rõ ràng |
| TAP | Nằm chính giữa lane |
| HOLD | Nằm dọc trong lane |
| DOUBLE | Hai lane đồng thời |
| SLIDE | Di chuyển theo đường nối giữa lane |
| BOMB | Nằm trong lane cần né |
| Judgement Line | Vạch ngang chung cho 4 lane |

### 4. Cách triển khai kỹ thuật

Mình đề xuất sửa Three.js theo nguyên tắc:

js

```js
const LANE_COUNT = 4;
const LANE_WIDTH = 2.0;
const LANE_SPACING = 2.0;

function getLaneX(lane) {
  return (lane - 1.5) * LANE_SPACING;
}
```

Với lane đánh số từ 0 đến 3:

js

```js
// Lane 0: -3
// Lane 1: -1
// Lane 2:  1
// Lane 3:  3
```

Tất cả note phải lấy tọa độ X từ `getLaneX(lane)`, không tự tính vị trí riêng.

Đặc biệt, cần dùng chung hệ tọa độ này cho:

- Lane renderer.
- Note spawner.
- Note movement.
- Hit detection.
- HOLD và SLIDE.
- BOMB collision.

Như vậy đường kẻ và vật thể sẽ luôn khớp nhau.

# TASK: REDESIGN GAMEPLAY TRACK – FIXED 4-LANE SYSTEM

## Project

NEON PULSE / 3danceYTApp

## Objective

Redesign the existing gameplay track into a clean, fixed 4-lane rhythm highway.

The game must have exactly 4 parallel lanes with clearly visible neon divider lines. All gameplay objects must travel strictly inside their assigned lanes.

IMPORTANT: Modify the existing project. Do NOT rebuild the game from scratch. Preserve all existing gameplay mechanics, music engine, scoring, effects, UI and settings.

## 1\. FOUR-LANE TRACK DESIGN

Create exactly 4 fixed, parallel lanes:

- Lane 0: Far Left
- Lane 1: Center Left
- Lane 2: Center Right
- Lane 3: Far Right

Requirements:

- Four lanes must have equal width.
- Lanes must be perfectly parallel.
- Add visible vertical neon divider lines between lanes.
- Add clear outer boundaries on both sides.
- Add subtle floor/grid details.
- Use a futuristic dark neon visual style.
- Lane boundaries must remain visible from the far distance to the judgement line.
- Use perspective projection to create depth.
- Avoid excessive glow that makes lane boundaries blurry.

## 2\. LANE POSITION SYSTEM

Create one centralized lane coordinate system.

Example:

js

```js
const LANE_COUNT = 4;
const LANE_WIDTH = 2.0;

function getLaneX(lane) {
  return (lane - 1.5) * LANE_WIDTH;
}
```

All gameplay objects MUST use this centralized coordinate function.

Never calculate lane positions independently in different gameplay modules.

Validate that lane indices are integers from 0 to 3.

## 3\. NOTE POSITIONING

All notes must be positioned precisely at the center of their assigned lane.

Requirements:

- TAP: centered in one lane.
- HOLD: remains inside its assigned lane throughout its entire length.
- DOUBLE: occupies two distinct lanes simultaneously.
- SLIDE: follows a clearly visible path between assigned lanes.
- BOMB: stays centered in its assigned lane.

No note may randomly drift between lanes.

No note may visually overlap an adjacent lane unless the gameplay mechanic explicitly requires a transition, such as SLIDE.

## 4\. NOTE MOVEMENT

All notes must travel along the same forward direction toward the judgement line.

Requirements:

- Maintain constant lane X position during normal travel.
- Only SLIDE may transition between lanes.
- Ensure perspective scaling is consistent.
- Ensure notes do not leave their lane boundaries.
- Ensure the note's collision position matches its rendered position.

## 5\. JUDGEMENT LINE

Create one horizontal judgement line across all four lanes.

Requirements:

- Clearly visible.
- Consistent with the neon theme.
- All notes must reach the correct judgement position.
- Maintain accurate audio synchronization.
- Preserve existing PERFECT / GREAT / GOOD / MISS judgement logic.

## 6\. INPUT AND COLLISION

Preserve and verify the existing keyboard input system.

Each lane must have a clearly defined input mapping.

Requirements:

- Input lane must match note lane.
- Hit detection must use the centralized lane system.
- HOLD must validate both lane and hold duration.
- DOUBLE must validate both required lanes.
- SLIDE must validate its lane transition sequence.
- BOMB must detect interaction in the correct lane.

Do not introduce timing regressions.

## 7\. VISUAL DESIGN

Visual direction:

- Dark futuristic tunnel.
- Cyan and blue neon lane boundaries.
- Subtle purple ambient lighting.
- Clear lane separation.
- Slightly glowing floor.
- Readable notes with distinct colors.
- Consistent 3D perspective.

The four lanes must be immediately recognizable even when multiple notes appear simultaneously.

Avoid unnecessary visual clutter.

## 8\. PERFORMANCE

- Reuse existing Object Pool.
- Reuse geometries and materials.
- Avoid creating new objects every frame.
- Use InstancedMesh where appropriate.
- Preserve smooth rendering at approximately 60 FPS.
- Avoid unnecessary post-processing.

## 9\. REGRESSION TESTING

Test all five existing note types:

1. TAP
2. HOLD
3. DOUBLE
4. SLIDE
5. BOMB

Verify:

- All notes stay in their assigned lanes.
- All four lanes have equal width.
- Lane divider lines align with note positions.
- No note drifts into adjacent lanes.
- Judgement line alignment is correct.
- Keyboard input matches the correct lane.
- Audio timing remains accurate.
- Existing score, combo and health systems still work.

Test NORMAL, HARD and EXPERT.

Test both desktop and mobile layouts.

## 10\. IMPLEMENTATION RULES

- Inspect existing source code before editing.
- Identify all modules responsible for lane positioning and note rendering.
- Reuse existing architecture wherever possible.
- Avoid duplicating lane coordinate calculations.
- Do not remove existing features.
- Do not replace the audio engine.
- Do not change existing scoring rules unless required to fix a confirmed bug.
- Keep code modular and maintainable.

## 11\. FINAL DELIVERABLE

After implementation:

1. Summarize all modified files.
2. Explain the new 4-lane coordinate system.
3. Provide screenshots of the new gameplay.
4. Report test results for all five note types.
5. Report any remaining issues.
6. Confirm that existing gameplay and music synchronization remain functional.

SUCCESS CRITERION:

The final game must display exactly four clearly separated, fixed parallel lanes. Every gameplay object must travel precisely inside its assigned lane, creating a clean, readable and professional rhythm-game experience.

Lưu ý của mình: Có một điểm rất quan trọng trong prompt này: không chỉ vẽ thêm ba đường kẻ để chia lane, mà phải đồng bộ lại tọa độ giữa `Lane Renderer`, `Note Spawner`, `Note Movement` và `Hit Detection`.

The codebase already has four lanes, but the lane centres are duplicated across framing, pads, effects and a legacy road shader, and the current stage has no explicit lane borders or judgement bar. ## Logic hiệu ứng DOUBLE cần sửa

Khi người chơi đánh DOUBLE:

1. Kiểm tra cả hai lane.
2. Cả hai lane phải được xác nhận HIT.
3. Kích hoạt hai hiệu ứng Impact độc lập.
4. Hai hiệu ứng bắt đầu cùng timestamp.
5. Hai vòng năng lượng, particle và ánh sáng xuất hiện đồng thời.
6. Hiệu ứng PERFECT / EPIC hiển thị chung ở giữa hai lane, không hiển thị lệch một bên.

Đặc biệt, không được chỉ gọi `spawnImpact(lane)` một lần. Phải xử lý cả `lane1` và `lane2`.

## Prompt gửi OpenCode sửa lỗi

Writing

# BUG FIX – DOUBLE NOTE MUST TRIGGER TWO SIMULTANEOUS HIT EFFECTS

## Problem

In NEON PULSE, DOUBLE notes occupy two lanes simultaneously.

However, when a DOUBLE note reaches the judgement line and is successfully hit, the visual impact effect appears on only one lane.

This is incorrect.

## Expected Behavior

When a DOUBLE note is successfully hit:

- Both lanes must trigger impact effects.
- Both effects must appear at exactly the same time.
- Each effect must be positioned at the center of its corresponding lane.
- Both effects must have independent particle and energy-ring animations.
- Both effects must have synchronized animation timing and duration.
- The visual feedback must clearly communicate that TWO lanes were hit simultaneously.

## Implementation

### 1\. Inspect existing systems

Inspect:

- DOUBLE judgement logic.
- lane1 and lane2 handling.
- ImpactEffect / EffectManager.
- Particle spawning.
- Hit feedback animation.
- Score and judgement display.

Identify why only one impact effect is currently triggered.

### 2\. Fix DOUBLE impact handling

For a successful DOUBLE judgement:

js

```js
spawnImpact(lane1, hitZ, judgement);
spawnImpact(lane2, hitZ, judgement);
```

Use the existing effect architecture and centralized lane coordinates.

Both effects must be scheduled from the same gameplay event and share the same start timestamp.

Do not trigger the second effect through a delayed callback.

### 3\. Visual requirements

Each lane must independently display:

- Bright circular energy impact.
- Expanding neon ring.
- Particle burst.
- Brief lane illumination.
- Matching judgement color.

Both effects must be visually balanced.

Display the PERFECT / EPIC text once, centered between the two affected lanes.

### 4\. Other note types

Do not change TAP, HOLD, SLIDE or BOMB behavior.

Do not change scoring, combo, health or audio timing.

### 5\. Regression tests

Test DOUBLE on:

- Lane 0 + Lane 1
- Lane 0 + Lane 3
- Lane 1 + Lane 2
- Lane 2 + Lane 3

Verify:

- Exactly two impacts per successful DOUBLE.
- Both impacts start simultaneously.
- Each impact is centered on its assigned lane.
- Neither impact is missing.
- No duplicate impacts occur.
- Missed DOUBLE does not incorrectly show successful hit effects.

### 6\. Deliverables

- Identify root cause.
- List modified files.
- Provide before/after screenshots.
- Provide a test proving both impact effects trigger simultaneously.
- Run existing QA suites.

IMPORTANT: Fix the actual root cause. Do not simply add a decorative second glow that is disconnected from the DOUBLE judgement system.

Mình nhấn mạnh thêm: Hai hiệu ứng phải là hai điểm va chạm độc lập nhưng đồng bộ tuyệt đối. Không phải một hiệu ứng lớn kéo dài qua hai lane.

Như vậy người chơi sẽ cảm nhận được DOUBLE thực sự là đánh trúng hai lane cùng lúc.



I’ll trace the DOUBLE judgement path from `Gameplay` through the effect manager and inspect how impact particles and rings are instantiated, then fix the event at its source and add a focused simultaneous-effect test.

The DOUBLE judgement is scored once, but `_applyHit()` only flashes and bursts at the single completing lane. I’m checking the HUD’s use of the event lane now so the two impacts can be added without duplicating the judgement text or breaking lane-aware reactions.

I’ve confirmed the source bug: DOUBLE is scored once, then `_applyHit()` emits a single pad flash and a single impact at the completing lane. I’ll capture a pre-fix frame first, apply the fix at that shared hit path, then capture the same double-lane hit after.



















The fix is in place: the hit path now emits one impact per lane for a completed DOUBLE, with a shared timing object, while the judgement event remains single. The new regression suite passes 70/70 across all four lane pairs, plus miss/partial cases and real `ImpactFX` timing. I’m capturing the matching post-fix frame and then running the existing suites.

The live anchor check caught a real coordinate bug in my first HUD pass: it projected the desktop lane width instead of the responsive `notes.laneX`. I’m correcting that before finalizing so the label sits between the actual rendered lane centres, not their unscaled positions.










## Root cause

`Gameplay._applyHit()` used only the lane of the press that completed the DOUBLE:

```js
this.world.hitLine.flash(lane, ...);
this.impactFX.burst(positionAt(lane), ...);
```

`lane2` was correctly stored on the note and the DOUBLE was correctly scored once, but neither lane-expanded pad feedback nor a second impact was ever emitted.

A second timing issue would have remained: `ImpactFX` randomizes ring duration per burst, so two independent calls could start together but finish at different times. DOUBLE impacts now receive one shared timing object from the same hit event.

## Fix

- `Gameplay` now derives both lanes for a completed DOUBLE and emits:
  - one pad flash per lane;
  - one `ImpactFX.burst()` per lane, at each live lane centre and the judgement depth.
- Both bursts use the same `startedAt` and the same `0.48 s` ring duration. They are called synchronously from one hit event, not through a delayed callback.
- Scoring, combo, health, judgement tier and audio timing remain once-per-object.
- A missed DOUBLE takes the miss path and produces no successful impact.
- A partial DOUBLE only lights the lane that was actually pressed.
- The judgement event remains single and now carries `lanes`, allowing the HUD to keep one label while positioning it between the two lanes.
- The HUD anchor uses the **live responsive `notes.laneX`** values, including portrait scaling.

## Verification

`qa/stage96-double-impact-check.mjs` — **70/70 passed**:

| Pair | Judges | Scored | Impacts | Pads | Shared start | Shared duration |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|---:|
| 0 + 1 | 1 | 1 | 2 | 2 | yes | 0.48 s |
| 0 + 3 | 1 | 1 | 2 | 2 | yes | 0.48 s |
| 1 + 2 | 1 | 1 | 2 | 2 | yes | 0.48 s |
| 2 + 3 | 1 | 1 | 2 | 2 | yes | 0.48 s |

The suite also verifies that each impact is at its assigned lane X, both are at `hitZ`, both use the same judgement colour/weight, and the real `ImpactFX` creates independent ring, shockwave, flare and particle instances whose paired rings advance in lockstep. Missed and partial DOUBLE cases are covered.

**31 QA suites pass** after the change, including the existing mechanics, judgement, bomb, note-visual and four-lane suites. The stage93 feedback-count assertion was updated because a DOUBLE now correctly produces two lane impacts.

`npm run build` passes: **148 modules**, **693 ms**.

1111111111111  save chart edit
Có, đây là vấn đề cần kiểm tra. Nếu bạn đang vào chế độ Edit để thêm obstacle (vật cản/BOMB) mà không thấy nút Save, có thể Chart Editor hiện mới hỗ trợ thao tác chỉnh sửa trong bộ nhớ tạm nhưng chưa có giao diện lưu thay đổi.

Mình nghĩ cần xử lý triệt để, không chỉ thêm một nút Save cho có. Khi lưu xong, các obstacle mới phải được ghi vào chart và vẫn còn nguyên khi tải lại game.

## Prompt gửi OpenCode sửa Chart Editor

Writing

# BUG FIX / FEATURE – ADD SAVE FUNCTION TO CHART EDITOR

## Problem

When entering Edit mode and adding new obstacles (BOMB) or other notes, there is no visible Save button.

Users need to save their chart modifications and preserve them after leaving the editor or refreshing the browser.

## 1\. Inspect existing implementation

Before coding, inspect:

- Chart Editor UI
- Note/Obstacle creation logic
- BeatMap data structure
- Chart loading and serialization
- Existing music library
- Existing local persistence system

Identify whether edits are currently stored only in memory.

Do not rebuild the editor.

## 2\. Add a visible SAVE button

Add a clearly visible SAVE button to the Chart Editor toolbar.

Requirements:

- Always visible in Edit mode.
- Use a recognizable save icon.
- Highlight the button when there are unsaved changes.
- Show a saved indicator after successful saving.
- Disable Save when there are no changes.
- Display an error if saving fails.

Suggested UI:

\[ + NOTE \] \[ + OBSTACLE \] \[ UNDO \] \[ REDO \] \[ SAVE \]

## 3\. Save functionality

When clicking SAVE:

- Serialize the complete current chart.
- Include all existing notes.
- Include newly added obstacles.
- Include TAP, HOLD, DOUBLE, SLIDE and BOMB.
- Preserve lane positions.
- Preserve timestamps and durations.
- Preserve BPM and chart metadata.
- Preserve difficulty settings.
- Preserve the original music reference.

Do not lose or overwrite existing chart data accidentally.

## 4\. Persistent storage

Implement reliable local persistence using IndexedDB if the project does not already have an appropriate storage mechanism.

Requirements:

- Save charts between browser sessions.
- Reload previously saved charts.
- Update an existing chart without creating unwanted duplicates.
- Maintain a unique chart ID.
- Track the last modified timestamp.
- Handle storage errors gracefully.

Do not rely exclusively on JavaScript variables or temporary application state.

## 5\. Export and Import

Also provide:

- EXPORT JSON: Download the current chart as a JSON file.
- IMPORT JSON: Load a previously exported chart.
- Validate imported chart structure before replacing the current chart.
- Keep the original chart safe if import fails.

## 6\. Unsaved Changes Protection

If the user modifies a chart and attempts to:

- Leave Edit mode.
- Return to the main menu.
- Load another song.
- Refresh or close the page.

Show a confirmation dialog:

"You have unsaved changes. Save before leaving?"

Options:

- Save
- Discard
- Cancel

Do not silently discard user edits.

## 7\. Undo / Redo

Verify that adding, deleting and moving notes can be undone and redone.

Save must persist the current state, not an earlier undo state.

## 8\. Testing

Test the following scenario:

1. Open a song.
2. Enter Edit mode.
3. Add 5 BOMB obstacles.
4. Add 3 TAP notes.
5. Move one obstacle to another lane.
6. Click Save.
7. Leave the editor.
8. Reload the browser.
9. Reopen the same song and chart.

Verify that all changes remain exactly as saved.

Also test export/import JSON and unsaved-change protection.

## 9\. Deliverables

- Root cause of missing Save functionality.
- List of modified files.
- Screenshot showing the Save button.
- Screenshot showing successful save confirmation.
- Persistence test results.
- QA results.
- Production build result.

IMPORTANT: Saving a chart must never modify the original audio file. Chart data and music assets must remain separate.

Preserve all existing gameplay, audio synchronization, lane coordinates and scoring mechanics.

## 2. Mình đề xuất giao diện sau khi sửa

CHART EDITOR

SavedLast saved: Just now

Add Note

Add Obstacle

Undo

Redo

SAVE CHART

Export JSON

Import JSON

Một khuyến nghị quan trọng: Nên có cả Save thủ công và Auto-save. Auto-save giúp tránh mất công chỉnh sửa, còn Save thủ công giúp người dùng chủ động lưu phiên bản chart hoàn chỉnh.



I’ll trace the existing editor toolbar, draft/commit model, and map store before changing anything. The key question is whether edits are already committed through an existing persistence layer but lack a visible save action, or whether the editor is genuinely memory-only.

The editor already has a robust `SongMapStore` and an internal explicit save path, so this is not a missing persistence engine. The actual gap is the UI and leave/import workflow: the only save control is a header “SAVE DRAFT”, there is no toolbar Save/Export/Import, and closing/opening another song can bypass a proper Save/Discard/Cancel decision. I’ll reuse the existing store, add the visible toolbar workflow, and preserve the current chart on failed imports.







The controller now has explicit `exportMap()` / `importMap()` handling that validates the payload, rejects wrong song/difficulty/audio-length charts, and only adopts the working copy after validation succeeds. Next I’m wiring the toolbar, Save status, three-way unsaved dialog and page-leave guard into the existing editor shell.
















The data-layer suite is green: **83/83 checks**, including the exact 5 BOMB + 3 TAP + lane-move scenario, reload persistence, export/import failure safety, undo/redo save semantics and storage failure handling. The production build also passes. I’m checking the actual editor UI now for button state, save confirmation and the unsaved dialog, then I’ll capture the required screenshots.

The live editor confirms the visible behavior: the Save button is always in the Chart file group, turns amber with **UNSAVED** when dirty, disables with **SAVED** when clean, and the close guard shows exactly **SAVE / DISCARD / CANCEL**. I also verified after a real browser refresh that the saved chart reopened with **50 notes, 6 obstacles, revision 14**, and the moved BOMB remained on its new lane; the Discard path left the stored revision untouched. I’m running the full existing QA suite now.

## Root cause

The editor already had a working full-map persistence layer (`SongMapStore`), so edits were not only in memory. The real problems were:

- Save existed only as a small header **SAVE DRAFT** button, not a visible toolbar control.
- No Save status, dirty highlighting, disabled clean state, or `Ctrl/Cmd+S`.
- No Export JSON / Import JSON.
- Closing the editor offered only **Close anyway / Cancel**, with no Save choice.
- Opening another song used `close({ force: true })`, bypassing any save decision and potentially discarding edits.
- Refresh/close had no `beforeunload` protection.
- There was no import validation path that guaranteed the existing chart stayed intact.

The fix reuses the project’s existing localStorage chart store rather than introducing a second IndexedDB storage layer. That store already provides the stable `songId::difficulty` chart key, revisions, `updatedAt`, previous revisions, and quota handling.

## Modified files

- `src/ui/mapEditorMarkup.js` — toolbar Save/Export/Import controls, save icon, status area, third modal action.
- `src/game/map/MapEditor.js` — Save states, Ctrl/Cmd+S, export download, import file flow, Save/Discard/Cancel dialog, refresh guard, safe song switching.
- `src/game/map/editor/MapEditorController.js` — `saveChart()`, `exportMap()`, validated `importMap()`, safer close behavior.
- `src/styles/mapEditor.css` — visible Save button, dirty/saved/error states, toolbar layout.
- `qa/stage97-chart-save-check.mjs` — new 83-check persistence/import/undo/storage suite.
- `qa-artifacts/stage97-*.png` — UI evidence.

No audio files or audio data are touched. The saved chart contains the audio fingerprint only, and the QA checks confirm no `data:audio` is written.

## Save UI

![Save button](qa-artifacts/stage97-chart-save-button.png)

![Unsaved state](qa-artifacts/stage97-chart-save-unsaved.png)

![Successful save](qa-artifacts/stage97-chart-save-success.png)

The button is always present in Edit mode. It turns amber with **UNSAVED** when dirty, disables and shows **SAVED** when clean, and the successful-save toast shows the new revision.

## Unsaved protection

![Unsaved changes dialog](qa-artifacts/stage97-unsaved-dialog.png)

The in-app close path now presents **SAVE / DISCARD / CANCEL**. Verified in the live editor:

- **Cancel** kept the editor open with edits intact.
- **Discard** closed the editor, left the saved revision unchanged, and removed the autosave recovery draft.
- The same guard is used before opening a different song.

Refresh/close uses the browser’s native `beforeunload` confirmation. Browsers do not allow custom three-button text there, so it uses the native leave prompt while synchronously flushing an autosave recovery draft.

## Persistence results

Live browser test:

- Opened the existing song; chart started at 42 notes, revision 13.
- Added 5 BOMBs and 3 TAPs.
- Moved one BOMB from lane 2 to lane 0.
- Clicked toolbar **SAVE** → revision 14.
- Refreshed the browser and reopened the same song/chart.
- Reopened with **50 notes, 6 obstacles, 40 TAPs, revision 14**; the moved BOMB remained on lane 0.
- BPM, duration, title, difficulty and audio fingerprint were preserved.
- After capturing evidence, the demo chart was restored to its original 42-note revision 13.

Automated scenario also verifies exact note/time/lane/duration/reference equality after a fresh store/controller reload.

## Export / import and other checks

- Export/import round-trip preserves all five note types, durations, doubles, slide destinations, metadata and title.
- Invalid JSON, wrong song, wrong difficulty, wrong audio length and invalid note data are rejected without changing the current working chart or stored revision.
- Imported charts save as one map, not duplicates.
- Undo/redo verified for add, delete and move; Save persists the current post-undo/post-redo state.
- Storage failure leaves the chart dirty for retry and reports `SAVE FAILED`.
- Export/import was tested through the real controller methods; the OS file picker/download itself was not automated.

## QA and build

- `qa/stage97-chart-save-check.mjs`: **83/83 passed**
- All existing stage suites: **32/32 passed**
- Production build: **148 modules transformed, built in 629 ms**
- Browser console: no runtime errors; only Vite/HMR debug messages and successful 200 responses.

The new revision-history-panel idea would make saved chart changes easier to compare and roll back.
